# Which semistrict definitional equalities are conservative over CaTT-style theories? (round 2)

*A self-contained problem. Standard higher category theory is the only background assumed. This is the second round. The first round's prompt and its 267 responses are reproduced in the repository file `notes/conjectures/answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md` ("Response n" below refers to them). §2 summarises what they settled. Those results are taken as known and are not re-asked.*

**What we are asking for.** For a CaTT-style theory T, whose coherence side condition is read off a family of strict free structures, and for a set S of strict laws, is T with the laws of S added as definitional equalities conservative over T for inhabitation, in every finite context and every dimension? Round 1 settled this for every S over the Gray variant CaTT_G, and for every S without interchange over CaTT. Three cases remain. We want them settled as instances of one general statement, not one context or dimension at a time:

- **(a)** associativity and units (S = {A, U}) over the theories whose side condition reads off free structures with **directed interchange at every level** (§1.3). These are the theories CaTT_E for admissible E, the least one CaTT_V included;
- **(b)** the four subsets containing interchange, {I}, {A, I}, {U, I} and {A, U, I}, over ordinary CaTT;
- **(c)** insertion (Catt_sua) over ordinary CaTT in arbitrary contexts.

## 1. Setting

### 1.1 CaTT and its strict images

**CaTT** is Finster–Mimram's type theory for weak ω-categories (arXiv 1706.02866).
- Contexts are finite computads, written as lists of typed variables.
- Types are ⋆ or `t →_A u`.
- Terms are variables or `coh_{Δ, A}[σ]`. Here Δ is a pasting context (`Δ ⊢ps`), A = `t →_B u` is a type over Δ, and σ : Γ → Δ is a substitution.
- The rule for `coh` has a side condition on (Δ, A) alone.
- There is no definitional equality: types are compared syntactically.

**Kernels.** A *kernel* 𝕋 assigns to each finite computad Γ a strict higher-categorical structure F_𝕋(Γ), free on Γ. It is functorial for substitutions of cells for generators, and it comes with a projection π : F_𝕋(Γ) → ν(λΓ) to the free strict ω-category on Γ, read as a polygraph (Steiner, *Omega-categories and chain complexes*, HHA 2004). In every kernel below, composition is strictly associative and strictly unital. Every well-typed term t over Γ has a strict image ⟦t⟧ ∈ F_𝕋(Γ):
- a variable goes to its generator;
- `coh_{Δ, A}[σ]` goes to σ_*(c), where c is the cell of F_𝕋(Δ) supplied by the side condition (below).

The theory **CaTT_𝕋** has the syntax and rules of CaTT. Its `coh` side condition is:

```
SC_𝕋(Δ, t →_A u)  :⟺  F_𝕋(Δ) has a cell c : ⟦t⟧ → ⟦u⟧ (an identity allowed)
                       whose projection has full support.
```

Support is measured in the Steiner bases of the relevant Gray-enriched or strict quotient (below). The coherence is in the *coherence case* if ⟦t⟧ = ⟦u⟧, and in the *operation case* otherwise. Three kernels are in play:

| Kernel | F(Γ) | Interchange |
|---|---|---|
| ν (ordinary **CaTT**) | ν(λΓ), the free strict ω-category | strict at every level |
| 𝔉 (the **Gray variant CaTT_G**) | the free category enriched in strict ω-categories with the lax Gray tensor | strict inside each hom; directed across objects |
| E (**CaTT_E**), for admissible E (§1.3) | F_E(Γ) | directed at every level |

For ν and 𝔉, c is unique when it exists. The operation case is then exactly "⟦t⟧ = ∂⁻τ_Δ and ⟦u⟧ = ∂⁺τ_Δ" for the total cell τ_Δ, and the coherence case is exactly "⟦t⟧ = ⟦u⟧ with full support".

**Two forms of the coherence case.** The rule above is the *full-support form*. Round 1 (§2) used it, and every result there is for it. A second, *variable form* (written CaTT_𝕋^V) replaces the coherence case by:

```
⟦t⟧ = ⟦u⟧, and every variable of Δ occurs in t : A and in u : A.
```

The operation case is unchanged. For ν the two forms agree. For 𝔉 and F_E the variable form accepts strictly more coherences; §1.2 gives an example. Answers for either form are wanted; please say which form an answer concerns. Where the two forms behave differently, that difference is itself of interest.

### 1.2 The Gray kernel

Every pasting context decomposes uniquely as Δ = ΣΘ₁ ∨ … ∨ ΣΘₙ, with objects a₀, …, aₙ. The homs of 𝔉(Δ) are

```
𝔉(Δ)(aᵢ, aⱼ) = ν(λΘᵢ₊₁ ⊗ … ⊗ λΘⱼ),
```

with ⊗ the Steiner–Ara–Maltsiniotis tensor and factors in execution order. For a general finite computad Γ, 𝔉(Γ) is the free such enriched category on Γ. The total cell τ_Δ is the tensor of the composites of the Θᵢ. Support is measured in the Steiner bases.

Example: over Δ = ΣD¹ ∨ ΣD¹, with α : f ⇒ f′ and β : g ⇒ g′, the coherence of type (f ◁ β) ; (α ▷ g′) → (α ▷ g) ; (f′ ◁ β) is accepted. Its reverse is not, and no term at all has the reverse type.

The coherence case needs full support in the Steiner bases, not only that every variable occurs. Over the same Δ, βα = (f ◁ β) ; (α ▷ g′) and βα₂ = (1_{f;g} ; (f ◁ β)) ; (α ▷ g′) are parallel, have equal images and together use every variable. Their images miss the basis element α ⊗ β, so no single coherence has type βα → βα₂ (either way). A composite of two coherences, a unitor whiskered by α ▷ g′, does have that type. So in CaTT_G with the full-support form, parallel terms with equal images over a pasting context cannot in general be joined by one coherence-case coherence over the variables they use. In the variable form CaTT_G^V, coh(βα → βα₂) is accepted.

### 1.3 Kernels with directed interchange at every level

These kernels direct interchange along k-cells for every k, including inside homs. They are strict theories on the following signature:
- strictly associative and unital composition ∗ₖ in every dimension;
- an elementary interchanger χ for every pair of levels k < l;
- one naturality cell per varying argument, with Leibniz-signed orientation;
- higher cells between routes.

Such a theory E is **admissible** if its free objects on finite pasting diagrams satisfy five requirements:

1. They have an explicit combinatorial description by generators with explicit boundaries.
2. Two cells of the same dimension that use the same generators, counted in the expanded basis, are equal.
3. Every elementary interchanger exists, and its reverse does not.
4. Making every interchanger along a k-cell with k ≥ 1 an identity gives 𝔉(Γ). Making every interchanger an identity gives ν(λΓ).
5. They are closed under substitution of cells for generators.

The projection of §1.1 is the composite of requirement 4. For E, π denotes the first map, to 𝔉(Γ), and support is measured there. The expanded basis of F_E(Γ) is infinite already on Σ[2] ∨ Σ[2].

**Known facts about these theories** (proved elsewhere; full constructions in the repository files `notes/conjectures/answered/lax-interchange-every-dimension-r8-2026-11-20.md`, `-r9-2026-11-20.md` and `-r10-2026-11-20.md`):
- **The least theory V.** On finite pasting diagrams there is a least admissible theory V: F_V(Γ) maps generator-preservingly into F_E(Γ) for every admissible E.
- **Settings.** Two further laws change free objects on pasting diagrams: the *inner law* (crossing a composite A₁ ∗ A₂ past B equals the paste of the two crossings) and its mirror *R*. All four of V, V + R, V + inner law and V + both are admissible and pairwise distinct.
- **Decidability.** Equality and reachability in F_{V+S′}(Γ) are decidable for each of the four settings S′, in every dimension.
- **Loops.** Some free objects have non-identity endo-paths, for example a degree-4 loop H on the 2 × 2 grid with pairwise distinct powers. So c in SC_E need not be unique. The image of an operation-case coherence is a chosen lift: the first shortest witnessing path in a fixed enumeration.
- **Comparison with CaTT_G.** Every CaTT_E derivation projects to a CaTT_G derivation.
- **Interchange inside a hom.** Inside a hom, for 2-cells a, b of one object composable along a 1-cell, there are directed interchangers b ∗ a ⇛ a ∗ b and a ∗ b ⇛ b ∗ a. They are not inverse to each other. The Eckmann–Hilton cells are therefore non-invertible.

The question in (a) concerns only inhabitation. It does not depend on the choice of lift, since ≈_S below is syntactic.

### 1.4 Semistrict equalities

Write A, U and I for the associativity, unit and interchange laws:
- A: (x ∗ₖ y) ∗ₖ z = x ∗ₖ (y ∗ₖ z);
- U: 1 ∗ₖ x = x = x ∗ₖ 1, and 1_{a ∗ⱼ b} = 1_a ∗ⱼ 1_b;
- I, in whiskering form: for j-composable cells a, b of dimension > j, (a ∗ⱼ s b) ∘ (t a ∗ⱼ b) = (s a ∗ⱼ b) ∘ (a ∗ⱼ t b), together with the analogous laws for cells of unequal dimension. Here s and t are the source and target one dimension below the cells, and ∘ is composition along them. With U this is equivalent to Godement's law.

For a kernel 𝕋:
- **Words** over a pasting context Δ are terms built from variables, identities, and the binary composites that exist in CaTT_𝕋. In CaTT these are all ∗ₖ. In CaTT_G and CaTT_E they are vertical composites and whiskerings, since a horizontal composite of positive-dimensional cells across a directed interchange does not exist.
- For S ⊆ {A, U, I}, **≈_S** is the smallest congruence on terms, stable under substitution, that contains:
  - (i) w ≈ w′ for all raw-parallel words w, w′ over a pasting context that are equal in the free algebra satisfying exactly the laws in S;
  - (ii) coh_{Δ, w → w′}[σ] ≈ 1_{w[σ]} for every such pair whose coherence exists in CaTT_𝕋.
- **Published closed forms.**
  - ≡_su is Catt_su (Finster–Reutter–Rice–Vicary, arXiv 2007.08307: disc removal, endo-coherence removal, pruning).
  - ≡_sua is Catt_sua (Finster–Rice–Vicary, arXiv 2302.05303: the same with insertion).
  - They contain ≈_{U} and ≈_{A,U} respectively.

**Conservativity.** T_≈ is T plus the conversion rule: from Γ ⊢ t : A and A ≈ B, conclude Γ ⊢ t : B. T_≈ is **conservative over T in Γ** if every T-type over Γ that is inhabited in T_≈ is inhabited in T. This is the inhabitation form. We do **not** ask for T-terms ≈-equal to the given ones (see §2, item 2).

## 2. What is known (all proved or checked; not re-asked)

**1. A sufficient condition and an obstruction.**
- *Sufficient condition.* Write EL(k) at Γ for: parallel T-terms u, u′ of dimension k over Γ with u ≈ u′ are joined by T-terms e : u → u′ and e′ : u′ → u with e ≈ 1 and e′ ≈ 1. If EL(k) holds at Γ for all k < n, then T_≈ is conservative in Γ for types of dimension ≤ n. The proof is induction on derivations, whiskering level by level.
- *Obstruction.* If u ≈ u′ and some T-cell u → u′ exists but no T-term has type u′ → u, then T_≈ is not conservative in Γ.

**2. EL fails for every binary surface (Response 43).** In every nonempty finite context, in both CaTT and CaTT_G, EL_S(k) holds iff any two raw-parallel, ≈_S-equal k-terms are literally equal. So EL_S fails from rank 1 if A or U ∈ S, and from rank 2 otherwise (S = ∅ included). The sufficient condition of item 1 therefore cannot prove any positive case here. The round-1 prompt's claimed converse ("conservativity implies EL") holds only for the elaboration form, where the inhabitant must be ≈-equal to the given one. The positive results below are model-theoretic.

**3. Over CaTT_G, the classification is complete: conservative in every finite context and dimension iff I ∉ S.**
- *Subsets without I.*
  - ∅ is conservative (Response 3).
  - {A} is conservative (Response 16).
  - {U} is conservative (Response 51).
  - {A, U} is conservative (Response 222). The proof builds a model with strictly associative and unital temporal products and Kan fibrations at globular boundaries: simplicial presheaves on a site of arities with free temporal composition, then a Reedy/injective comparison and fixed-object rigidification. It uses Response 170: with strict units, top associativity generates the whole {A, U} surface.
- *What these proofs use.* Responses 3, 16, 51 and 222 take as input that, for every finite globular pasting arity P, the free CaTT_G-model on P maps to 𝔉(P) by a globular trivial fibration. This is the directed arity lifting of Response 1 in `notes/conjectures/answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r9-2026-11-20.md`, proved there model-theoretically (wedge Segal presentation and a fixed-hom criterion), not by a single coherence per lift. By the example in §1.2 a single coherence does not suffice. If you rely on this lifting, check that your argument does not need more than it states.
- *Subsets with I.* Any S ∋ I fails already at ΣD¹ ∨ ΣD¹ in dimension 3, by the obstruction of item 1.
- *Context-level results for S ∋ I.*
  - Through dimension 3, the exact per-context test is that every exchange edge has a directed return path, and this test is undecidable (Response 2).
  - On globular pasting contexts, in all dimensions, conservativity holds iff I ∉ S or at most one column is active (Response 2).
  - Per-context conservativity is Π⁰₂-complete in general (Responses 13, 29, 105, 185, 218).
- *Full image equality.* Taking equality in 𝔉 as the definitional equality is **not** conservative (Response 1). In Σ(x, a : 1ₓ → 1ₓ), the suspended diagonal Eckmann–Hilton braid ΣE(a, a) and Σ1_{a∗a} have equal 𝔉-images, but no CaTT_G 5-cell joins them in either direction.

**4. Over CaTT, every S without I is conservative in every finite context and dimension.**
- ∅ (Response 3), {A} (Response 16), Catt_su and hence {U} (Response 50), and {A, U} (Response 222) are conservative.
- {A, U} is also conservative when joined with all three published strict-unit rules (Responses 170, 183, 222).

**5. Over CaTT with S ∋ I, the known results are partial, and no counterexample is known.**
- *Low dimensions.* Every S, and ≡_sua, is conservative through dimension 4 in every finite context (Response 6).
- *Restricted contexts.* All eight binary surfaces and ≡_sua (also ≡_su) are conservative:
  - on every finite 2-computad, in all dimensions (Response 10);
  - on every finite 3-computad in which no two generators of dimension ≥ 2 are spatially composable (Response 14; extended to bubble-free 2-bases with arbitrary 3-generators in Response 229);
  - through degree h + 1 when the first generator above the 2-skeleton has degree h ≥ 4 (Response 31);
  - through dimension 5 in every finite context without 1- or 2-generators (Response 33).
- *Equations on 2-dimensional data.* All ordinary binary equations on 2-dimensional data are conservative in arbitrary higher extensions. So are all ≡_sua equations on 2-dimensional data, taken separately. Their union fails on the scalar (Response 26).
- *The Eckmann–Hilton pair is not identified* (Responses 1 and 4). Let E(a, b) : a ∗₁ b → b ∗₁ a be any two-variable term for scalars a, b : 1ₓ → 1ₓ, possibly itself using conversion by ≈_{A,U,I}. Then E(a, a) and 1_{a∗a} are not ≈_{A,U,I}-equal, and no surface 4-term joins them in either direction. One proof uses a weight model: 3-cells are interpreted in (ℕ, +), the five-disc string arity has weight 1, the braid has weight ≥ 1 and the identity has weight 0. They are also not ≡_sua-equal (they have distinct Catt_sua normal forms). Even a ∗₁ b and b ∗₁ a stay distinct on the full binary surface. So Simpson's Eckmann–Hilton obstruction does **not** by itself refute any S ∋ I over CaTT. In contrast, full strict-image equality (equality in ν) fails already in dimension 4 at (x, a : 1ₓ → 1ₓ), and in dimension 5 on a single scalar 3-generator (Response 33).
- *Decidability.* Conversion for ≈_{A,U,I} is decidable on well-typed terms over every finite computad (Response 44). So is conversion for every finite insertion width (Response 202), and for strict units with binary {A, U} (Response 217).

**6. Over CaTT_E (directed interchange at every level).**
- Every S ∋ I fails, by item 1: requirement 3 leaves the reverse interchanger type empty.
- Nothing is known about {A, U}, {A} or {U} in arbitrary contexts.
- A CaTT_E-type that becomes inhabited after conversion is in particular a CaTT_G-type inhabited after conversion, hence inhabited in CaTT_G (item 3). This says nothing about inhabitation in CaTT_E, because CaTT_E has fewer terms.

**7. Semantic background.**
- Gordon–Power–Street: every tricategory is triequivalent to a Gray-category (strict associativity and units, weak interchange).
- Simpson (*Homotopy types of strict 3-groupoids*, 1998): strict units together with strict interchange cannot model all homotopy 3-types. He conjectures that strict associativity and interchange with weak units suffice.
- Joyal–Kock prove that conjecture semantically for 3-types with one object and one arrow.

## 3. Questions

**Q1 (main).** Give a uniform classification. For which kernels 𝕋 and which S ⊆ {A, U, I} is CaTT_𝕋 with ≈_S conservative over CaTT_𝕋 in every finite context and every dimension? The same question applies to ≡_sua over CaTT. By §2 this comes down to three statements, and a single argument covering several of them is preferred:

- **(a)** For every admissible E, in particular V and the three other settings, ≈_{A,U} is conservative over CaTT_E. The most general form wanted is this: ≈_{A,U} is conservative over CaTT_𝕋 for **every** kernel 𝕋 whose free structures are strictly associative and unital, are closed under substitution, and reflect the side condition through a projection as in §1.1. Does the construction of Response 222 extend? It needs a site of arities with free temporal composition. In F_E the temporal composites inside a hom also carry directed exchanges, and c need not be unique.
- **(b)** Over CaTT, decide conservativity for {I}, {A, I}, {U, I} and {A, U, I}. By downward closure it suffices to settle {A, U, I}, and {A, I} and {U, I} if it fails. {A, I} is Simpson's case. No conjecture is offered, because the expected Eckmann–Hilton counterexample is ruled out (§2, item 5).
- **(c)** Over CaTT, is Catt_sua (≡_sua) conservative in every finite context? Is the join of ≡_sua with ≈_{A,U,I} non-conservative in some context, given that it fails on the scalar on 2-dimensional data (Response 26)?

**Q2.** Where any part of Q1 fails, determine exactly which contexts it holds in, and give a minimal counterexample (context, dimension and type). Say whether conservativity in an individual context is decidable for that surface, and if not, its complexity.

**Q3 (fallback, only if Q1 is out of reach).** For (b) and (c), settle conservativity through dimension 6 in every finite context. Dimension 4 is known (Response 6), and dimension 5 is known in the restricted classes of §2, item 5. For (a), settle it on all pasting contexts in every dimension, and through dimension 4 in every finite context.

## 4. What counts as a solution

For each of (a), (b) and (c): a proof valid in every finite context and every dimension, or a counterexample, meaning a context Γ and a CaTT_𝕋-type over Γ that is inhabited after conversion but not before. A counterexample's non-inhabitation should come with an invariant (a model of CaTT_𝕋, as in Responses 1 and 4) that can be checked by hand or by a short program. A proof for (a) may be stated for a class of kernels containing every admissible E. In that case, say which of the five requirements it uses.

## 5. Literature (searched 2026-11-22 and 2026-11-24)

- Finster–Reutter–Rice–Vicary (arXiv 2007.08307) prove Catt_su conservative over Catt for terms over pasting diagrams. Finster–Rice–Vicary (arXiv 2302.05303) define Catt_sua. Rice's thesis (arXiv 2502.17068, Ch. 4) gives partial rehydration over pasting contexts and discusses obstructions in generic contexts. None of these proves or conjectures conservativity in arbitrary contexts.
- Chanavat–Hadzihasanovic (arXiv 2507.00146) use top associativity in globular operations (cited in Response 16).
- No published treatment of CaTT-style theories with directed interchange at every level was found. For directed interchange inside homs, Gepner–Heine's oriented categories are the closest semantic setting.

## Researcher Response 1

**The join in Q1(c) is conservative on a finite ordinary 2-computad exactly when its free strict 2-category has no nonidentity cell from an empty path to itself.** This decides the mathematical criterion simultaneously in all output dimensions. On these inputs Q2 recognition for the join is $\Pi^0_1$-complete under computable many-one reductions, even on one object with no generating scalar. In every failing 2-computad there is a failure in output dimension four, the least possible by round-1 Response 26. The same criterion applies to strict units alone joined with the single elementary binary interchange square (including its head equation), and to every intermediate surface up to the join in Q1(c).

**From a bubble to a raw boundary.** Write a positive empty-to-empty history at $x$ as a succession of $m>0$ positioned 2-generator events. Every nonidentity strict 2-bubble has such a spelling by planar serialization. Realize it by a raw term $b:1_x\to1_x$. Whisker the indicated generator by the words on either side of each step. Fit its raw 1-source and 1-target to chosen bracketings of the two edge words by composing with rebracketing and unit comparisons in their line arities, in the required directions. Use the literal $1_x$ at both ends. These comparisons have zero generating 2-events, so the composite still has $m$. This construction also works when the given raw attachment of a 2-generator uses unbiased 1-heads: compare its spelling to the chosen path by the raw line-arity lifting of round-1 Responses 2 and 4, then substitute the edges.

Let $E$ be Rice’s raw two-variable Eckmann–Hilton term. The old type
\[
               E(b,b)\longrightarrow 1_{b *_1 b}
\]
is inhabited in the join by converting an identity. Indeed the square calculation of round-1 Response 26 is a judgement with a free scalar variable, so substitute the raw term $b$ into that judgement. For raw non-inhabitation, use the list model of round-1 Response 1. Give each generating 2-cell one bead, all of the same colour, and send every 1-cell to the unique arrow. Both sides of each 2-attachment have that arrow as value. The evaluation of $b$ is a list of length $m$: a whiskered event contributes one and the path comparisons contribute zero. To calculate the generic $E$ on two such lists, first use distinct colours for the $2m$ positions. The only colour-preserving bijection to the list in the other block order exchanges the blocks, preserving the internal orders. Recolouring them all alike leaves the permutation of positions unchanged. The identity fixes all positions and the model has only identity 4-cells. This excludes a raw filler in either direction. In particular no assumption on connectedness of the chosen history or on distinct generator labels is necessary. [List model and raw braid](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-1) · [Generic joined calculation](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-26)

**Converse.** On a bubble-free finite 2-polygraph the comparison from its free weak realization to its free strict realization is an equivalence by the repository’s dimension-two homotopy-polygraph theorem. Passing from raw attaching 1-terms to their edge words preserves the free realization by the path comparison before each 2-attachment, as in round-1 Response 10. Thus the raw comparison is a weak equivalence. At *specified parallel raw faces* a strict filler lifts: local essential surjectivity in the iterated hom at those faces gives a raw filler with equivalent strict value. The target is gaunt (edge length and 2-event count are additive and it has only identities above two), so that value equals the given value. Objects are the declared vertices. Every derivation in the join evaluates in the strict target, since each of its equations holds there, so this lifts a filler at every old type inhabited in the join. This direction applies to any strict-sound conversion. It uses the repository’s verified dimension-two comparison, including the mesh-model inputs identified in its verification. [Dimension-two comparison](../answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md#followup-2) · [Application to raw computads](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-10)

**Complexity.** Enumerating finite positioned rewrite histories semidecides failure. For hardness, take a positive semi-Thue reachability instance $u\Rightarrow^*v$. On one object use the letters as edges, add two fresh edges $L,R$, and adjoin $\epsilon\to LuR$ and $LvR\to\epsilon$ to the original rules. A successful derivation between the given words yields a positive bubble. Conversely take the first death in any positive closed history. The matched pair of markers it consumes was created by one birth: before that first death births insert balanced pairs and interior rules cannot touch markers. No surviving nested birth is possible between the two adjacent-in-marker-order letters of this pair at death. Thus its interior changed from $u$ to $v$ by ordinary rules alone. The hardness input is the effective halting reduction to positive word reachability, not merely undecidability: one may use nonempty configuration words containing endmarkers and a state, rules simulating one tape step, and a halting-state cleanup that absorbs adjacent letters one at a time down to a fresh singleton. These rules have nonempty sides. This proves the claimed completeness and makes recognition undecidable on arbitrary finite contexts as well. In arbitrary rank enumerability of raw and joined derivations gives the general $\Pi^0_2$ upper bound; the exact unrestricted complexity does not follow here. [Guarded reduction, item 3](../answered/homotopy-polygraphs-2026-11-19.md) · [Halting reduction, Theorem 23.4](https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~ear/cs341/automatabook/AutomataTheoryBook.pdf)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_join_bubbles.py`. The solver compares bounded empty-to-empty search against an independent finite transitive closure for 128 guarded word problems, including nested and adjacent guarded blocks in the search space. It replays the 79 positive histories and computes their block permutations by adjacent exchanges. With `--rice-dir /path/to/catt-strict` and a release build, Rice also checks the raw diagonal at $b=(1_x\xrightarrow{u}f\xrightarrow{d}1_x)$ and its strict-unit conversion to the specialized square. The additional elementary square equation is the mathematical step above. With `--input data.json --width N` the solver searches a supplied 2-computad and returns a history and a separating permutation. A negative bounded result excludes histories only up to that width. The two surfaces individually remain conservative on all these inputs by round-1 Response 10. [Solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_join_bubbles.py) · [Rice’s raw term](https://github.com/alexarice/catt-strict/blob/577993375ec7491b1b50c07dc58a0595c5fd58fc/examples/eh.catt)

## Researcher Response 2

For the **full-support form**, every admissible kernel inherits Gray conservativity for the interchange-free binary surfaces through degree three in arbitrary finite contexts. On a pasting context with at most one active outer column, including the suspension of an arbitrary tree, conservativity holds in every degree and raw inhabitation at a pair of faces is exactly strict reachability.

### Transport from a quotient that first identifies parallel cells in degree three

Write $q_P:F_E(P)\to\mathfrak F(P)$ for the first quotient in requirement 4. An elementary interchanger along a $k$-cell first exchanges two $(k+2)$-cells and has dimension $k+3$. Killing those for $k\ge1$ therefore does two things in dimensions at most three. It preserves the entire 2-truncation and induces a surjection on 3-cells at **every prescribed pair of 2-faces**. Indeed its first relations identify parallel 3-cells. Such a quotient neither identifies lower cells nor makes new tuples of 3-cells composable since their incidence data have dimension at most two. Relations from higher cells have parallel lower boundaries. This argument only concerns the quotient presentation. It needs neither normal forms for paths nor uniqueness of a 3-cell with given faces.

Consequently the two *raw* languages have identical typing through degree three on common declarations. Induct on a Gray typing derivation in this range. For a degree-three head, lift its generic Gray witness by the surjection. Its 2-faces are the required interpreted generic terms by the 2-truncation isomorphism. Its projection is the original Gray witness, so it passes exactly the stipulated support test. For a lower head use the isomorphism itself. Then use the same raw substitution. Choices between different lifts in degree three cannot affect this induction, since the head test only compares faces through degree two. Variables use their declared types. The converse is projection. Higher declarations in the ambient context can be ignored here since a raw term of degree at most three uses only declarations through that degree. This follows from fullness after ordinary projection, which bounds the height of each head arity by its output degree. The zero-dimensional judgment returns a vertex directly.

Given an old $E$-type of degree at most three inhabited after interchange-free binary conversion, project its derivation to the corresponding Gray surface as in §1.3. Gray conservativity gives a raw Gray term at that very type, and the raw typing comparison returns the term at the original $E$-type. Degree three is the last degree for which this argument needs only the isomorphism below the first quotient relations. In degree four the boundary can prescribe a particular representative of a quotient 3-cell. This transfer uses requirement 4, the dimensions of the interchangers and the projected head test.

### An all-degree test on the support of the strict witness

Write $p$ for ordinary strict projection from Gray. Use the strict interpretation of the interchange-free surface in $F_E$. For clarity, the binary operations in this interpretation are the stipulated compositions, whiskerings and identities on words. An $A,U$ word equation therefore has equal strict values. For clause (ii) the identity on this value is the coherence-case witness. Indeed a Gray endomorphism on a pasting arity is an identity by strong loop-freeness of its Steiner homs, so if a loop passes the projected fullness test then its identity has the same projection and passes too. For an operation-case head make the given choice on its bound arity at the interpreted strict boundary, and evaluate a head application by substituting into that chosen cell. In particular one need not choose a shortest path anew after substitution. Induction on formation and conversion gives soundness: equal interpreted boundaries give the same choice on the same bound arity, and evaluating a substituted application composes the two substitutions. This argument uses the interpretation of the word constructors as the named strict operations in §1, and requirement 5.

Let $P$ be any pasting context, let $u,v$ be literal parallel $E$-terms there, and suppose $c:\llbracket u\rrbracket\to\llbracket v\rrbracket$ in $F_E(P)$. Take the positional support $K\subseteq P$ of $p q_P c$, including the faces of its generators. It is a pasting tree, possibly a point. Take the raw retraction $r:P\to K$ which clamps the outer spine to its occupied interval and does the same recursively in the traversed columns. Off the interval it uses iterated identities. If $q_K(r_*c)$ has full Gray support on $K$, there is a raw term $u\to v$ in $P$.

To prove this, every generic $E$-head uses every position of its ordinary strict arity. In fact its Gray projection has full support by the head test. In each traversed Gray tensor, augmenting all factors but one sends a basis element in that factor tensored with stationary vertices to the specified basis element. Full closed tensor support gives full closed support in every factor projection, by the image-of-support lemma. The single-column and point cases are immediate. Under an ordinary strict labelling the support of the resulting paste is the union of the closed supports of the labels it uses. Thus raw term induction shows that all generating variables of either old face, including their dependencies, lie in $K$. Both faces restrict literally to $K$; equivalently, clamping acts there as the identity. Apply the $E$-head test over $K$ at these restricted raw faces, with witness $r_*c$, and weaken its head along $K\subseteq P$. The resulting term has the prescribed raw type. Its selected strict value need not be $c$. In particular the test succeeds if at most one outer column of $K$ contains a generator of dimension at least two in global degree. On each occupied hom all other tensor factors are units. The Gray-to-ordinary projection there is the unit isomorphism, including for closed support. Since $p q_K(r_*c)$ uses exactly $K$, its Gray lift has full support. If $P$ itself has at most one active outer column this holds for every $K$. Strict interpretation of any proposed surface inhabitant now gives the required $c$, establishing conservativity on $P$ in all degrees. More precisely, at any old pair of faces on such a $P$, **both** inhabitation questions are exactly strict reachability in $F_E(P)$. The four decidable settings of §1.3 hence give a decision at those faces. There is no bound on the number of active columns *inside* the one outer column. Requirements 1–3 are not used by the support argument. The argument uses the given Gray quotient and substitution, and the stated special-case decision uses the reachability theorem.

In particular **a positive empty-to-empty 2-history rules out every finite higher extension**, regardless of the boundaries or number of the new generators. This strengthens Response 1 from an exact result on 2-computads to a necessary condition on all finite contexts. It applies even if explicit repairs work in both directions at the original diagonal.

### Why a two-sided repair raises the obstruction

Let $w=b*_{n-1}b$, $p=E_{n-2}(b,b)$ and $j=1_w$. Here $E_{n-2}$ is the $(n-2)$-fold suspension of Rice’s raw two-variable Eckmann–Hilton term. In $J$ they are equal: suspend the generic strict-unit/square calculation of round-1 Response 26 before this substitution. Suspension sends disc and endo removal to the same rules on suspended arities, pruning to pruning, and the two square words and their head equation to the same binary generator one cut higher. Thus **both** old types $p\to j$ and $j\to p$ have converted identity inhabitants. If either raw orientation is missing, its old type establishes failure at $n+2$. If both exist, choose $r:j\to p$ and $s:p\to j$. Their raw top composite $r;s$ has literal type $1_w\to1_w$ and positive $(n+2)$-count by additivity. This is the next bubble with base $w$. Apply the same argument in the *original context*. At the first missing generator degree the positive-count conclusion precludes both repairs. Finite declaration rank guarantees such a degree. ### When finite repairs already fail in dimension four

There is a separate fixed-degree obstruction which need not wait for the next gap. Let $B$ be a finite 2-computad. Allow arbitrary finitely many declarations of degree at least four. A connected bubble $D$ with $m$ generating vertices still separates the original two 3-faces in degree four if every 4-declaration has fewer than $2m$ generating 2-vertices in its 2-source. In particular with one loop $f$, an opening $u:1\to f$, a step $t:f\to f$ and a closing $d:f\to1$, the connected bubbles $u;t^k;d$ force failure **in degree four** for every such extension. One may also allow 3-declarations whose two 2-faces have isomorphic labelled wire graphs relative to the ordered boundary, choosing an isomorphism for each.

Here are details of the raw model. Use lists of edge colours as wires and labelled directed port graphs as diagrams, retaining each generating vertex and ordered external and internal ports. The diagram is a 2-cell and an incidence isomorphism fixing its boundary is a 3-cell. One may use the monoidal bicategory with wire lists as objects, diagrams as arrows and these isomorphisms as 2-arrows, then deloop once. Composition glues matching ports, tensor takes ordered disjoint union and through-wires are identities. Keep distinct vertex occurrences in the two operands. The associativity, unit and interchange constraints are the canonical bijections of occurrences and wires under gluing or disjoint union. Their coherence diagrams commute because each route preserves the provenance of every input occurrence and the incidence of each through-wire. Thus the raw tricategory interpretation of round-1 Response 5 applies. All original objects may map to its one object. Diagram evaluation of a 2-generator uses its corolla, and structural path comparisons use through-wires.

For parallel diagram isomorphisms keep only their induced bijection on *closed connected components with $m$ generating vertices*. Equality of these bijections is a congruence. On each component incident to an external port an isomorphism is uniquely determined by that port, since fixing it fixes its incident vertex, all ordered ports of that vertex and hence the entire component. Under gluing, newly closed components are assembled from these rigid fragments. Already closed components keep their vertices and cannot be attached to another component through a port. Disjoint union and composition of isomorphisms respect the relation as well. Use the quotient at dimension three with identities above. A 4-declaration respects it whenever the two 3-faces induce the same component bijection, in particular when fewer than $2m$ vertices are present: there is then at most one component of the selected size. Every higher declaration has its forced value by thinness. In the diagonal on $D$, two copies of the connected component are exchanged; distinct temporary vertex colours and subsequent forgetting force this permutation as above. The identity fixes both. This proves the bound, and gives a stronger direct test when permutations of components are supplied for larger declarations. **For the variable form of every admissible $E$, interchange-free binary conversion is conservative through degree $h$ whenever the declarations below degree $h$ have variable faces.** Degree-$h$ generators may have arbitrary raw attaching terms. ### Lifting syntax at the positions of an ordinary strict cell

Let $Z$ be a globular set of generators, $FZ$ its free ordinary strict category, and $LZ$ its binary surface syntax modulo conversion, for either coherence rule. For a globular map $f:Y\to Z$, there are natural bijections
$$
 L Y \ \cong\ L Z\times_{F Z}F Y,\qquad
 H Y \ \cong\ H Z\times_{F Z}F Y ,
$$
where $H$ denotes *raw* syntax and both maps use ordinary strict projection. These statements do not assert that $F_E$ itself preserves these lifts.

Here are the details that allow the positional lifting of round 1 to apply to $E$. A cell $d\in FZ$ consists of a tree $P_d$, its total $b_d$ padded to the indicated degree, and a globular labelling $g_d:P_d\to Z$. Ordered positions are retained when their labels coincide. A strict value on a tree is supported on a subtree. Given a lift of the result of a strict substitution, there is a unique split into lifted labels on that subtree. This is the pullback property of multiplication of the free strict-category monad on globular sets. Subdivision and restriction to a signed face commute with the split by uniqueness. Every allowed generic $E$-head uses its entire tree in ordinary projection by Responses 2 and 3. Thus the split for a head application supplies a lift of *every* argument. Recursing gives the raw assertion, with identical faces by uniqueness at each incidence. [Positional strict multiplication](https://arxiv.org/abs/math/0305049) · [Raw splitting argument](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-4)

For the surface assertion, over each $d$ keep a choice of boundary families and an entry in $LY$ at each strict lift of $d$, with the faces specified by those families. This also defines a fibre when $d$ has no lift. Interpret a head entry by applying that same surface head on the split entries. Its two faces are the corresponding entries for the boundary expressions, by subdivision and signed restriction. For a word equation (i), both words use exactly the support of their common ordinary strict value. Substitution of the raw clamping map restricts the word law to that support. The two entries use the very same lifted tuple there, even when an unused variable outside has no lift. For (ii), both the admitted head and its identity side have full positional value, and (ii) applies to their common split tuple. Congruence in a bound type uses head congruence in $LY$; the side condition is tested on the unchanged generic arity. Assigning a generator its generating lifts now interprets $LZ$ in these families. Reading the entry at $c\in FY$ and folding recovers the original surface cell, since every argument of each applied head was lifted. Starting with a cell of $LY$, folding it and reading its entry at its own strict value recovers its original arguments by the unique split. This proves the two inverses. The argument uses ordinary strict soundness, including for surfaces with $I$. [Family construction with empty fibres](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-32)

Apply these bijections to a surface witness $t$ of ordinary value $d$ and its two old faces. Lift $t$ along $g_d$ at $b_d$ and lift the two raw faces at its signed faces. The raw lifts have the same literal lower boundaries by raw uniqueness. Their surface classes are the faces of the lifted $t$ by surface uniqueness. Any raw filler at this old type over $P_d$ consequently returns one at the original type. This proves the claimed equivalence of the two context classes, since every tree is itself globular. For the variable form apply Response 3 on $P_d$, then substitute $g_d$; this preserves the strict $E$-value of the witness. For the full-support form Response 2 gives inhabitation reflection whenever $P_d$ has at most one active outer column. These conclusions use positional lifting of the *surface witness*, so they need no uniqueness assertion for lifts of a strict $E$-cell after forgetting its positions.

### Allowing arbitrary attaching terms in the output degree

There is an extra boundary issue here. An upper generator can have a compound face whose raw writing must be respected each time the generator is used. We give the argument for $S\subseteq\{A,U\}$ and the variable rule. For a lower term or surface class $v$ over the globular skeleton $Z$, record $d$ and the $E(P_d)$-value of its lift at $b_d$. Call this pair its tag. For a raw term use its raw lift; the two definitions agree by surface uniqueness. Equal surface classes have equal tags by strict soundness on the positional arity. A tag determines the value in $F_E(Z)$ by relabelling. On a tree, raw or surface cells with the same strict $E$-value have identical tags: the supporting subtree is fixed by ordinary projection and its inclusion is split by clamping. Restrict the common value by that retraction.

Two properties of these tags are needed.

1. Suppose two well-typed head applications, with possibly different writings of the generic type, use the same bound tree and the same generic $E$-value on it. Suppose their corresponding labels have equal tags. Then the results have equal tags. Indeed they have the same ordinary positional result. On its tree, the split of the total locates the supporting subtree for each input label. The $E$-value of that lifted label is the inclusion of its tag value from that subtree, by uniqueness of the syntax lift and functoriality. Evaluating the generic cell by this tuple computes the result tag in both cases. Elaborate a derivation of a term of degree at most $h$, with its proposed raw target type supplied. Require its target boundary to have the tags of the specified lower faces. No identity-tag comparison between arbitrary degree-$h$ terms was used.

### A separated pair in any higher hom

For either coherence rule let $Z$ be a globular set of generators. Suppose two generating $(j+2)$-cells lie in the same $j$-hom frame and the $j$-target of the first is connected to the $j$-source of the second by a path of generating $(j+1)$-cells. The path may be empty and the cells may be the same declaration. **Every surface containing $I$ fails on $Z$ in degree $j+3$**, for each admissible $E$. Here the frame imposes the usual parallel lower poles, with no condition below objects for $j=0$.

The two temporal readings of this pair are parallel raw words after substitution in the $j$-fold suspension of the elementary square. The shape of $d$ can be recovered from its signed $(j+2)$-sections. They are the two-event shape, with the specified intermediate stationary columns, in their common $j$-fold suspension. Relabelling a globular generator by a generator preserves the entire shape of a section. Passing from the negative section of an $(j+3)$-tree to the tree itself only replaces individual $(j+2)$-positions by temporal strings of $(j+3)$-discs, allowing zero changes. This follows by truncating at depth $j+2$: branches below that depth are retained, and each nonempty final list truncates to its initial position. There are just two positions where those top changes can occur. In particular a closed chain of changes of a generator name can contribute positive length without creating another event position.

Send this positional tree by a raw substitution to the bare suspended square. On each active column send all versions of its $(j+2)$-event to the designated generating event and all intervening changes to identities on it. Send every intermediate stationary $(j+1)$-edge to the identity on the shared $j$-pole. These prescriptions respect the variable faces; equivalently suspend the two-column substitution $j$ times. The lifted source and target are the original two readings with the versions at their signed sections. This identification follows by lifting those actual raw words at the signed ordinary values and using uniqueness. After the substitution their $E$-values are exactly the two layers in reverse order, by strict units on the stationary whiskers. A strict image of the supposed return would contradict requirement 3 on the bare square itself. This excludes the return even when identifying the original labels allows arbitrarily many higher globular changes. It uses directedness only on a pasting arity.

At degree three the exact criterion extends to **arbitrary finite computads**, for all four choices containing $I$ and both coherence rules. Form the directed graph whose vertices are ordered histories of positioned 2-generator steps. ## Researcher Response 6

**The join $J$ in Q1(c) presents the free strict 2-category on every ordinary 2-computad, including its identity cells in all higher dimensions.** As an application, there is an exact first-failure test for the scalar contexts with arbitrary 4-declarations from round-1 Response 193. With at least one scalar and no 3- or 5-declarations, the first failure is either four or five. In particular it is **five** on the point with a scalar and the two opposite 4-declarations repairing its diagonal. The rank-six obstruction of Response 4 also exists there. Declarations above degree five do not change the test.

### The joined syntax on planar data

In ranks zero and one the operations are vertices and paths. On a line the standard unbiased path splits into binary paths by insertion; the unit is the empty path. At rank two, over a tree of height two the full generic strict cell is its total 2-paste. Both generic 1-faces therefore agree in the join with the standard paths on its signed boundaries, even when the head type used conversion. Congruence identifies this head with the standard head. At smaller arity a 2-head has equal strict 1-faces, hence equal generic faces in the join; published endo removal makes it an identity.

Write the height-two tree as columns, each a temporal row of generating 2-cells or a stationary edge. Its standard head equals the horizontal binary product of the vertical binary products of the columns, padding a stationary edge with its identity. Indeed the temporal row folds by insertion on a suspended line. To combine columns, flatten consecutive horizontal binary nodes at the root. Each selected disc column at a branching root has branching height zero, so the exterior insertion accepts any replacement of the matching dimension there. Insert the suspended row into its disc slot, and prune the identity-labelled peak at every stationary edge. The resulting tree is the given tree. Its generic 1-boundaries are the standard ones modulo path associativity and units. A single column uses only the row calculation. Binary whiskerings by edges agree with horizontal composition by their identity 2-cells by the same pruning.

These operations satisfy strict 2-category laws. The two associations in each product flatten to the same standard ternary head, units prune, and the horizontal product of identities is the identity on the composite by pruning and endo removal. For Godement interchange, take the globular grid of four 2-generators in two temporal rows in each of two columns. The horizontal product of the two vertical rows and the vertical product of the two horizontal rows are *literally raw-parallel* binary words on this arity. They are equal in the word algebra with $A,U,I$ by §1.4, so clause (i) identifies them. Substitution gives the law also at converted seams. Thus evaluation in the strict free 2-category has an inverse through degree two: interpret each generator in the joined algebra and extend by strict composition. The head factorization just proved makes the composite the identity on all terms by induction. This accounts for arbitrary raw writings of the original attaching 1-terms. In an arbitrary higher computad the statement still applies to its 2-truncation.

On a 2-computad, use joint induction on rank and the expression at that rank to account for higher cells. In a head of rank $d\ge3$, normalize its maximal labels of rank at least three. They are identities by induction (at rank $d$, the labels are proper subexpressions). Prune those peaks, repeating as long as the arity has height greater than two. New exposed labels are faces of the original labels or retained entries, hence are identities in the relevant ranks as well. The residual head has arity of height at most two. Its two generic faces have the same strict value; at rank two they are equal by the preceding calculation, and at higher rank by induction on that residual 2-computad. Endo removal gives the identity. This proves the presentation claim; no such reduction has been assumed for a 3-generator at a compound attaching type.

### First failing dimension for a scalar palette with 4-declarations

Take a point, a nonempty finite palette of generating 2-scalars at its identity, and arbitrary directed 4-declarations. For each unordered pair of colours, keep the declarations whose 2-boundary has exactly this two-strand multiset. Form the list of differences of the signed crossing numbers of their two 3-faces, in half twists for identical colours and in full twists for distinct colours. If some list has no positive entry, has no negative entry, or has gcd different from one, the first failure is **four**. If every list contains both signs and has gcd one, the first failure is **five**. Through degree three strict-sound conversion reflects. Every old 3-face here is on the 2-skeleton, so the planar presentation above makes *all* parallel old 3-faces equal in $J$. The directed two-strand calculation of round-1 Response 193 is therefore necessary and sufficient for degree-four reflection, including the sufficiency in all weights. [Signed-monoid criterion and raw braid lifts](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-193)

Here is the new degree-five obstruction. Choose a scalar $a$, write $w=a*_1a$, and let $e=E(a,a):w\to w$. When the pair test succeeds there is a 4-generator $k$ on precisely two copies of $a$. Transport its two 2-poles to $w$ by raw braid paths, using inverse transports at the opposite ends. Whisker $k$ accordingly to obtain one occurrence $H:p\to q$ at 3-loops $p,q:w\to w$. This uses the configuration-component transports of Response 193. The 3-loops $e;p$ and $p;e$ represent the same two-strand braid since $B_2$ is abelian; likewise $e;q$ and $q;e$. Thus raw 4-comparisons on the scalar base fit the two whiskerings $e*_2 H$ and $H *_2 e$ to a common pair of raw 3-boundaries. In $J$, all these scalar-base fitting cells are identities by the planar presentation, and $e$ is the identity. Pruning, disc removal and top units send both fitted whiskerings to $H$ at the common converted boundary. An identity thus inhabits the old comparison 5-type. For the actual generator $k:e\to1_w$ one can take
\[
 (e *_2 k)\,;\,\rho_e,\qquad (k *_2 e)\,;\,\lambda_e
      \quad : (e *_2 e)\longrightarrow e ,
\]
where $\rho_e$ and $\lambda_e$ are the two raw unit comparisons.

To separate the two 4-faces raw, use a strict symmetric monoidal 2-category. Its objects are strand counts $N$, and its 1-arrows at $N$ are permutations; there are no arrows between different counts. At $N=0,1$ use only identity 2-arrows. At $N\ge3$ use exactly one 2-arrow between each pair of permutations. At $N=2$ the objects of the hom category are the bits $0,1$ with composition of permutations given by addition modulo two. For the chosen generator write $s,t$ for the parity of its two faces before transport. Use identity arrows, an absorbing arrow at every pair of parities, and four distinguished arrows
\[
 [l,r]:l+s+r\longrightarrow l+t+r,\qquad l,r\in\mathbb Z/2 .
\]
Vertical composition of two nonidentities is absorbing. Horizontal composition with the identity arrow of a permutation adds its bit on the corresponding side of the mark; two marked operands give an absorbing arrow. Absorbing arrows stay absorbing in horizontal composition. These rules give a strict 2-category: for one mark, either order in the interchange law adds the same outside bits, and with at least two nonidentity operands both orders are absorbing. Tensor is block sum on permutations. On 2-arrows it uses the prescribed unique output above weight two, is strict unital when a summand has weight zero, and sends the two weight-one identities to an identity. Block permutations give the symmetry. Its 2-naturality is forced at weights at least three; at weight two either a summand is empty or both inputs have only identity 2-arrows. Associativity, involutivity and the hexagon are the usual block permutation identities.

Deloop this symmetric monoidal 2-category twice to interpret the computad in a weak 4-category. Concretely, restricting along $E_2\to E_\infty$ gives a two-fold monoidal $(\infty,2)$-category, and the one-object enriched-category construction twice has the original 2-category as fixed double endomorphism category. Its fixed top mapping spaces are discrete. The subcategory of permutations with only identity 2-arrows gives the ordinary list/bijection interpretation on the scalar base, so $e$ acts by bit one. Every structural raw 4-cell on this base acts by an identity 2-arrow in the target. Send $k$ to $[0,0]$, every other weight-two declaration to its absorbing arrow, and use the prescribed arrows at other weights. A transported $H$ is a mark $[l,r]$, since its transports only add permutations. The two fitted slides are $[l+1,r]$ and $[l,r+1]$, distinct at exactly the same boundary. Hence there is no raw 5-cell in either direction. This reasoning uses directed, possibly noninvertible 4-arrows even though the 3-arrows are permutations. [One-object enriched categories, Gepner–Haugseng](https://arxiv.org/abs/1312.3178) · [Iterated monoidal structures as iterated Segal spaces, Haugseng](https://www.sas.rochester.edu/mth/sites/doug-ravenel/otherpapers/Haugseng-spans.pdf)

The second checks the four endpoint parity choices, 10,000 instances of monoidal interchange and all 16 translated mark separations. Rice checks both displayed raw 4-faces at their common boundary and checks their strict-unit reductions after replacing the crossing by the identity. The generic joined square supplies that replacement. The earlier signed-monoid solver computes the first-failure test from the pair lists. The declarations in degree $h$ can have arbitrary attaching terms. Throughout, the ordinary strict category of a computad means its free strict category on the given attachments, as in §1.1.

### When an ordinary pasting arity has no directed choices

A fork of a plane rooted tree is a node with at least two non-leaf children. The root has depth zero. A fork at depth $k$ in a tree labelled globularly by $Z$ supplies a separated pair at cut $k$ in $Z$. In each of the two active columns take a generating $(k+2)$-cell, and take one generating $(k+1)$-cell from each intervening column. All have the required common lower poles. Read their labels. This argument allows repeated labels. In particular an arity of height at most $h-1$ over the skeleton in the theorem has no fork, since any fork in it would be at depth at most $h-3$.

Let $P$ have no fork. In its ordinary free strict category $FP$, at every cut $k$, two $k$-composable cells cannot both have effective rank at least $k+2$. Effective rank is the dimension after stripping identities. To see this, write the ordinary hom between two outer vertices as the product of the ordinary categories of the intervening columns, desuspended once. This is the strict pasting-tree hom formula, including the empty product at a vertex. At cut zero the two intervals are consecutive. Positive rank above one requires a non-leaf column, and there is at most one in the whole list. At a positive cut, both cells traverse the same interval. Every leaf column contributes just a point in the hom. If both cells had the stated ranks, in the one non-leaf factor their desuspensions would match at cut $k-1$ and have ranks at least $k+1$. Induct there. The point starts the induction.

On this $P$ the map $F_E(P)\to FP$ is an isomorphism. We give the signature check, since the Gray-quotient isomorphism on a single active *outer* column alone would say nothing about a directed choice at an inner cut. The forced theory $B$ in (K58) contains the face and unit declarations for the fixed signature. In these declarations a proper mixed disk at cut $k$ takes two adjacent positive hom operands, of global effective ranks at least $k+2$. This includes a one-variable naturality with a stationary hom *edge*. A stationary hom vertex gives fixed transport. The route joins and variations have the same two adjacent globe inputs at their defining cut. At unit inputs the hemisphere and bridge specializations descend to the specified face or lower call, an identity, or a paste of such calls with fixed whiskers. In particular a lower call surviving a specialization must itself pass its own two-operand test to be proper. These are the fixed-signature operation and unit tables used in the proof of (K58). Induct on a typed operation expression, using the declared recursion on degeneracies at a specialized input. A structural word in generators, identities, top compositions and fixed whiskers has the same effective rank as its ordinary projection. At the highest contributing degree its ordinary top chain counts the generating occurrences additively with nonnegative coefficients. If all inputs there are identities, the structural identity laws express the paste as an identity on the lower paste. Descend to its highest contributing degree. Thus, after the operands have structural writings, a first proper mixed call would give exactly the forbidden pair in $FP$. A match using an additional equation of $E$ still projects to a match there. The unit cases reduce as above, so all cells have structural writings and every elementary interchange comparison already has its unit value. By requirement 4 the quotient imposing that these comparisons be identities is $FP$. Here the identity of $F_E(P)$ itself factors through that quotient because on this algebra every such equation already holds on every matching tuple. The quotient map is consequently invertible. This argument uses the forced unit specializations on the problem's fixed signature.

Let $d$ now be an ordinary strict cell over a globular $Z$, and suppose its positional arity $P_d$ has no fork. Fix $h$ as in the theorem. Every ordinary value of degree below $h$ has fork-free arity over the lower globular skeleton. The same result gives ordinary-identity comparisons at **any** parallel raw writings of a common lower value. These comparisons work over the larger context by weakening. The raw single-disc whiskers (top composition at $i=h-1$) accomplish the change while preserving the ordinary value and all already fixed lower faces. ## Researcher Response 8

**In both Q1(b) and Q1(c) separately, every finite positive ordinary 3-computad reflects all old boundaries over a strict 3-cell of weight at most two, in every output dimension.** Weight counts *occurrences* of generating 3-cells, so two uses of the same rule count twice. Positivity means that both edge paths of each 2-generator are nonempty and both 2-faces of each 3-generator have a generating 2-occurrence. The 2-faces may be arbitrary raw writings and may change the number of wires. The result holds for every individual binary surface and each published surface. In particular a failure in degree five or six on a positive 3-computad must use at least three generating 3-occurrences in its ordinary strict 3-label. A computad whose 3-labels all have weight at most two satisfies the assertion of Q1 in every dimension for these surfaces.

For precision, through degree four use the unrestricted reflection in §2. Above that, a surface filler necessarily has strict value an iterated identity on a strict 3-cell $c$. The theorem applies when $|c|\le 2$. At degree five it includes a noncontractible raw fibre. Equality of the winding numbers of the two raw paths, with common endpoints, is necessary as well as sufficient even after conversion.

### Cutting through a changing-width circuit

The positive planar fibre calculation gives a point, an interval or a circle for each strict label of weight at most two. In the circular case there are two serial orders and two edges between them. Each edge is a simultaneous circuit with two marker boxes (the two 3-events) in an ordinary planar circuit of 2-gates. The two circuits differ by the order of the markers along a shared nonempty wire word. This word passes unchanged through **both faces of each marker**. Expanding either marker in either direction gives the same one-marker sides in the two frames. The concrete wire ports matter, including when their labels coincide. These statements follow from the classification of two-marker circuits, also when the ordinary gates split or merge. [Positive planar fibres, Response 5](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#positive-3-computads-with-no-matching-idle-boundary-paths)

Choose one wire of the shared word. In the first frame trace it through both marker boxes, pairing its incoming and outgoing ports by the idle correspondence. Extend backwards from the earlier box to an external input, and forwards from the later box to an external output. At an ordinary gate there is always a port available in the required direction by positivity. The diagram is progressive, so the trace terminates at both boundaries. This is a simple time-monotone arc in the drawing. Colour regions to its left and right by $0,1$ respectively. At every edge occurrence the colours are stationary or go from $0$ to $1$. The path continues through the actual unchanged wire when either face is expanded inside a marker. All other wires and regions in that expansion are on their indicated side of the arc. Reversing the order of the two markers gives the other frame with the *same* colours on its four one-marker sides. In fact the interposed wire word is unchanged on all four expansions; outside the two boxes use the same exterior trace. Inside, use the same idle ports in the other order. This argument uses a wire of the shared word, not a count of how many wires lie to its left. That count can change during an expansion.

The region colours need only be consistent on **positions**. Here is why this suffices even when the object graph has loops. Use the occurrence polyplex of the strict label with its actual weak attaching terms, as in round-1 Response 40. A decorated representative of the strict cell supplies the colouring on this polyplex. Explicitly, form the strict positive cover with two copies of each object, all monotone endpoint lifts of each edge, and at each next generator a copy for each pair of lifted strict faces with matching globular boundary. The coloured frame is a representative in this cover: expand the two faces of either marked box along its idle port as above; ordinary external gates have the colours given by the trace. These expansions agree on every glued 1-boundary and give compatible 3-generator copies. The universal property of the strict cell polyplex gives its map into the cover. Reading the other frame gives the same lifted strict cell, since the two coloured frames have the same coloured serial sides and each induces the strict exchange there. Weak attaching terms lift along these positive generator labellings by the discrete Conduché theorem of Response 40. Its pullback formula lifts an entire purported surface filler with the old raw faces to the specified strict position, for either surface separately. If the old type has no raw filler below, it has none on the lifted polyplex, since any such term could be folded back. Equivalently one may first lift to the uncoloured universal polyplex; if its fibre is circular, perform the cut in that fibre and use its universal position as just described. [Positional lifting on positive computads](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-40)

Send the two colours to the poles of the suspension of the ordinary 2-scalar context on two colours, with unique crossing edge $g$. A stationary edge goes to an identity and every crossing edge to $g$. Give 2-generators raw path comparisons at those assignments. All their strict boundaries are the same edge or the same empty path, so these comparisons use no scalar. The two marked 3-generator occurrences on the universal polyplex straddle the cut; label them by the two suspended scalars at $1_g$, fitted at the actual raw 2-faces. Such fitting is raw: compare the written 1-boundaries with $g$ in the graph, transport the scalar to those poles, and compare its 2-faces to the assigned faces using only that graph. Equivalently use ordinary specified-boundary lifting through degree three at the prescribed strict generator. Distinct top occurrences on a positive polyplex have separate top generators in this two-marker frame. Giving them the same label would also detect the full twist. Stationary positions have only the point data. This is a raw substitution, so it preserves any proposed surface comparison. [Cut substitution and raw fitting in the rectangular case](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-41)

### The winding survives the cut

In the fixed hom of $g$ the two markers are two planar particles. The exchange edge in each chart is the specialization of the generic exchange of its two row slots. Its two temporal orders are the two paths around that square. The trace reads the two marker slots in one spatial order in the first frame and in the other order in the second. Consequently, when oriented between the *same* serial orders, their images are opposite half twists. To check this calculation in the presence of other gates, restrict the simultaneous circuit to the cut column. Each ordinary gate on the trace maps only to a comparison in the one-edge graph. Each marker maps to one scalar, and the order of the nonempty slots in the column is precisely their order along the trace. In the strict-unit braided interpretation of this local raw hom, empty rows contribute units; the generic exchange acts by the braiding on the two remaining rows, or its inverse when the square is traversed backwards. The four one-marker sides agree under the common trace and lie in one-particle components, so changing their endpoint fits contributes no loop. This is the same generic-square calculation as for the middle-wire example, now on a trace through arbitrary positive faces.

The circle generator therefore maps to the full two-strand twist, up to sign. A loop of winding $j$ maps to braid exponent $\pm2j$, which detects every nonzero winding. The suspension target reflects each *individual* surface in all dimensions by round-1 Responses 10 and 12. Thus there is no surface filler for two raw paths of different winding on the polyplex. A raw filler for equal windings follows from the fixed-boundary raw realization comparison: the two paths in the fibre are homotopic relative endpoints. The same argument gives raw existence in the interval and point cases. In degrees at least six the fibres have no higher homotopy groups, so parallel higher paths there already have raw fillers. This uses the planar fibre description in a 3-computad, where above dimension three the cells are groupoidal, and the positive 2-skeleton comparison at the lower poles. [Raw fibre calculation and fixed-boundary argument](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#a-positive-bubble-free-3-computad-with-a-circle-as-a-full-support-fibre) · [Suspension target](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-12)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_two_marker_cuts.py`. It enumerates the actual two-marker circuits, groups the two frames by their concrete serial sides, extracts input-to-output paths using ports idle in *both* face expansions, and compares their restrictions on all four sides before computing the braid of the two exchanges. With five inputs it detects every one of 33 circles through three gates and all 719 through four gates (35 and 856 compatible cut choices). The input alphabet has splitting, merging and width-changing gates, a single wire type and repeated gate labels. The proof gives the result without these search bounds.
