## Researcher Response 40

**Each of the ten individual ordinary surfaces is conservative in every dimension on every finite iterated planar substitution of Response 16. Bubbles may occur in every block, including the root and nonterminal blocks.** More generally, for either insertion or the full binary surface with disc removal, substitution into the 2-generators of an arbitrary finite planar computad preserves the existence of a surface model raw weakly equivalent to the free realization, provided each inserted presentation has such a model for that surface. Consequently the two-degree result for arbitrary higher extensions applies above each of these bases. For the *join*, the first-failure classification on the same iterated presentations is instead that of Response 16. [Planar substitutions and their attaching poles](#researcher-response-16) · [Replacement under higher attachments](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-32)

### The outer planar model with its actual gate transports

Fix an ordinary 2-computad $P$. Use a Gray-category $G(P)$ whose objects and 1-arrows are the vertices and edge words. Its 2-cells are **serial** planar histories of generating gates at actual offsets. Between two histories its 3-cells are paths of planar switches modulo endpoint homotopy in the planar tier complex. A switch specifies the actual ordered simultaneous row, even if another row gives the same serial ends. The presentation on paths is by the disjoint-switch squares and actual three-gate hexagons. Indeed with individual gate marks each tier cell is a product of permutohedra. The two-skeleton gives those relations. On forgetting the marks, each tier still orders every event by row and then by position in its row, so its stabilizer on vertex marks is trivial. Equivalently, lift paths and relations with the marks fixed at their start. This accounts for paths which induce a nonidentity permutation at the same unmarked serial end. [Marked and unmarked planar tier spaces](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#the-planar-diagram-spaces-are-aspherical)

At fixed object poles the hom 2-category uses vertical concatenation of histories and the corresponding whiskering and pasting of switch paths. The two orders of whiskering paths agree by disjoint-switch squares. Spatial whiskering shifts each positioned step onto the indicated substring. For cubical composition use the order left block then right block. Its compositor moves one block across the other by the rectangle of actual separated switches, using the horizontal order specified by the two input substrings. Different sweeps agree by disjoint-switch squares. Splitting a block subdivides the rectangle, and moving a switch from one block past a gate in the other gives the hexagon for the two simultaneous gates of that switch and the separated gate. With three side-by-side factors the elementary crossings are the same whiskered switches in either bracketing and the three-block reorderings agree by the same hexagon. Empty histories give the units. These are the strict hom and associative cubical composition laws of a Gray-category. At two empty substrings the left-right order specifies *which* crossing, whose inverse traverses that edge backwards. There is no identification of the two different horizontal orders at a shared empty gap.

For the published surface take $K(G(P))$, and for the binary surface take the history model $E(G(P))$ of round-1 Responses 5, 11 and 26. Denote the selected model by $Q(P)$. The raw generator map from $H_P$ is a weak equivalence. For $K$, at fixed 1-paths all raw components of the space of 2-cells are 1-types by the marked planar theorem. Serialization supplies the vertices and the two-row comparison maps to the class of its specified switch: its two column triangles are the identity on the chosen order and the cubical compositor for the two rows, with orientation given by the order. Rows before and after them whisker the same switch. This is the two-row calculation of Response 10 one dimension lower. Thus the map induces the equivalence of fundamental groupoids, including the loops coming from permutations. Its target fixed hom is exactly that groupoid. Below this comparison the graph flags are the free paths; number of gates rules out equivalences between distinct paths and the zero-gate component at an identity has no braid. For $E$ take the comparison on singleton atoms through the raw span with $K$. Normal evaluation identifies its 3-arrows at any two history objects with the paths between their normal evaluations, conjugating at the two ends. It is essentially surjective on 2-objects by singleton atoms. These arguments give the comparison for each palette of copies of the gates as well. [Evaluation of a row exchange](#reflection-at-the-written-boundary) · [History atoms, normal forms and the binary equations](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#interchange-at-the-binary-positions)

In $K$ the positions of a 2-cell are the actual generating events of its serial word. In $E$ use the generating events in the **flattened planar word of its normal evaluation**. An atom itself may contain many such events or none. Each 3-arrow transports these positions by its actual planar path. Giving fresh tags at the start lifts that arrow uniquely to the tagged models at the correspondingly tagged end. At a binary 2-object the normal representative sorts using binary addresses and stationary paths, which are unchanged by tagging. Thus assigning tags there tags each constituent atom history uniquely, and exchanges transport the tags by the rectangle of constituent switches. Applying the sorting comparisons gives exactly the two endpoint transports on arrows. For $K$ tagging commutes with concatenation, spatial shifts and the chosen cubical comparisons. Hence forgetting tags in either construction commutes with the surface operations, including specialized heads. This is the extra structure needed from the planar base; an arbitrary unstructured choice of a weak equivalence to a surface model would not by itself specify these transports.

### Raw substitution over a moving diagram

For each gate $g$ let $K_g$ be the inserted presentation, with a point for a stationary gate, and write $\mathcal D_g$ for its free realization. Over two fixed outer paths the inner category of the substituted computad has the following description. For each underlying labelled planar diagram type $w$, take its fully vertex-marked tier space $T_w$ with the set $O(w)$ of identified gate occurrences. Form
$$
 \left(T_w\ \times\!\prod_{o\in O(w)}\mathcal D_{g(o)}\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}(w)} .
$$
Take the disjoint union over $w$. The action transports the marks and the corresponding product factors together. One may include every component of the space with the indicated marks. The formula is an inner higher category: at an inner nerve rectangle $R$ use the space $\mathcal D_{g(o)}(R)$ in each factor. In particular, the base at that rectangle includes the full marked tier space, even when its gate permutation is trivial.

For discrete colour sets at the gates this is the marked mesh calculation itself. A fully marked lower mesh takes one independent colour at each of its events. A refinement transports its labels identically on the marks. Forgetting the marks is the action groupoid of simultaneous relabellings of that coloured mesh. The formula holds with outer cuts as well, since the external wire ports are ordered and gluing transports the tags of each piece to those of the pasted diagram. For space-valued colours resolve by simplicial sets: at a fixed inner rectangle the horizontal globe attachments are the parallel coloured gate attachments of the planar substitution calculation. Horizontal localization commutes with this geometric realization, and realization in spaces commutes with finite products. It commutes with the homotopy orbits as both are colimits. This gives the displayed formula from the discrete one, naturally in the rectangle. For inner Segal and completeness use the projection to the uncoloured planar groupoid. Locally over it the displayed spaces are products of inner nerves, with transition equivalences permuting the identified factors. The Segal pullbacks use fibre products over this same groupoid (equivalently pull back along the marking cover and check fibrewise), so the tests hold there and descend. The outer identity has no gates and the old graph flags are unchanged. This supplies the fixed-hom calculation at the written lower poles using the usual graph boundary fits. [Space-valued colour resolution](../answered/relative-coherence-all-dimensions-r6-2026-11-20.md#a-product-formula-for-substituting-higher-presentations-at-top-generators) · [Planar mesh gluing with automorphisms](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#the-planar-diagram-spaces-are-aspherical)

### Surface operations and comparison at the fibres

Choose a raw weak equivalence $H_{K_g}\to UY_g$ to the selected surface base of each child, using cofibrancy and fibrancy as in Response 17. Decorate $Q(P)$ as follows. Its lower objects and paths stay as they are. A 2-object carries an object of $Y_g$ per actual gate position. A 3-arrow carries the underlying planar 3-arrow and a child arrow for each input position, ending at the label of the **transported** output position. In degree $2+j$, $j>1$, the planar boundaries have the same 3-arrow and there is a child $j$-cell on each input position at its specified two child faces. Thus pure braids remain in the base, while two paths with different transported label endpoints cannot be compared by forgetting their positions.

Here are the head operations. Tag the contributing planar positions distinctly, transporting their names along the 3-strings of the arity by its planar arrows. A higher slot forces equality of the transports on its two 3-faces. Evaluate the head in the tagged $Q(P)$. In rank two the chosen planar paste uses the events of each contributing 2-slot once, including inside binary atoms. Above rank two the generic source visits exactly one 2-position from each transport class on the supporting tree. Hence a particular output tag can be read in the weight-one cut model $W_2(Y_g)$ of round-1 Response 37: give its position its child label wherever it occurs in the input, and use the weight-zero value on slots where it is absent. Use the child's track on the corresponding input strand at higher entries. Evaluate the same head there and take its weight-one result. Its two faces use precisely the labels at the indicated transported ends. Below degree two use the base operations.

For a nested evaluation the same tag splits back to the same actual input occurrences. On planar histories this follows by flattening each substituted word; in the binary model the intermediate sorting exchanges expand to their rectangles, with the endpoint comparisons composing. At the level of the generic tree this is the distinguished-position splitting, with at most one occurrence of the tag in each relevant 2-source. Thus the weight-one readings after substitution agree with those from the original pieces. In a surface equation the two evaluations in the **tagged** planar model agree, so the same tag and child tuple are selected on the common support. The $W_2(Y_g)$ equation supplies the equality for each selected coordinate. Use the retracted support for a word equation and the joined arity for insertion. Disc removal holds in the chosen full binary model. This is the tagged strand construction of round-1 Response 37 with actual planar gate positions in place of scalar positions. It does not require a gate to be at a crossing object cut in the ambient computad. [Weight-one cut and tagged surface operations](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-37) · [Two arity cuts for private attachments](#the-outer-diagrams-and-the-local-coordinates)

Assign the generators at each replaced gate to singleton positions with their indicated child values. Its private suspended attaching terms evaluate there by the two arity cuts, which recover the actual child operations. This gives the raw comparison to the decorated surface model at the original weak attaching paths. To verify it over fixed outer paths, forget the child entries. The base comparison is the equivalence to the planar groupoid proved above. Over a marked base diagram the target is the product with the child models: a twice-suspended hom head cuts back to its original inner head on each named position. On the source the corresponding fibre is the product of the $\mathcal D_g$ by the mesh calculation. At a serial representative the fibre comparison is the product of the chosen child equivalences. Indeed the fibre is obtained by putting one inner label at each gate of this fixed mesh. On its private suspension the comparison is the chosen generator map. Pasting the labelled mesh on the target selects that very input for each tag, with the other slots stationary. This holds for the whole inner rectangle and its restrictions by the cut-substitution formula. Equivalently one can check on a serial paste of the single-gate rectangles; the outer flag fits and choices of interleaving for that paste are contractible. This identifies the map on fibres without choosing a trivialization along a moving pure braid.

Finally the source is the indicated locally constant family over its planar groupoid and the target has unique tag transport along each planar path, acting by reindexing the factors. Over marked representatives the preceding fibre equivalences therefore give a fixed-hom equivalence after descent. More explicitly, a mapping category at untagged ends splits by the bijection from the chosen start marks to the end positions. For each such bijection use the mapping category at those tagged ends, **retaining all planar paths with that bijection**. Every target 2-object admits start marks, and pasting tagged inputs before forgetting them gives the same comparison. Together with the graph flags this proves the asserted raw weak equivalence. Induction from the arbitrary planar leaf models now proves the all-dimensional result. By round-1 Responses 31–32, above such a generating $r$-skeleton all arbitrary higher attachments preserve reflection through $r+2$ ($r\ge2$). The substitution hypothesis still requires that each child's attaching terms live at its one outer gate.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_braided_planar_substitution.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/BraidedPlanarSubstitution.agda` with an existing directory. A birth creates the two edges of a lens, a death consumes them and an internal point lies between them. Starting with two closed lenses, the solver enumerates 19 serial words and 38 distinctly tagged words. The possible return permutations are exactly the identity and the simultaneous exchange of both triples of gates. Exchanging the lenses twice returns every tag but has signed birth crossing two; the signed count is invariant by the planar squares and hexagons (forget all points except the two birth events in a planar braid). Independently quotienting the actual two-colour schedules gives 36 components, with eight fixed colourings and 28 paired colourings at the chosen word. The solver also checks 360 transports of local updates and reads both orders of two different child letters at either inner gate. Agda 2.8.0 checks the mixed terms on the two closed lenses with their padded weak path poles and the selected child terms. [Braided planar substitution solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_braided_planar_substitution.py)

## Researcher Response 41

**For the variable coherence rule of every admissible directed kernel in §1.3, \(A,U\) reflect old inhabitation through degree four under the following hypotheses. The 2-skeleton is globular, and the ordinary strict faces of each 3-generator are individual 2-generators.** The raw faces of a 3-generator may have different writings and different written 1-poles from those of the corresponding variables. The 4-attachments are arbitrary. This extends Response 5, which required literal variable faces below the tested degree. The transport theorem below works for both coherence rules and in higher degrees.

### Changing the faces of a generator

Fix \(n\ge3\), a common raw base \(B\) through degree \(n-1\), and fixed parallel \((n-2)\)-cells \(p,q\). In any extension \(X\), form the category \(C_X(p,q)\) whose objects are raw \((n-1)\)-cells \(p\to q\). Its arrows are raw \(n\)-cells at the indicated faces modulo existence of raw \((n+1)\)-cells in **both** directions. Top composition gives composition. It respects the quotient by fixed whiskering, and the temporal associativity and unit comparisons on rows give the category laws. Equality of arrows is exactly mutual reachability at their written boundary.

Suppose \(X\) has an \(n\)-generator \(z:u\to v\) in this category. In \(B\) choose changes of poles \(a:p'\to p\), \(b:q\to q'\), with reverse \((n-1)\)-cells. Require both composites at each end to be isomorphic to the corresponding identity as objects of the relevant category \(C_B\). Let \(Ww=(a;w);b\), with \(;\) denoting composition of \((n-1)\)-cells at cut \(n-2\). At the new poles choose raw faces \(u',v'\) and isomorphisms \(Wu\cong u'\), \(Wv\cong v'\) in \(C_B(p',q')\). All poles compared in such a move have the required literal parallel lower boundaries. Identity poles or identity face changes are allowed.

Replace \(z\) by \(z':u'\to v'\). One may do this independently for every \(n\)-generator. Transfer the actual \((n+1)\)-attachments by the raw substitution constructed below. **Reflection through \(n+1\) is equivalent in the two presentations**, for any of the binary surfaces and either directed coherence rule. The witnesses to the changes of poles and faces are required in the raw base. There is no assumption on the direction of the generators being moved.

**Whiskering is an equivalence of categories at these poles.** On arrows \(W\) uses the same two fixed whiskers. It is a functor: comparison of a whiskered composite with the composite of its whiskerings has a generic arity with a temporal string of moving \(n\)-cells at just one \((n-1)\)-position. The fixed slots at the other positions are \((n-1)\)-cells. This tree has no separated pair at any cut. The two raw \((n+1)\)-comparisons follow by lifting the ordinary identity there. The same argument gives the identity law. Nested fixed whiskers are naturally isomorphic to those with their fixed factors first associated together. The components are raw associators, and naturality with one moving \(n\)-cell uses a string with only that one moving position. Unit whiskers use the corresponding unitors and their one-position naturality. These again have fork-free arities. All assertions refer to the actual binary writings with the indicated bracketings. [Raw lifts on fork-free arities](#researcher-response-7)

Changing a fixed factor by an isomorphism \(c\) in \(C_B\) gives a natural isomorphism of whiskering functors. There is a direction issue here. First whisker \(c\) by the stationary object to obtain the components. They are invertible by the functor law just proved for a single fixed whisker. For an arbitrary arrow \(t\) in the moving position, the two routes in the naturality square admit the native directed comparison on the \((n-2)\)-fold suspension of the elementary square. To get the return, write the routes as \(LR\) and \(RL\), where \(L\) is the change in the invertible position and \(R\) the other change, each at the required stationary value of its neighbour. For the convention \(RL\to LR\), start at \(LR\), insert \(L^{-1}L\) at the end, apply the native comparison to \(RL^{-1}\), and cancel \(LL^{-1}\) at the start. This gives \(RL\). If the invertible position is \(R\), insert \(RR^{-1}\) at the start, switch \(R^{-1}L\) using the native orientation for that pair as appropriate, and cancel at the end: explicitly \(LR\to RR^{-1}LR\to RLR^{-1}R\to RL\). The insertion and cancellation use only the two inverse identities after fixed whiskering. Temporal reassociation and unit comparisons fit these operations to the two original routes. Thus the naturality square commutes in \(C_X\). [Native square at every cut](#a-separated-pair-in-any-higher-hom)

Taking the reverse pole changes gives a functor \(V\) in the other direction. By the associator comparisons, the two composites \(VW,WV\) are naturally isomorphic to whiskering by the corresponding composites of fixed factors. Use their given isomorphisms to identities and then the unit comparisons. This constructs \(VW\cong 1\) and \(WV\cong 1\) in every extension. In particular \(W\) is fully faithful. Choosing representatives for the base isomorphisms makes the construction by raw expressions uniform under substitutions fixing \(B\).

**The substitutions at written faces.** Conjugate \(W\) by the two chosen face isomorphisms. This gives a bijection between arrows \(u\to v\) and arrows \(u'\to v'\) in every \(C_X\) involved. Its inverse on arrows is represented by undoing the face isomorphisms, applying \(V\), and using \(1\cong VW\) at \(u,v\). This is a left inverse by naturality and is the inverse since \(W\) is fully faithful. Define a raw substitution \(F\) from old generators to terms in the new context by taking that inverse expression on \(z'\). Define \(G\) from new generators to old terms by taking the forward expression on \(z\). Both fix \(B\). On the respective generators \(GF(z)\) and \(FG(z')\) represent the original arrows in \(C_X\), hence have raw comparisons both ways with the original variables.

The same holds for **all raw \(n\)-terms**. Here it matters that a term can start with an unbiased coherence head. For a head with a maximal \(n\)-slot, the full Gray projection of an operation implies that only one outer column can have positive hom dimension. Indeed the column containing that slot already has hom dimension \(n-1\), and the sum of hom dimensions is bounded by \(n-1\) at output rank \(n\). A coherence case at this output rank has no \(n\)-slot, including with the variable rule. Raise a chosen maximal \(n\)-slot to two parallel copies with an \((n+1)\)-variable between them. The old generic \(n\)-head evaluated on either copy has the same literal lower faces. Its strict value carries the raised step between the two evaluations: expand structural Godement products into chronological composition and fixed whiskers. In that spelling a generating \(n\)-slot occurs once by its ordinary top count. A proper structural letter at degree \(n\) uses lower-dimensional inputs; the occurrence is therefore a variable with fixed whiskers and a temporal prefix and suffix. Whisker the raised variable there. This uses only the sesquicategory operations of the fixed signature, independently of additional equations on the strict paths. Its ordinary projection covers the whole raised tree, since the original ordinary paste was the total. There is still only one active outer column, so the projection has full Gray support as well. A raw operation head therefore supplies the comparison at the two literal generic faces. Either choice of a *raw input* comparison can be substituted, yielding the required two orientations when the input arrows represent the same class. Changing one top slot at a time and inducting on the term proves the assertion. Lower labels are fixed literally. [Single-column support](#researcher-response-2) · [Chronological letters and structural products in the fixed signature](../answered/lax-interchange-every-dimension-r10-2026-11-20.md)

For each old \((n+1)\)-declaration take its \(F\)-image boundary as the new attaching type, extending \(F\) by the corresponding new variable. Extend \(G\) by using the old variable and fitting its two \(n\)-faces to the \(GF\)-images with the just-proved comparisons. If a surface inhabits an old type, substitution by \(F\) gives a surface filler at its image. Assuming reflection there, apply \(G\) to a raw filler. Through degree \(n\) the tested boundary is fixed. In degree \(n+1\), compose the two comparisons at the ends to recover the original type. The reverse implication uses \(FG\) on new \(n\)-terms in the same way. No comparison on \((n+1)\)-variables is needed. Higher declarations can be truncated by the arity bound.

### Applying the change to directed conservativity

Let \(B\) be globular through degree \(n-1\). Suppose the ordinary strict values of the faces \(u,v\) of each \(n\)-generator are individual variables \(A,D\) of degree \(n-1\). Require the written \((n-2)\)-poles \(p,q\) of \(u,v\) to be literally parallel to the corresponding poles \(f,g\) of \(A,D\), respectively. Ordinary equality of the strict pole values forces \(A,D\) to have the same variable poles \(f,g\). At \(n=3\) the parallelism requirement is automatic: 1-terms projecting to an edge have its literal object endpoints.

The pole \(p\) has ordinary value the single variable \(f\). By positional lifting, it is a substitution instance on that variable's globe, and likewise for \(q\). The forward and backward pole changes exist at the prescribed faces, as do their inverse comparisons and the comparisons making these invertible in \(C_B\). Indeed every one of these diagrams over its single globe has the required ordinary identity value with the specified parallel faces, and that globe is fork-free. Positional uniqueness makes the lifted lower faces agree literally before this test. Next \(Wu\) and \(A\) are parallel at \(f,g\), with ordinary value \(A\): the pole changes had ordinary identity value. The same globe argument on the globe of \(A\) supplies a 3-isomorphism for \(n=3\), or an \(n\)-isomorphism in general, with the required inverse data. Apply it also to \(Wv,D\). These constructions work for both coherence rules; a test for equality after folding strict labels in a cyclic computad is unnecessary. [Positional lifting on globular generators](#researcher-response-5)

After the change all declarations below degree \(n+1\) have variable faces. For the variable rule, Response 5 now gives reflection for every \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\) through \(n+1\), proving the stated fourth-degree result. For the full-support rule one also gets reflection through \(n+1\) for all binary surfaces if the normalized lower globular skeleton has no separated pair at a cut \(k<n-1\), by Response 7 with \(h=n+1\). These conclusions allow arbitrary directed \((n+1)\)-generators at the original written attaching types. They do not establish Q3(a) on all finite 2-skeleta or at arbitrary composite strict faces of 3-generators.

**Validation.** `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_directed_reattachment.py --agda-dir DIR` builds a raw return on the elementary directed square using the inverse of its first change and two oriented cancellation witnesses. It composes seven typed comparisons, including the native square for the inverse, and checks its suspension at the positive cut. It also constructs both substitutions when the old 1-source is a unit-padded edge and the new 1-source is the edge itself. Run `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/DirectedReattachment.agda` in the same directory setup. The certified Gray kernel verifies the concrete terms and their exact declared faces. The admissible-\(E\) argument uses the native direction and fork-free arities as proved above. [Raw return and pole substitutions](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_directed_reattachment.py)

## Researcher Response 42

**The all-dimensional theorem for planar substitution extends to simultaneous rewrites of words along the wires of an arbitrary planar block.** The structural diagram may have floating components, nested bubbles and gates on empty paths. Each wire rule can replace an entire unary string by another, including the empty string at either end. The rule may change the number of generating 2-cells in that string. All ten individual ordinary surfaces reflect on these presentations, including at old faces carrying a nontrivial outer braid. Finite iteration and the two-degree bound above the resulting generating skeletons hold as in Response 40.

More precisely, partition the actual edges into families of parallel edges connected by unary transitions (2-generators). For each family $e$ let $W_e$ be a finite local 2-computad: its objects are these edge colours, its arrows are the unary transitions and its 2-generators are the directed word rewrites. In the ambient context these have dimensions one, two and three. A rewrite may use any raw writings of its prescribed strict unary strings at parallel faces. Choose each structural gate $g$ with its actual input and output words of edge colours. At $g$ one may use the full private substitution by $K_g$ of Response 40. Its objects give gate colours, all with these same actual ports. Assume the child bases have the surface-model comparisons of that response (use a point for a stationary gate). These and the wire rules are the generating attachments. Thus a gate-free change acting on an entire word on one wire is allowed, including one on the identity of that edge. A free 3-scalar on the identity of an empty *spatial* path is a separate kind of attachment treated in Response 31 under its hypotheses. [Local wire presentations](#researcher-response-31) · [Private gate substitutions](#researcher-response-40)

### Fixed-hom calculation including motion of components

Fold each edge family to one edge, suppress the unary transitions and use one gate for each structural gate family. Call this planar computad $P$. Fix the two **actual** outer path boundaries. For a folded diagram $w$ between their images, let $O(w)$ be its structural gates and $I(w)$ its wire segments, ending at a structural port or an exterior port. The gate and exterior ports prescribe actual endpoint colours $x_i,y_i$ on every segment, including through-wires. In the notation of Response 40 the raw category of 2-cells and higher over this diagram type is
$$
 \left(T_w\times\prod_{o\in O(w)}\mathcal D_{g(o)}
       \times\prod_{i\in I(w)}\mathcal W_{e(i)}(x_i,y_i)\right)_{h\operatorname{Aut}(w)} .
$$
Here $T_w$ is the fully marked planar tier space, $\mathcal W_e(x,y)$ is the fixed hom of the free weak category on $W_e$, and $\mathcal D_g$ is the free realization of $K_g$. Automorphisms act simultaneously on the components, the gates, and their incident segments, fixing all exterior ports. Distinct components of the marking space are allowed. Marks on the gates determine the segments because the ports are ordered and a wire with no structural vertex ends on the exterior. There is no independent choice of a wire permutation.

To see the change from the anchored formula of Response 31, first take a horizontal lower chart with gate colours discrete and with a change index. At each structural gate it records its colour (or its indexed child label). On each segment it records an ordered word of unary transitions and indexed wire markers between its fixed end colours. Conversely these data subdivide the corresponding segment in the folded diagram. After fully marking the structural gates, every subdivision vertex is distinguished by the segment and its index along that segment. Forgetting subdivisions is a weak equivalence on the lower components **at those fixed discrete data**. Indeed they are 1-types by planar asphericity, and their marked loop groups are generated faithfully by the pure braids on the sibling floating components in each face, with each strand carrying its descendant components. Inserting a unary vertex in an edge leaves the connected components, their nesting, all faces and all exterior attachments unchanged. Each generating motion still moves the full subdivided component. Erasing its extra vertices takes it to exactly the corresponding unsubdivided motion. Thus the map on the pure groups is an isomorphism, also on an entirely anchored piece or the empty diagram. The components themselves match by subdivision of the actual port diagram and planar serialization. A change index on a unary marker expands it to either specified word on that same segment. The map just used is actual erasure, so it commutes with all these restrictions and with restriction of gate labels. [Marked component braid decomposition](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-164) · [Lower chart calculation for whole unary words](#the-raw-mapping-space-with-fixed-ports)

For detail on realization, before inner categorification the diagram on a segment is the word in the local change-index graph of $W_e$, with endpoint expansions as above. Its inner categorification is exactly $\mathcal W_e(x_i,y_i)$ by the transition presentation. At an inner rectangle a private gate contributes the corresponding parallel label of its child's cellular presentation. This follows by evaluating the suspended globe attachments there, just as for the space-valued colours in Response 40. The wire globes attach on their specified unary words and have no structural gate to colour. Thus at discrete indices the combined horizontal calculation is the subdivision calculation above, with independent choices at gates and segments. Resolve space-valued gate labels by discrete simplicial levels. Realization preserves these finite products and the simultaneous homotopy orbits. Inner categorification preserves products, carries a groupoidal parameter to that same groupoid, and preserves coproducts and homotopy orbits as a localization. Equivalently take the associated family over the planar groupoid and check the inner Segal products over the marking cover. This yields the displayed formula. On the input and output paths use the usual free graph fits. For wire rules at different raw writings of the same strict unary word, the lower fitting components are contractible: their diagrams consist solely of boundary-anchored unary vertices with a fixed word. The fixed-boundary comparison therefore uses precisely the declared attachments. [Space-valued horizontal calculation](#raw-substitution-over-a-moving-diagram) · [Transition presentation](#why-chart-components-suffice-for-4-tracks)

### A model that reads the wire histories at the actual braided ends

Use the planar surface base $Q(P)$ of Response 40 for the folded skeleton. The following additional reading is needed because stacking can turn two segments into a single segment. For an auxiliary palette of copies of structural gates, retain the actual graph and the **strict planar 2-diagrams with these gate names and unary words**. At a folded diagram in which each auxiliary gate name occurs at most once, all gate ports and segments are individually determined. Between 2-diagrams with this same folded diagram take in degree three a serial local wire-rewrite history per segment, and in degree four its local track per segment. There is no gate decoration in this auxiliary reading. On $W_e$ use the local Gray-category of actual words, serial rewrites and planar switch paths from Response 31, now also the planar model construction of Response 40. Hence a local track remembers which chart exchanges the rewrites, even on empty local words.

These data define the low grades of a cartesian enrichment in Gray-categories as in Response 31. Under stacking, join all constituent pieces of each resulting segment in port order by local cubical Gray composition; the interchanger is the block interchanger on the joined pieces. For three stacks the same successive pieces occur in the same order, and local Gray associativity, subdivision and naturality give the composition laws. Exterior juxtaposition uses disjoint sets of segments. The argument uses unique *incidence* identification of the named segments; no assertion about contractibility of their folded diagram space enters the definition of the reading. For a folded diagram repeating a name, use one 3-arrow per pair of 2-diagrams with that folded diagram and its identity track. Allow no 3-arrows between different folded strict diagrams. Repetition persists under planar pasting and all higher compositions preserve their common folded shape, so laws at a repeated-name output hold by uniqueness. This gives a global enrichment for each auxiliary palette. Take $K_o$ of it for insertion or $E_o$ for binary, as in round-1 Response 42. These models retain the strictly evaluated 2-diagrams. At a low grade the 3-objects and 4-arrows are equivalent by normal evaluation to the product of wire history groupoids, fully faithfully at both original endpoints. Injections of auxiliary palettes preserve the constructions, including binary sorting transports. [Local Gray composition along joined segments](#a-surface-target-through-degree-five) · [Both surface interpretations](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-42)

Assemble one surface model as follows. Through degree one retain the original graph paths. A 2-object includes an outer $Q(P)$ object at the folded boundaries, its gate labels in the child surface models, and a wire-reading 2-object at the actual path boundaries. Use the outer positions as distinct auxiliary gate names. Require the strict folded diagram of this wire reading to be exactly the flattened planar diagram of the outer object with those marks. At degree three retain the outer planar path, a child arrow for each transported gate as in Response 40, and a wire-reading arrow between the two wire objects **after identifying names along that path**. The common named strict diagram exists because the path transports the actual incidence diagram. At higher degrees use the corresponding child and wire cells at the specified parallel ends. Above degree three the two planar paths already agree. In particular two different base braids with the same induced permutation retain different base values.

For an operation refine the planar tuple by tagging its output positions and propagate those tags to each contributing argument along its transport strings. Evaluate the planar and gate entries by the tagged operations of Response 40. Evaluate the same head on the whole wire tuple in the auxiliary wire model on this common palette. Its folded 2-diagram is the strict paste of the named input diagrams, hence it satisfies the incidence condition at the output. Every contributing occurrence in any one 2-face uses its name once. Successive refinements choose the same actual gate occurrences as refinement after substitution, including normal sorting of binary histories. The wire readings are relabelled by injections on each restricted tuple, so composition of evaluations follows from substitution in their model. In an equation the tagged planar evaluations agree, giving the same named tuple on the common support or spliced arity. The wire model equation on that tuple and the child equations give the required equality, including at converted faces. This supplies operations at all grades of the assembled algebra.

Assign a gate generator its singleton planar position, its actual port diagram and its child value. A unary transition gets an empty structural history and its actual unary diagram, and a wire rule gets the single local change at its prescribed 2-faces. The wire reading of a private child attachment is constructed at the stationary named 2-diagram of its gate. At rank three choose an empty wire history. In the published reading this is the single zero-change history there. In the binary reading any two zero-change representatives at that stationary diagram have empty normal wire histories, and there is exactly one track between them: without a local rewrite the path component on each unary word is contractible. All the suspended private faces have zero local change count by substitution. At degree four take that unique track at the evaluated faces, and above degree four parallel such tracks agree and the forced identities suffice. This inductively gives the assignment without a choice of binary bracketing for a composite child term. These choices evaluate on the written types.

For the raw comparison, over fully marked representatives the category of child entries at a fixed planar path is the corresponding product of fixed child homs. The wire groupoid between the two subdivided diagrams is the product of local history groupoids in the published reading and equivalent to it by normal evaluation in the binary reading. At actual raw ends the comparison on wires sends a local elementary exchange to the specified planar switch by the two-row calculation, with the binary conjugations at the endpoints. It is therefore the planar equivalence for $W_e$ on each local hom. Objects there are realized by their unary words and arrows by serializing their local changes. A useful way to verify the comparison with the displayed raw formula is first to fix a serial marked skeleton, insert the prescribed ordered subdivisions in its segments and paste the single-gate rectangles. The gate comparison on that fibre is the selected child equivalence by the private cuts; the wire comparison reads exactly the inserted word and the successive indexed local changes. Any outer switch between unrelated pieces leaves these local words fixed after incidence transport. Forgetting subdivisions downstairs and normal evaluation upstairs compare the full planar base by Response 40. Since that base is a groupoid, one may check the fibre equivalences at serial representatives and then descend along its paths, keeping the full base path at each pair of ends. Every outer object admits the marks and every target subdivision with those marks occurs in the source fibre. This proves the equivalence at fixed outer paths. A 2-equivalence of paths can use no structural gates by their nonnegative count, and in the gate-free part each local $W_e$ has discrete object flags: an invertible local 2-change has zero generator count and a word invertible in the resulting free path category is empty. Thus the graph comparison also gives the lower flags. The assembled surface model is raw weakly equivalent to the free presentation. Apply the same argument recursively and then the higher base change of round-1 Responses 31–32 for the claimed consequences.

For instance take two copies of a closed two-wire lens and place a generating 3-endomorphism of the identity on one of its edge colours. At a 3-history with three such changes on each of the two corresponding segments and fixed identity outer braid, its raw 4-loop group is $\mathrm{Br}_3\times\mathrm{Br}_3$. This follows by applying the formula to the two local 2-scalar homs. The actual outer exchange transports the two factors into one another. Its square transports each back but retains the full twist in the underlying outer 3-path. Thus the ordinary surface model must retain more than the permutation on the equal lenses.

**Reproduce.** In an existing directory `DIR` run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_braided_wire_changes.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/BraidedWireChanges.agda`. The two marked closed lenses each enclose a scalar gate. The checker expands a unary step on either wire to two successive steps through a different edge colour. With no subdivisions, the two single steps, one expanded step, and both expanded steps, it finds 38, 552, 1,500 and 4,518 marked schedules. In each case it checks the spanning-tree return words on the actual signed graph by their full Artin action, verifies they are powers of the full block twist, executes that twist, and checks erasure onto every folded schedule. The last case checks 12,411 return words. It also checks the noncommuting local braid generators and the actual swap of segment labels. Agda 2.8.0 checks a change of birth colour on one closed lens composed with a one-gate versus two-gate wire rewrite on another at their written faces. [Braided wire solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_braided_wire_changes.py)

## Researcher Response 43

**For both coherence rules and every admissible \(E\) on the signature of §1.3, associativity and units are conservative through degree four in the following finite presentations.** There are two distinct objects \(0,1\). Every generator of positive degree through four goes from \(0\) to \(1\) at its object poles. The 2-generators have variable 1-faces. The 3- and 4-generators have **arbitrary raw attaching terms**, including changes between composite words and changes at identity words. The result tests every old boundary, including one written using operations across several outer columns. More generally it applies at an exterior word of length one in cyclic presentations as described below.

There is an exact criterion for an old fourth-degree boundary. In the hom from \(0\) to \(1\), take the generating global arrows as local objects, the 2-generators as local arrows, and the 3-generators as local rules between paths. A local 2-cell is an **ordered history** of positioned rule applications, with its initial path specified. The 4-generators give changes between two such histories. The permitted directed moves are those changes in context and a switch of independent local rules from right-first to left-first. An old 4-type is inhabited, raw or with \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\), exactly when the history of its source reaches the history of its target. Repetitions and rules with empty source or target are allowed. At empty substrings independence means two separate slots of a positioned horizontal paste, with their displayed order; a shared gap can supply such a paste. Equivalently the test is reachability in degree three of the free local lax-Gray category on these data. [Positioned rewrite presentation, round-1 Response 2](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-2)

### Reading an arity before identifying its labels

For a single outer column \(\Sigma P\), where \(P\) is a globular pasting context, its crossing strict \(E\)-cells through global degree three read, in order of degree, as the vertices of \(P\), the paths in \(P\), and the ordered local 2-histories on \(P\). Here are the checks for this assertion. A proper outer crossing needs two adjacent active outer intervals. A single outer column has no such pair. Expand each structural product into temporal composition and fixed whiskering. In degree three this leaves individual generating changes in chronological order. The only possible additional primitive at this rank is the outer crossing, whose unit specializations are identities. Conversely each positioned history has a spelling by these operations. [Degree-three signature and realization](#researcher-response-13)

Distinct histories on this arity stay distinct under the admissible equations. Ordinary projection fixes their per-column paths of generating changes, with the individual versions of each change. Indeed the ordinary hom on the local tree is the product of its columns; its one-dimensional factor paths are free on the corresponding edges. On the global tree this computation uses the projection to the **global Gray** hom, which is the ordinary strict category on \(P\). The only remaining discrepancy could be an interleaving across two local columns. A discrepancy reverses a particular pair of occurrences in different columns. Within its column an occurrence crosses a specific interval of the forward spine, visited just once. Clamp to that pair. Map all parallel versions at either chosen interval to its distinguished change, the other intervals to the appropriate stationary identities, and all intervening local columns to units. Higher globular labels over identified versions go to identities. This is the version-clamping substitution into the suspended elementary square. It would identify the two schedules there. Their native inner square would then have an identity return, contrary to requirement 3. This proves the asserted distinction using equations on trees alone. Substituting histories in a retained top slot places its history in that slot and concatenates in the displayed order. This follows by expanding the same strict expression before substitution. [Version clamping and the separated square](#a-separated-pair-in-any-higher-hom)

Every global 4-cell on \(\Sigma P\) reads as a local directed path between its boundary histories. In a strict expression only the global 4-variables and the inner square can supply proper steps: a proper outer naturality or join requires another active outer interval. The inner square on composite inputs is a right-first to left-first block switch. In the local Gray category it is realized by switching the individual steps of the two blocks in succession, retaining their internal orders. All matched 3-faces in the expression have identical local histories by the preceding argument. Extra equations in degree four can identify the witnessing paths without affecting their existence. This calculation uses the fixed signature and its unit specializations; it requires no splitting equation for the inner square in \(E\). [Native generators and the outer join](#which-degree-four-operations-survive-the-head-test)

To read any head in the two-object presentation, colour the vertices of its bound tree by the two outer objects of the actual tuple. The colours are nondecreasing. At crossing output keep the unique crossing column \(j\). On the strict generic value apply the substitution to \(\Sigma P_j\) that keeps this column and sends all stationary columns to identity towers at their respective poles. Read the resulting expression as above, and evaluate it on the local readings of the retained labels. A stationary output has only its point value. On a crossing variable use its local generator. This defines the local attaching paths of a 3-declaration and then the two local histories of a 4-declaration, in declaration order.

For precision, this reading interprets even a derivation using conversion. Take the local free Gray category through local degree two, with a single local 3-cell precisely at each reachable pair. Use it as the crossing hom, with terminal stationary homs and empty reverse hom. A nested generic evaluation through global degree three commutes with clamping: the other columns factor through a point and the retained strict expression substitutes its paths and chronological lists exactly as above. Thus evaluating at a converted generic type with the same designated strict value gives the same operation. In a binary word, fixed whiskering by a stationary outer factor acts as the identity on the reading. Composition in a positive cut becomes composition at the cut one lower. Consequently both sides of each \(A,U\) word equation read equally; its coherence-case head reads as the identity. This verifies the equality checks on generic tuples and hence on all specializations. In degree four the clamped strict expression supplies a path by the preceding paragraph and equality at a fixed boundary is automatic. Above the tested degree one may put a unique cell at each parallel boundary. This proves necessity for a surface inhabitant. In particular it never identifies the two inner schedules merely because their **global** Gray projections agree. [Cut calculation on binary words](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-11)

### Returning the directed path to the original raw faces

Let \(B=X_{\le2}\). This is a globular set with no separated pair: two generating 2-cells cannot be composed across an object, and there are no higher generators. By Response 7 every ordinary strict value on \(B\) lifts at specified raw faces in either rule. Its crossing strict 2-cells are just words in the local arrows. In particular, at fixed written 1-poles any two base 2-writings of a word are joined by a base 3-isomorphism modulo mutual raw 4-reachability. That base isomorphism is unique at the specified ends: all base 3-terms there have ordinary identity value and the ordinary identity in degree four lifts both ways. The same conclusion applies to stationary terms on an object. These are assertions about witnesses in the base, so the indicated equalities continue to hold after any higher attachments. [Fork-free lifting](#researcher-response-7)

Write \(C_X(p,q)\) for the category in Response 41 at \(n=3\). Its arrows carry the order given by directed raw 4-reachability. Each crossing raw 1-pole \(p\) has the value of a single generating arrow \(f\). In the graph choose raw 2-changes between \(p\) and \(f\) in both directions. Their composites are isomorphic to the identities by base lifting. Whiskering by the pole changes therefore gives the equivalence of Response 41 from \(C_X(p,q)\) to \(C_X(f,g)\). It also reflects the order on each hom-set: both whiskering functors are monotone and their round trips are naturally isomorphic to the identity, with cancellation by the invertible components. For each local path \(U:f\to g\) choose a raw base representative \(N(U)\) at \(f,g\). Given a raw 3-arrow, transport its poles and conjugate its ends by the unique base isomorphisms to these representatives. The resulting arrow will be called its normalized arrow. For parallel written arrows this procedure preserves and reflects their order. [Whiskering equivalence and directed naturality](#researcher-response-41)

At each \(f,g\) keep the paths \(U\) as objects and the ordered hom-sets between the representatives \(N(U)\) in \(C_X(f,g)\). These data admit strict left and right whiskering by paths: whisker by the corresponding representative, then conjugate to the representatives of the concatenated paths using the unique base isomorphisms. These operations and top composition make a local 2-sesquicategory (categories of 2-arrows with associative and unital fixed whiskering). Here is the compatibility at nonliteral intermediate poles. If \(d:r\to p\) is the fixed 2-factor and \(L_r,L_p\) are the chosen 2-changes **from** the normalized 1-poles, the two fixed factors
\[
                        L_r;d,\qquad N(\overline d);L_p
\]
are parallel at the same written poles with the same strict 2-word. Their base isomorphism is natural for whiskering a moving 3-arrow by Response 41, including the reverse square for an invertible change. At the outside ends its components are base arrows, hence the unique ones between those writings. This proves agreement after endpoint conjugation. For a right factor use the pole changes to the normalized poles on the right. Nested fixed whiskers use the natural associator from the same response; its components again coincide with the base transports. Unit whiskers use its natural unitor. These checks give strict associativity of each action, their mutual compatibility, and preservation of top composition. They also show that normalizing a raw whiskered arrow gives its normalized whiskering. No exchange of two arbitrary moving factors was used.

Interpret each local 2-generator by the normalized arrow of its global 3-declaration. We claim that for **every** raw 3-term its normalized arrow is the evaluation of its history in this sesquicategory. Consider one head application. Each stationary label is literally a term on its one outer object: a positive-dimensional crossing variable cannot be used on a stationary interval, since object spines are nondecreasing and a head uses its positional support. Thus first substitute the *actual stationary terms*, however they are written, at the two poles of \(\Sigma P_j\), keeping the crossing variables generic. This gives a raw term on a single outer column. Subsequent substitution of the retained tuple gives the original head application literally. The strict value on the single column is the clamped one since point terms have only identity strict values.

On this single-column tree normalize the poles and 2-faces of the specialized head as above using only its base through degree two. This base is fork-free. Build another raw representative at those same faces by spelling its history with the top variables of the column, fixed 2-whiskers, temporal composition and the base fits for the representative words. The two parallel representatives have equal \(E\)-value there: both are the same chronological history, and the base fits insert identities in that reading. The single-column comparison of Response 2 gives raw 4-cells both ways, also for the full-support rule. Indeed on the supporting subtree the global Gray projection is the ordinary single-factor value, so the identity comparison has full support there. Specialize both comparisons. All base choices from the tree now lie in \(B\); the pole and endpoint normalization just proved identifies the specialized result with the same spelling on the normalized input arrows. Induction on the proper top arguments proves the claim. A head with no crossing change has the empty history; the same argument compares it to the fitted identity. This step accounts for crossings with nonliteral point terms and for all raw writings of the original 3-faces. [Single-column full-support comparison](#researcher-response-2)

Finally each local directed move gives an inequality of normalized arrows. For a declared change this follows by normalizing the actual 4-variable and applying the claim to its two actual 3-faces. For a native switch apply the raw suspended elementary square to representatives of the two moving arrows. Use their fixed whiskers and the base transports at the intermediate and outside words. The intermediate transports cancel, and the outside transports are precisely those in the normalized operations. This gives the indicated orientation. Placing a move inside a longer history or at a fixed path context preserves order by composition and fixed whiskering. A directed sequence therefore gives an inequality between the normalized arrows of the two old 3-terms. Order reflection returns a raw 4-cell at their literal boundary. Stationary boundaries lift on the point. The lower degrees were already settled on arbitrary computads. This proves sufficiency and the assertion through four.

### Unary exterior words in general presentations

There is a fixed-query consequence even if the object graph has cycles and many spatially composable generators. Give every generating edge length one. Suppose the 2-relations preserve length, and take a query whose exterior path has length one. Use the two-sided interval of this grade from Response 32. Its only object positions are before and after the sole edge. Every placed edge crosses. Suppose every placeable 2-generator has literal variable faces and no 2-, 3- or 4-declaration on an empty exterior path is placeable at either stationary end. Longer declarations cannot occur. Then the fourth-degree criterion applies at the query, with arbitrary placeable 3- and 4-attachments. Indeed, between the fixed initial and final object copies there are no internal edge positions. A positive-dimensional declaration used in a crossing term must have just these exterior copies, since its column is the unique crossing and its stationary neighbours contain no generating arrows. Retain those declarations and their dependencies. This is the two-object presentation above, and placement and folding preserve the original judgments. In particular the result applies on any graph with globular 2-skeleton and positive exterior length for all 3- and 4-declarations at every query of exterior length one. [Exact placement and folding of old types](#researcher-response-32)

In the four effective settings of §1.3 the history at an old face is computable: after the generic clamp, enumerate the finitely many interleavings of the projected per-column paths and compare their strict values on that tree. On finite 3-computads in the stated class **individual degree-four inhabitation is decidable**, even with compound 3-attachments. More generally this holds if the differences in the numbers of local rule occurrences between the two faces of the 4-declarations are all nonnegative, or all nonpositive. In the first case bound the number of occurrences by the count at the proposed target, in the second by the count at the source. If \(m\) is that bound, the length of every local path visited inside a candidate history is at most the initial path length plus \(m\) times the maximum positive length increase of one local rule (zero if none). There are finitely many words, rule sequences and placements in these bounds, including exact gap placements for empty words. Enumerate the directed moves between them and test graph reachability. Native switches preserve count, including switches of two occurrences of the same declaration.

The exact test can be undecidable on one fixed presentation even where reflection holds. Take one crossing edge, one 2-generator \(a\) from it to itself, and 3-endomorphisms of \(a\) for generators and formal inverses of a fixed finite group presentation with computably enumerable complete word problem. As 4-declarations put both directions of its relators and inverse cancellations against the identity. At the local path consisting of the single arrow \(a\), each change uses that entire local arrow. There is no pair of disjoint positions for a native switch. A 4-cell between a written word of these 3-changes and the identity therefore exists exactly when the word is trivial in the group. The positive direction lifts an actual word derivation and the negative direction follows from the path criterion. These elementary queries use no choice among proper generic lifts. [Nies and Sorbi, Theorem 3.2 and the identity class](https://www.cs.auckland.ac.nz/~nies/papersnew/NiesSorbi_wordproblems.pdf)

It enumerates the paths for two changes on disjoint two-letter substrings together with a change of version. It builds a raw witness using the native switch and the placed 4-declaration and checks that restriction recovers the local witness literally. A separate term places a nonliteral point unitor in a stationary column of an outer crossing. The checker constructs both comparison directions at its actual faces. `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/CrossingSchedules.agda` verifies the displayed terms in the certified Gray kernel. The proof of the single-column reading and the match with every admissible \(E\) is the arity argument above. [Crossing-schedule checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_crossing_schedules.py)

## Researcher Response 44

**An independent 3-scalar in a face of a moving planar diagram sees one puncture per directly enclosed floating component.** This gives a degree-wise reduction for the class in Response 42 with arbitrary additional free 3-scalars at vertices. For each individual ordinary surface and each degree $n\ge5$, uniform reflection on that class is equivalent to reflection in degree $n$ on the one-object presentations with only 2-scalars and 3-scalars on the identity of the empty diagram. Already at degree five it suffices to test **one occurrence of one 3-scalar**, together with any number of individually distinguished 2-scalars used once at the planar pole. The local raw obstruction in that test is a word in the free group on those punctures. There is no bounded number of punctures whose subfamilies detect all such words by erasure.

The added 3-generators have both strict 2-poles the empty diagram on the empty path at the specified vertex. The other attachments are the wire rules and private gate substitutions of Response 42, with the same surface-model hypothesis on its children. This calculation concerns those presentations themselves. In particular it permits arbitrary planar 2-computads with only these independent 3-generators above them.

### The scalar space at prescribed ends

Use the notation of Response 42. First distinguish the structural gate positions and the occurrences of the extra 3-scalars. Fix a structural diagram $w$, a **particular planar path class** $\beta$ between the two written 2-ends after erasing all inner data, and a face at the starting diagram for each added scalar, transporting faces along $\beta$. In the category of maps between these 2-ends the summand with the stated data is the scalar-free summand at $\beta$ times the groupoidal factor
$$
              \prod_F \operatorname{Conf}_{S_F}\bigl(\mathbb R\times F\bigr).
$$
Here $S_F$ is the set of scalar names in the face; $F$ has the homotopy geometry of an open disk with $h_F$ punctures, one per directly enclosed floating child. In the formula one can use an actual plane with $h_F$ specified points omitted for $F$. The real coordinate is the execution time of the scalar. In particular a scalar in a bounded face can wind around children of that face. The motions of the boundary of its face are retained in the planar base.

We give the fibre and naturality arguments, since a face with a hole cannot use the contractible unary chart of Response 31. Choose a representative gate of each directly enclosed child. A nonempty floating component has a structural gate: unary transitions alone cannot form a closed spatial component. At a horizontal change index put the ordinary unary *lower markers* for the changing 3-scalars in their specified faces (these markers are point gates with empty input and output). Temporarily distinguish all of them. The gate labels and ordered segment words give the other factors of Response 42. Over fixed discrete choices of these factors, adding $s$ named points to a face adds $s$ siblings to its $h$ children. In the marked lower groupoids forgetting them maps the pure group $P_{h+s}$ onto $P_h$ there, with all other sibling factors as in the marked component decomposition. The map keeping one representative gate per child and the $s$ markers induces the ordinary deletion-of-strands square over the corresponding $h$ named planar points. On the kernel it is the actual inclusion of motions of the $s$ points around the children, and an isomorphism onto the fibre group. Indeed at a grouped reading the siblings execute their cabled pure braids; restriction to one representative of each cable recovers exactly those braids. The map $\operatorname{Conf}_{h+s}(\mathbb R^2)\to\operatorname{Conf}_h(\mathbb R^2)$ is a locally trivial bundle (move the distinct centres in disjoint disks). Its fibre is $\operatorname{Conf}_s(\mathbb R^2\smallsetminus\{h\text{ points}\})$. All the lower components and these fibres are 1-types, the latter by iterated point deletion with punctured-plane fibres. The fibre comparison is consequently an equivalence. Taking the distinct face projections together gives the product of these fibres. The same verification works after subdivision of a structural wire; a chosen gate in each child remains a strand of that child. [Component cables and their projections](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-164) · [Subdivided wire charts](#researcher-response-42)

This comparison uses actual erasures, separately for the scalar-free data and for the selected points at each face. Before categorification it is an equivalence over the marked structural groupoid to the fibre product of the scalar-free data with those punctured-plane families pulled back along the representative-gate maps. It is natural under splitting a time tier: a marker going constant is forgotten on that side, and gate changes and wire expansions keep the same representative in the folded diagram. For a full inner rectangle resolve the gate colours as in Response 42. One can take all subsets of the prescribed scalar palettes at once: faces of a stopped marker keep no occurrence of that name, and no step on a wire or gate can create such an occurrence. The summand of total named grade uses each name once. These are families over a **groupoid independent of the inner index**. Inner categorification can be performed on each fibre with its transport along that groupoid. Indeed each presheaf level is the homotopy colimit over the groupoid of its fibres, the inner localization preserves colimits, and the colimit of categories indexed by a groupoid with equivalence transports is their category of transported objects and arrows. Equivalently check the fibrewise Segal products over the marking cover before descent. On each fibre the independent factors are products, preserved by inner categorification. This argument permits nontrivial action of a pure base braid on punctured-face paths. At the specified planar path $\beta$ its path-space component is contractible by planar asphericity, so one uses its transport once in comparing the two ends.

For clarity, in a single fibre with $h$ stationary punctures write $U=\mathbb R^2\setminus\{p_1,\ldots,p_h\}$. For a temporal partition $\pi$ of the named scalar set, the coefficient is $D_\pi=\prod_{B\in\pi}\operatorname{Conf}_B(U)$, with inclusion into $D_{\pi'}$ when a tier is split. The extreme hom attachment with these space coefficients gives their homotopy colimit over ordered partitions directed by splitting: subdivide each product of permutohedra by its face flags and apply the facet maps. This is $\operatorname{Conf}_{S}(\mathbb R\times U)$. Indeed stratify the real coordinates by temporal weak order and $U^S$ by a triangulation compatible with all its pair diagonals. Such a subdivision is obtained by a joint coordinate-order fan on planar coordinates including the puncture coordinates, subdivided into simplices. Over each open time face the allowed planar coordinates are precisely $D_\pi$. The allowed product faces form the Grothendieck poset of the allowed planar faces under time refinement. Its nerve computes that homotopy colimit (the inclusions under subdivision carry the same planar face to itself). It also computes the configuration complement: barycentrically subdivide the product complex and renormalize the weights on the allowed vertices in each simplex. The largest face of a point in the complement is allowed, so the deformation stays in the complement. One may first compress the coordinates into open bounded intervals and use the induced subdivisions there, omitting boundary faces. This proves the formula, including for $h=0$ or $S$ empty. [Space-indexed permutohedral attachments](#why-chart-components-suffice-for-4-tracks)

### Specializing a proposed surface witness

The comparisons just used can be read at old terms. First tag the structural gate positions via the folded planar occurrence calculation of Response 40 and tag the new 3-scalar positions by the rank-three occurrence model of round-1 Response 33, assigning empty scalar readings to every other declaration. The colouring lift extends across all declarations here: a private child variable has exactly its one inherited structural tag and no added scalar, a wire variable has neither, and a free scalar declaration has exactly its own scalar tag and no structural gate. These specify the copy of a variable uniquely at its refined labelling. For a head split and transport tags along the corresponding strings as in the two occurrence constructions. Shared faces get the same tags, nested splittings compose, and the coloured equation applies on the same restricted support or joined arity. Forgetting the tags reconstitutes the original term. Thus one may lift a proposed surface witness and its raw faces with the tags fixed at the source. In degree five the two raw paths have the same transported scalar names at their common end. Above that degree the same coverings compute higher homotopies at the named ends. At these degrees the boundary already contains raw 4-arrows, so its allocation of named scalars to transported faces can be read on the raw ends.

For a particular occupied face specialize one tagged gate per direct child to a distinct generating 2-scalar, and its selected 3-scalar names to generating 3-scalars at the identity. Send other material to units. This is a substitution into the basic mixed scalar context. For arbitrary weak gate ports, collapse the graph first and fit the two unit-path writings on the two sides of the selected 2-scalar, using the graph-only unit comparisons. All copies of a private gate colour then have the same fitted 2-value, and the higher private labels can be assigned at their required faces in the single-particle sector. Over one such selected particle with no changing events the planar component is contractible, so these assignments extend on all private attachments; the empty sector does likewise. Unary word rewrites have empty particle data and use the empty sector. Use the one-event fit for a retained free 3-scalar. At the meshes these maps erase the other strands. In particular the lower map on motions is deletion to the indicated representative points, not a signed sum of crossings. The collars for the unit-path fittings are stationary up to contractible choices. There is also the scalar-free erasure keeping the entire planar and wire/gate data. Both kinds of substitutions take a surface witness to a witness for that same surface.

For degree $n\ge5$ use the raw product calculation at the named ends. The scalar-free projection has its raw filler by Response 42. If the corresponding basic mixed scalar queries reflect at this degree, the specializations give the required relative homotopies in every punctured-face factor. The comparison there is an equivalence at the specified base path. These relative fillers combine in the product and descend by forgetting the tags. This proves sufficiency. Necessity for a theorem uniform over the class follows by taking the basic mixed contexts themselves.

In degree five the ordered configuration of $s$ points in the 3-manifold $\mathbb R\times U$ has fundamental group $\pi_1(U)^s$: loops and their bounding homotopies in the product can be put in general position away from all pair diagonals of codimension three, relative to their collision-free ends. Thus individual named probes detect the entire path groupoid at specified ends. One can erase all scalar names but the selected one in each basic query and retain just the representative 2-particles, each used once. If all occupied faces have no directly enclosed child, their named factors are simply connected and degree five holds outright at this boundary, even if closed components occur elsewhere in the diagram. If a face has just one scalar, its factor is aspherical for every number of holes, so it gives no obstruction in degrees six and higher. In particular with at most one scalar in each punctured face the only possible extra higher obstruction is the one on pure 3-scalars in the unpunctured faces. This gives reflection in every degree $n\ge6$ at such labels for both published surfaces by round-1 Response 59. The free-group test for one scalar and $h$ holes computes a **raw** obstruction; the general degree-five reflection question for these basic mixed surface judgements remains.

To see why retaining all the holes can matter already in degree five, take $h\ge2$ sibling closed lenses and one marked scalar outside them. The fibre group is freely generated by the $h$ meridians of the scalar around the lenses. An iterated commutator, starting with two different meridians and adjoining each new meridian once by a further commutator, is nonidentity by free reduction, and becomes identity upon erasing any one of the holes. Each meridian is an actual unary-coefficient motion of the scalar: pass it as a point across a closed lens packet and back with a full winding, whiskering by a path to the chosen packet. Deleting the scalar leaves a trivial base motion. The chart-to-globe comparison realizes the commutator as a raw 4-loop with no raw 5-cell to the identity. This gives the asserted limit on subfamily erasures, independently of how the unresolved surface test turns out.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_punctured_face_scalars.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PuncturedFaceScalars.agda` with an existing directory. The solver reconstructs the faces of closed lenses with internal points, executes the exterior commutator by 36, 108 and 264 legal signed switches for two, three and four holes, and compares the actual Artin actions. Choosing the birth or death as representative in each lens gives the same nonidentity on the retained strands for all 4, 8 and 16 choices. Erasing the scalar, or any one lens in the corresponding projection, gives the identity. Agda 2.8.0 checks the exterior scalar term and all four puncture substitutions for the two-lens boundary with their written unit fits. [Punctured-face solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_punctured_face_scalars.py)

## Researcher Response 45

**In degree $n\ge6$, at most $n-4$ occurrences of generating 3-scalars in one face suffice for the mixed surface test of Response 44.** One fixed computad suffices, with one object, one 2-scalar and one 3-scalar at the iterated identity. Its planar pole may require arbitrarily many occurrences of the 2-scalar. The same bound on the number of gap scalars strengthens the single-gap test of Response 23 in every degree. The bound is sharp for detecting raw obstructions by projections onto subsets of the original occurrences, even with a prescribed positive number of holes. There is also a geometric detection theorem. After passage to finite covers, configurations of at most $n-4$ points in ordinary three-space jointly detect all the higher homotopy classes of a punctured-face factor, including torsion.

### Detection by original occurrences

Put $V=(\mathbb R^2\setminus P)\times\mathbb R$ for a finite set of $h$ punctures and $C_m=\operatorname{Conf}_m(V)$ with named points. Fix a based $k$-sphere with $k=n-4\ge2$. Forgetting the last point is a bundle $C_m\to C_{m-1}$ with fibre $V\setminus Q$ at the $m-1$ fixed positions. It has a section placing the last point above all the other time coordinates at one specified planar position. If necessary join its value to the chosen basepoint in the fibre. Hence the homotopy sequence is short exact in degree $k$ and the fibre group injects. If the sphere projects nontrivially, apply the same argument to the projection. Otherwise it comes from a unique fibre class. The one-point base $V$ has contractible universal cover. [Fadell and Neuwirth, configuration fibrations](https://ncatlab.org/nlab/files/FadellNeuwirth-ConfigSpaces.pdf)

The universal cover of the fibre is $\mathbb R^3\setminus\widetilde Q$. Above each fixed particle $i$ the lifts are indexed by $g\in F_h$ after a choice of access paths. Indeed the universal cover of the punctured plane is an open plane and $\widetilde Q$ is locally finite. The complement is simply connected by general position. Its reduced homology is freely generated in degree two by the oriented meridians $L_{i,g}$. This follows by excision in the pair obtained by filling the locally finite set of missing points, using disjoint small balls and ordinary finite chains. Choose based stems to their boundaries. The map of the CW wedge of these 2-spheres into the complement is a homology equivalence of simply connected spaces of CW type, hence a homotopy equivalence. On forgetting any of $Q$ the corresponding meridians become null, and the stems and spheres for all retained particles still represent their meridians. ### The local presentation at two prescribed objects

At a crossing head exactly one column of its tuple crosses. Collect the stationary words of the preceding columns in order into \(u\), and those of the following columns into \(v\).
