### Detecting the cut in an individual exterior word

There is a finite test on any graph with globular 2-skeleton and arbitrary higher attachments. More precisely, start with one of the structural presentations of Response 42, with its stated hypotheses for the surface in question. At each object one may add finitely many designated 2-scalars on its identity path and any number of directed 3-rules between parallel raw composites of these scalars, including the empty diagram. Use separate colours from the structural 2-gates, even when a structural gate itself has both spatial ports empty. The added rules use just the point and its designated scalars. No additional rule can consume a structural gate as one of its scalar inputs. The result allows genuine 1-generators, arbitrary nesting, the wire rewrites, and the private gate changes from the structural presentation. Already with stationary structural gates this covers any planar 2-computad with such a system of 3-rules at its vertices.

### Scalar rules in a punctured face

Temporarily distinguish the structural gates. With the designated scalars suppressed, fix the underlying structural 2-diagram $w$. Let $F$ run over its faces, let $h_F$ count the directly enclosed structural components in $F$, and use an open punctured plane $U_F$ with $h_F$ specified holes. In the fibre over a structural diagram, the extra data are the product over $F$ of the directed scalar configuration categories formed **in $U_F$**. At a cut the particles are the currently live 2-scalar occurrences, with the labels of the object of $F$. In an active batch the particles are the stationary live wires and one site for each changing rule. A site is present even when its input or output consists of zero particles. An endpoint expands the site into its actual input or output diagram. The base transports these categories with its structural braid. At fixed written 2-ends and a fixed structural planar path class the comparison uses its transport once. It retains, in each face, the directed mapping space between the transported prescribed particle configurations.

To verify this description use the change-index charts before inner categorification. At a discrete index, both an active site and a stationary designated 2-scalar are planar point gates. After recording the structural gates, the ordered segment words and the set of points in each face, the fibre of their lower marked diagram groupoid is the product of the ordered configuration spaces of these points in the $U_F$. Indeed the sibling pure braid map in a face forgets the extra points and retains the $h_F$ structural siblings. Selecting one representative gate in each such sibling identifies its kernel with the point motions in the punctured plane. This checks the equivalence on each aspherical lower component and the face allocations give exactly its components. It applies to sites and ordinary particles together. On splitting a temporal tier a site goes to its prescribed port diagram on the side where it becomes constant. In the marked planar model this is insertion of that block of points at the site, including a zero-size block. Under the representative-gate map it is exactly the same insertion in the plane with the $h_F$ fixed strands. Exchanges involving the site become the rectangles of its port block, while the other faces keep their coefficients. These actual planar substitutions commute with iterated splitting by substitution of diagrams. The chosen written port diagrams specify the internal order of each block. Changing to a serial representative uses transport at the attaching map.

This is a comparison of the full change-index diagrams, before one selects any schedule. In particular it includes the intermediate configurations in the homotopy pullbacks for sequentially dependent batches. Over a fixed structural diagram the presheaf is the product of the scalar-free fibre and the scalar presheaves just described in each face. Private gate labels can be resolved at discrete simplicial levels as in Response 42. The structural groupoid and its transports are independent of the inner index, so inner localization computes the associated family of the fibre localizations, preserving their finite products. In the scalar fibres this is the usual directed scalar-rule category with cut and site coefficients in the punctured plane. Equivalently, use the scalar category in the ordinary plane with $h_F$ extra, individually named stationary particles. Forgetting the other particles projects to the groupoid of these $h_F$ positions. Over their fixed configuration the lower cut and batch coefficients are exactly the punctured-plane spaces above. Moving these stationary positions in disjoint disks gives local trivializations compatible with the small port insertions, or use the fibrewise localization over their groupoid. At two fixed ends the component of a specified path of these positions is contractible by planar asphericity. Thus its homotopy fibre in the fixed-end mapping space is equivalent to the whole summand over that path class. ### Two substitutions suffice

One raw substitution erases the designated scalar data, giving the original structural presentation. Another sends every vertex to one object, every edge to a unit, every structural gate family to a stationary 2-scalar of its own colour, and every unary wire step to the graph-only fit. Keep each designated scalar and each of its rules in a separate scalar rule presentation at that same object, taking the actual images of its written attaching faces. For the image of a structural gate put its stationary scalar between the graph-only fits for its collapsed ports. The colours of that structural family receive the same particle. On a wire rewrite both images are in the empty-particle sector. On private child attachments all faces lie in the pure one-particle sector of the corresponding structural colour. These sectors admit fillers at the required boundaries by the empty and singleton planar calculations (use the pure sector without any scalar rules). This constructs the substitution on the whole structural presentation. On lower charts it reads one strand per structural gate and keeps the sites and live particles of every scalar rule. The fits insert no extra particle. One advantage of retaining *all* these strands initially is that the choice of an inner face need never be imposed on an unmarked surface derivation. Both substitutions preserve a proposed witness of the same individual surface.

Consider a degree $n\ge5$ old query. If scalar-rule contexts reflect for this surface in this degree, the flattened scalar query has a raw filler, and so does the scalar-free query by Response 42. We detail the step from that scalar filler to each face. Name the stationary particles on the common 2-source and distinguish the rule occurrences and their live wires beginning with the common 3-source. On a raw scalar history the ordered input wires and the name of each use determine its fresh output names, with an output index even for equal colours. A birth receives its own use name and a death retains the identities of its input wires. These are the occurrence and cut-sheet lifts for arbitrary scalar ports. They apply to a raw homotopy with its boundary, with the names prescribed at its source. If the two 4-paths had different induced occurrence transports at their common target, they could not have had a raw homotopy in the flattened query. Higher relative spheres lift at the given names as well. All this naming on the flattened side uses a **raw** filler. [Wire and event marks with empty ports](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#forgetting-a-port-while-retaining-its-event)

For one original face retain the names of its mobile wires and uses, and one stationary representative gate per directly enclosed structural child. In the original history each use and all its incident mobile wires lie in the same transported face. Thus on the flattened named network this restriction keeps the *whole* port diagram of a retained use. It also keeps a designated use with no incident wire. Restrict at every cut and batch, projecting the other directions to constant directions, as in the mark restriction of round-1 Response 108. On the flattened boundary this gives exactly the representative-gate reading of the chosen face, with its path class for the stationary punctures. The raw relative filler restricts as well. By the fixed-path fibre calculation it supplies a relative filler in the required face factor. The scalar-free filler and these face fillers now combine at the prescribed structural path: its planar path component is contractible, and the remaining gate and wire factors are those in the scalar-free projection. Forgetting the auxiliary markings gives the required raw filler at the original written ends. Degrees through four are already known for individual ordinary surfaces. This proves the reduction to ordinary scalar rule computads, with no positivity hypothesis.

Apply round-1 Response 108 for arbitrary ports, Response 82 for two nonempty sides, and Response 38 for a common singleton side. For the latter two cases there is a further conclusion when the structural 2-diagram is stationary and there are no structural 3- or higher generators. Every fixed-end scalar-rule space in the flat test is then homotopy discrete by those results, as is its summand over a stationary-particle path class. The bare structural fixed-end space is also homotopy discrete. Consequently every old type of dimension at least five on this 3-skeleton is raw-inhabited and every raw 4-cell has a reverse. The higher-attachment theorem gives reflection for all ten surfaces through $h+1$ if arbitrary further declarations first occur in degree $h\ge4$. A mixture of signs of change in the number of particles is allowed in the nonempty-port case. [Nonempty ports](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-82) · [A common singleton side](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-38) · [Higher attachments](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-31)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_nested_face_ports.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/NestedFacePorts.agda` with an existing directory. Two lenses bound holes in the exterior face, and one of the lenses encloses two more. Place one changing site in each of these two punctured faces. The solver executes their meridian commutators on the actual planar gates, expands each site to zero, one or two ports and executes the cabled switches on the resulting diagrams. All nine pairs commute, including when the exterior motion carries the whole inner packet. The 72 comparisons using either boundary gate of each hole recover the cabled local motion and the identity for the other face. Agda checks a birth of two scalars inside a lens alongside a death of two outside, and both flattened substitutions at the original weak unit ports. ### Finite bounds when the edge word can grow

In the four effective settings, individual old 4-types on these cut presentations are decidable if all declared 4-changes weakly increase the number of local 2-events, or if they all weakly decrease it. This includes every finite cut 3-computad. Although the indexed graph can be infinite and a 2-rule can increase edge length, there are **two** finite bounds in addition to the history count. Let \(m\) be the target history count in the increasing case, the source count in the decreasing case. Let \(b\) be the number of indexed global 2-events in the common initial local path of the histories. Write \(d_3\) for the largest positive increase in this number across a global 3-rule, taking zero when none increases it. Along any candidate history of at most \(m\) local 2-events, every intermediate local path has at most \(M=b+m d_3\) indexed global 2-events. All these paths start at the same global edge word. If its length is \(a\) and \(d_2\) is the largest positive edge-length increase across a 2-generator (or zero), every word visited inside any of those paths has length at most \(a+M d_2\). Extension of the exterior indices changes neither increment. The left and right indices of each applied generator are subwords of the corresponding edge word, including for a change on an empty local substring. They too are bounded. Thus enumerate the finite histories and directed moves in these bounds and test reachability; an input endpoint outside the bounds cannot occur on a successful path. Generic history readings are effective as in Response 43. The argument does not require a positive weight conserved by the 2-rules.

A 3-change inserts this two-event excursion in an empty temporal gap. The checker enumerates the resulting positioned 2-paths through five insertions using their actual edge words and independently reconstructs the projected words from nonnegative prefix heights ending at zero. The numbers of distinct positioned paths are 1, 1, 2, 5, 14 and 42. It constructs both orientations of an inner comparison on structural 3-changes, substituting the two successive expansions in one slot and the two successive contractions in the other. The middle 1-pole has five edge occurrences. It also checks the 3-change whiskered at the expanded word. ## Researcher Response 50

**All ten ordinary surfaces reflect in every dimension for the following mixture of planar gate expansions and contractions.** Each generating 3-rule has a bare generating 2-gate on at least one side. Orient the rules with a bare source as expansions and those with a bare target as contractions, choosing either designation when both sides are bare. Require that no bare output colour of a contraction is a bare input colour of an expansion. The other side can be any parallel diagram, including the identity, and all spatial paths and gates may have empty ports. Every old type in degree at least five in this 3-computad is raw-inhabited. With arbitrary additional declarations first in degree $h\ge4$, the same surfaces reflect through $h+1$.

There is a stronger test at an individual history, even when the global colour condition fails. Record the individual 2-gate occurrences that are consumed and produced by its rule uses. It suffices that **no contraction output occurrence is consumed by an expansion**. Braid transport is included when identifying these two occurrences. Two gates of the same colour in an intermediate diagram can therefore have different roles. On each such history component, even with repeated or nullary rules, one has the following description at fixed initial and final diagrams $x,y$:
$$
       \coprod_{f,g} \operatorname{Path}_B(x[f],y[g]).
$$
Here $f$ is an expansion forest at the actual positions of $x$, $g$ is a forest of reversed contractions at the actual positions of $y$, and $B$ is the space of ordinary planar diagrams at the exterior paths. In the global statement take every forest pair. For an individual supported network take its summands and the components of paths matching its named leaves. Equality of the two rooted forests and of the signed middle braid decides a raw degree-four comparison on serial histories. This extends Response 47 even when a contraction consumes a lens in one region and an expansion creates a lens in another. [Forests at fixed planar ends](#researcher-response-47)

### Interchanging the two kinds of change

Work in the raw change-index presentation, temporarily naming all uses. A varying-end coefficient for a temporal batch is the groupoid of planar diagrams with one marker gate for every active rule. Its maps to the two ends of the batch expand the markers by their actual 2-faces. A sequence of batches uses the homotopy pullback of these coefficients at each intermediate diagram space. These pullbacks include paths at the intermediate diagrams. The homotopy colimit over ordered partitions directed by splitting batches gives the arrow space at named total grade, by the face-flag subdivision of the space-indexed permutohedral attachments. A coefficient may be empty. This permits using all ordered partitions of the names without an assumption on which orders are executable. At fixed ends use the same coefficients with the two exterior endpoint paths. [Change-index coefficients and their facets](#why-chart-components-suffice-for-4-tracks)

Split a mixed batch into a contraction batch $C$ followed by an expansion batch $E$. The map from the joined coefficient to the split coefficient is an equivalence when their bare positions in the middle are disjoint. In the middle the contraction side marks the gates just produced, and the expansion side marks the gates about to fire. Each diagram with either set of such marks maps by a covering to the middle diagram groupoid: on the tier charts it merely labels positions. Transport the marks along the middle path. The homotopy pullback of the covers is the simultaneous marking cover. At disjoint positions it is exactly the joined coefficient, by changing each marked gate back to the marker of its own rule. This also checks the two exterior expansions, since inserting the prescribed blocks at distinct positions commutes. The two bare gates can lie in the same floating component, or one can be inside a lens containing the other. Disjointness here is of **gate positions**; no common horizontal layer or disjointness of the adjacent wires is required. Restricting the rest of a schedule or fitting fixed endpoints is a homotopy pullback and preserves the equivalence. For arbitrary written attachments choose the planar fits on each bare side and the indicated serial writings on the other side, as in Response 47.

Disjointness holds at every such cut under the global colour condition. For the individual version, flatten the planar data by sending each gate to a scalar particle, collapsing the outer graph and retaining each 3-rule as a scalar rule at its two fitted port diagrams. At a changing site this remembers precisely the input and output gate positions, including the site of a zero-port change. The scalar wire and event labelling of round-1 Response 108 assigns fresh names at each use to its outputs and follows the actual braid transports. The incidence network is locally constant in the resulting named raw mapping space. Thus one can restrict the preceding pullbacks to a given incidence network (and to all its restrictions on the parts of a schedule). A contraction output that was consumed by a later expansion would be one wire of this network. Its absence ensures disjointness in every contributing split coefficient. A joined coefficient always has disjoint bare positions under this split. Naming at the common 3-source suffices to test a higher relative globe. In the sorted representation all expansion nodes are addressed from the given source and all contraction nodes from the given target. Hence forgetting temporary names introduces no isotropy fixing these addressed forests and the two ends: transport of a gate at an end is fixed at that literal end, and each subsequent address specifies the output index in the chosen rule diagram. The remaining permutations of middle leaves are already paths in $B$. [Wire and event lifts with zero ports](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-108)

### Sorting without losing a path class

Project the schedule poset to the pair of its restricted schedules on the expansion and contraction names. For a fixed such pair with $p,q$ batches, respectively, the fibre consists of lattice paths across the $p$ by $q$ rectangle, with a diagonal step for joining one batch of each kind. Refining a diagonal gives either order. This fibre computes the pointwise homotopy left Kan extension at the pair. Indeed if a schedule has projections coarser than the pair, the category of its refinements over that exact pair is a product of shuffle posets, one inside each of its old batches. Each is contractible: it stratifies time coordinates for two strictly ordered lists, allowing equalities across lists; the open region is convex and its weak-order face complex computes its homotopy type. Equivalently use the elementary collapses below with constant coefficients. These are the comma categories for finality of the fibre inclusion. [Schedule deletion and the refinement-fibre argument](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#sorting-a-schedule-without-changing-its-mapping-space)

In this fibre the strict shuffles are vertices of a cube complex. A cell makes independent adjacent pairs simultaneous, one diagonal per pair. Each cell has a unique vertex of greatest inversion number, replacing all diagonals by $CE$, where an inversion is a contraction before an expansion. Process vertices in descending inversion number, keeping the sorted vertex $E^pC^q$. At a remaining unsorted vertex $w$ the adjacent $CE$ pairs are disjoint. The cells having $w$ as their greatest vertex are the faces based at $w$ of the single cube on these pairs. Choose one such pair $i$. Pair each based face omitting $i$ with its cofacet including $i$, in descending dimension of the omitted face. It is a free face at its turn: cofaces with more inversions have already gone, and a coface at this same vertex adding a different pair has been processed in the higher dimension. The coefficient map from the cofacet to that face is precisely the split into $CE$ above, an equivalence. Such a free-face collapse preserves the homotopy colimit. In its face-flag model the cofacet contributes a ball with its coefficient, and the free facet has equivalent coefficient; together they retract up to homotopy onto the union of the other facets, with the original structure maps there. For dimension one this is the mapping cylinder of the map to the retained vertex. Iterating proves that the inclusion of the sorted vertex computes the fibre colimit for arbitrary coefficient maps in the other split direction. Concatenating the two restricted schedules is a section natural in both schedules. The section consequently computes the original homotopy colimit as they vary.

On this section there is one intermediate path between the output of all expansions and the input of all contractions. The two independent schedule colimits may be taken before their homotopy pullback over $B$: base change in spaces preserves colimits in slices, and the two indices vary separately. Response 47 on each side now gives exactly the displayed space. In particular one must compare the actual path between the two expanded ends, even if it is a full twist with identity permutation. Planar asphericity makes every component homotopy discrete. The specified-face globe test, the general through-degree-four theorem, and the higher-attachment criterion apply just as in Response 47. The higher-attachment claim uses the whole-skeleton hypothesis. [Planar fixed-boundary and attachment consequences](#researcher-response-47)

### Effective orientation and comparison

For the global test classify a rule whose source alone is bare as forced expansion, and a rule whose target alone is bare as forced contraction. Connect two rules when the first has a bare target equal in colour to the bare source of the second. Propagate the contraction designation forward from the forced contractions along these edges. Encountering a forced expansion forbids any designation satisfying the condition. Otherwise designate the reached rules as contractions and the rest as expansions. Forward closure is exactly the required implication, so this decides existence of a designation. The identical test on individual named uses uses actual connecting gate occurrences as its edges. Failure of the orientation test is a failure of this sufficient criterion.

On a serial history maintain the two forests and a braid between their ordered leaves. A contraction groups the target roots of its input block under the new target root. A planar switch exchanges two target roots and appends the block braid on their packets. For an expansion the target root must still be untouched by a contraction. Its single leaf traces through the middle braid to an actual source leaf. Fire the rule on that leaf, cable its strand in the whole preceding braid by the new arity, and replace the target root by the new trivial roots. With no output use a zero-strand packet. These operations give the path in the displayed formula by the two endpoint substitution maps, and its equality at the prescribed ends is decided by the faithful planar Artin action. The solver also replays every resulting crossing on the expanded positioned gates.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_peak_forests.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PeakForests.agda` in an existing directory. The solver compares 126 mixed and sorted histories with zero-size, one-gate and closed-lens blocks, both crossing signs, and up to six crossings at the middle. It distinguishes a surviving twist, distinguishes contractions with the same empty block but different names, and identifies a shared middle occurrence. Its orientation test agrees with exhaustive search on 512 three-rule presentations. It verifies the unique cofacet at each of 408 elementary collapses of the 25 shuffle grids through four batches on each side. Agda checks a raw 4-comparison from a lens contraction followed by an expansion to the opposite order at their exact common faces. JSON input compares another serial pair with designated rule orientations. [Two-forest solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_peak_forests.py)

## Researcher Response 51

**Q3(a) has a finite test at the ordinary strict label of a positive 3-computad, even when no labelling of edge words gives a common cut.** Let \(s,t\) be the old 3-faces. Different ordinary strict values already exclude a 4-cell. At a common value \(c\), take its finite ordinary occurrence diagram (polyplex). Colour the vertices left and right, require each edge to be nondecreasing, and require both object poles of every generating occurrence of degree at least two to be left and right, in that order. If this system has a solution, raw and surface inhabitation agree at the written boundary for both coherence rules, every admissible \(E\) and every \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\). Any **one** successful colouring suffices. In the four effective settings it gives a decision of individual inhabitation. The hypothesis here is source-positivity: the ordinary source of each generator in degree at least two is nonidentity in the preceding dimension. No length bound or variable-face assumption is made.

There is an extension to positive 4-computads. At each generating 4-cell test whether **every** colouring of its ordinary source occurrence diagram extends over its plex (the occurrence diagram including the generating cell). If these tests pass, the same reflection conclusion follows from a colouring of the ordinary value of the specified **source 3-term alone**. If the analogous tests pass on the 3-generators too, a colouring of its initial 2-diagram alone suffices. These are directed extension tests. They are decidable in polynomial time in the explicitly given finite incidence graphs, including for a boundary map that identifies vertices. They check whether the cut method applies; a negative test makes no assertion of nonconservativity.

### Lifting at an occurrence of a strict cell

Here is the required projection result for the directed kernels. Let \(f:Y\to X\) preserve generators and written attaching terms between source-positive computads, and write \(F\) for ordinary strict evaluation. In all dimensions, for raw syntax or an interchange-free binary surface \(L\) of either coherence rule,
\[
                         L(Y)\simeq L(X)\times_{F(X)}F(Y)
\]
as globular sets (literal terms for raw syntax, conversion classes for the surface). In fact the argument needs only unique lifting of specified strict binary factorizations and identities and that preimages of generating cells in their own degree are generators. To see this for \(E\), use Response 5 on globular inputs: specifying the operation and its ordinary positional labelling specifies its value, also for a head whose generic type uses conversion. Thus an operation can be applied to a family indexed by the strict lifts of its result. Split a specified lift over the positional tree of the operation by unique factorization, and apply the operation to the entries for the split labels. Faces restrict this split and substitution splits it in stages. Both identities follow from uniqueness, including under reassociation or ordinary strict exchange of the decomposition. Boundary families must be retained at indices with an empty fibre. This is the right-adjoint construction of round-1 Response 40, so pullback along \(Ff\) preserves the cellular pushouts in the slice over \(F(X)\). A formal generating disc relative to its boundary pulls back to one top disc per strict lift of its top cell: identity padding lifts uniquely, and on the proper faces the given attaching values can be composites. Induction on the presentation attaches exactly the generators of \(Y\), with their actual weak boundaries determined by the preceding-stage pullback. This gives the formula. The only input concerning the particular operations and equations in that construction is the globular positional result, which Response 5 proves for admissible \(E\). It does not require a cartesian lifting theorem for \(F_E\) itself. [Directed positional calculation](#researcher-response-5) · [Slice adjunction and relative disc calculation](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-40)

For source-positive presentations the two strict lifting hypotheses follow from Henry's representation: a strict cell is a labelling of its polyplex, a specified composite is represented by gluing the two factor polyplexes at their matched face, and a generator is represented by a plex. Restrictions give the unique split. Thus the same formula also reduces **any positive counterexample to Q1(a), in any degree**, to the universal cell of a single finite polyplex. Give its generators their weak attaching terms by lifting the old terms at the indicated strict faces, inductively in degree; the raw pullback ensures literal parallelism. Lift the proposed surface term and the old faces at the universal value, then fold any putative raw solution back. [Henry, §§2.2–2.4 and Theorem 2.4.8](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1711.00744) · [Weak attachments on the ordinary occurrence diagram](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-40)

More explicitly, construct a finite computad \(Y\) over the input through degree four. Duplicate each object in the two colours. Give each edge one copy at each nondecreasing colour pair. At every higher generator adjoin one copy for each pair of parallel lifted strict faces with the specified strict labels and with crossing object poles. Use the raw pullback on the lower skeleton to lift its actual attaching terms. There are finitely many choices: a lift of any fixed strict face is specified by a colouring of its finite polyplex. To verify this last assertion without circularity, start with vertices and edge occurrences; once their colours are given the edge copies are unique. On each next occurrence the lower copies give its two faces by composition, and the higher copy exists uniquely if its exterior poles cross. Conversely any strict lift labels its occurrence diagram by these very copies. This construction works with identifications in a face map; all maps out of the glued diagram must agree there.

Every higher generator of \(Y\) crosses one directed cut. Response 49 therefore applies even where its attaching paths have different widths. On a 3-computad the only candidate ordinary strict 4-value is \(1_c\). For a single chosen lift \(c'\), the pullback lifts *any* surface inhabitant at \(1_{c'}\), together with the two raw faces at \(c'\); uniqueness gives identical lifted lower boundaries. A raw filler in \(Y\) folds to the required one in \(X\). Conversely any raw filler downstairs lifts at this same identity. This proves the first test and its decision assertion via the finite cut 3-computad. In particular a strict 3-cell with an occurrence colouring can be used even if some other cell built from the same original declarations has no colouring. [Arbitrary attachments at a cut and individual decision](#researcher-response-49)

For the 4-computad assertion, the per-generator extension condition implies that any strict 4-cell lifts forward from any chosen lift of its source. Induct on a strict expression. At a generator this is exactly extension of the labelling of its source along its face map. At an identity use the identity on the given lift. For top composition lift the first operand and then lift the second from the resulting face. For a lower-cut composition split the given source over the displayed composite of sources by unique factorization, lift the two operands, and compose; their common lower face was fixed by that split. Whiskers are included by padding with identities. This produces a lift of the given strict cell, whose target determines a strict lift of the old target face. Apply the raw and surface pullbacks there as above. The same induction in dimension three supplies the assertion from an initial 2-diagram. One may equally use a target-extension test in either rank and start at that end, reversing the order of lifting a top composite. This concerns strict factorizations and does not reverse any directed generator. For the four effective settings with a common sign of the 4-rule count changes as in Response 49, this also decides individual inhabitation: fix a source lift, enumerate the finitely many compatible lifts of the old target, and use the cut test on each. Forgetting colours preserves each count increment.

### A graph test for extension, including the internal face data

For a polyplex \(P\), add vertices \(0,1\) to its object graph and edges \(0\to v\to1\) at each vertex, with \(0\to1\). For a generating occurrence of degree at least two with object source \(a\) and target \(b\), add \(a\to0\) and \(1\to b\). Edge reachability represents implications for a monotone Boolean colouring with constants \(0,1\). A colouring exists exactly when \(1\) does not reach \(0\). For a face map \(j:D\to P\), preserving the added constants, **every** colouring of \(D\) extends if \(D\) has none, or if
\[
        j(u)\leadsto_P j(v)\quad\Longrightarrow\quad u\leadsto_D v
          \qquad (u,v\in D_0\cup\{0,1\}).
\]
Indeed for a given colouring mark in \(P\) the upward closure of its marked images and the true constant. The displayed condition prevents this from marking any false boundary vertex or the false constant. It is therefore an extension. Conversely if the condition fails and \(D\) is consistent, mark in \(D\) just the upward closure of \(u\) and \(1\). This leaves \(v\) and \(0\) false, so no extension is possible. A vertex identification adds both implications; no inclusion assumption on \(j\) is needed. Two transitive closures thus test an entire generator, rather than enumerating its boundary colourings.

When the 2-generators have nonempty input **and** output words, the initial planar test has a direct description. For a nonempty planar 2-diagram, a colouring is a trace from an input wire to an output wire that visits every gate in temporal order. On a transverse row the nondecreasing colours switch exactly once; track that wire across each gate. Conversely the trace assigns colours to the gaps on its two sides on each row. At a gate all unaffected gap colours agree, including its two exterior gaps, so the assignments give a colouring of the incidence diagram. It follows that some colouring exists exactly when the gate occurrences are a chain under directed wire reachability. A trace gives that chain. For the converse, join consecutive gates by directed paths, and extend from the first and last to the external rows using nonempty ports. In a planar diagram with no gates there is no higher crossing constraint. For a nonempty 3-generator source diagram \(D\), let \(R_D\) be the pairs of exterior port positions of its complete traces. The 3-generator extension test is precisely \(R_D\subseteq R_{D'}\) for its target diagram \(D'\) with nonempty gates and ports. The two glued planar faces share their ordered input and output words; aligning these ports aligns their common boundary labellings, even if the strict face maps identify positions. In particular if these inclusions hold on a positive 3-computad, **every old fourth-degree query starting at a chain of gates reflects**, with a decision in the four effective settings. Neither unary 2-generators nor unary 3-rules are required.

In rank four one must retain the entire 2-faces. On one loop \(f\) take a unary gate \(a:f\to f\) and a gate \(H:ff\to ff\). Write \(L=a\mathbin{\triangleright} f\) for the change on the left wire. At the two-gate 2-diagram \(H;H\) take a generating 3-endomorphism \(d\). Also take 3-generators \(H\to L\) and \(L\to H\), and use their composite on the first gate with the second gate fixed. A 4-generator from \(d\) to this detour does not extend all source colourings. A lift of \(d\) chooses a crossing input and output port and independent middle ports in its two 2-faces, giving 16 choices. The detour requires the input and both middle ports to be left and permits either output: just two choices. Even at left input and left output, choosing right in the middle of both 2-faces rules it out. This demonstrates failure of the extension test, not a failure of reflection.

For an example outside all edge-word cuts, on the same graph take instead two generating changes \(u,u':L\to H\) and a 4-declaration \(u\to u'\). Its source colourings extend by using the identical lifted faces for \(u'\). The left occurrence of \(a\) gives a lift of \(L\), hence of \(u\). In every category-valued interval at the exterior word \(ff\), the two placements of \(a\) at the separate occurrences of \(f\) are both available. Making each cross the same nondecreasing cut would require the middle object copy to be both colours. The occurrence test distinguishes the placement actually used; it proves reflection at this boundary, including at parallel writings with these same ordinary labels.

**Validation.** The checker compares replay of a marked wire with vertex colourings on 1,757 planar charts and compares the relative reachability test with independent port-pair inclusion on 674 parallel chart pairs. It checks all 16 source lifts in the 4-generator example. It constructs the unit-padded placed repair on \(ff\), checks it at its written ends before and after colouring, and verifies that folding returns the original term literally. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_occurrence_cuts.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/OccurrenceCuts.agda`. These raw term checks use the certified Gray kernel. ## Researcher Response 52

**For an arbitrary ordinary 3-computad, a history whose 3-rule uses are totally ordered by dependence through generating 2-gates has no raw obstruction in degree six or higher.** The 2- and 3-declarations may have arbitrary compound attaching diagrams, empty ports, repeated colours and nontrivial braids at their interfaces. In degree five every such boundary reflects for the two published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange. For *all ten* surfaces every degree-five boundary there already has a raw filler if some initial 2-gate is consumed, or some produced gate survives to the end. In the remaining case degree five reduces, for each individual surface, to the one-probe mixed scalar test of Response 44. Thus an unfilled degree-six boundary in any finite ordinary 3-computad requires two gate-independent uses in its computation, even for the raw theory.

To test the hypothesis at the common 3-history of the old boundary, mark each use and give every produced gate a fresh address consisting of the use and the position in its written output diagram. Braid transport moves the addresses. Draw an edge between uses when an output gate of the first is an input gate of the second. Include the initial and final gates for the consumption and survival tests. The hypothesis says that reachability on the uses is a total order. Equivalently, in their execution order each consecutive pair shares an actual gate. For a single use the order condition is automatic. For zero uses use the planar base directly. The criterion concerns gate dependence: a shared stretch of generating 1-wires alone supplies no such edge.

### A group of compatible planar loops

Flatten to an ordinary scalar rule context. Send the underlying graph to the free point and each generating 2-gate to a scalar of its own colour with the fits on its collapsed paths. Introduce one 3-rule at the actual images of each original pair of 2-faces. A lower planar change-index diagram now records a point for each ordinary gate and a site for each active marker. Source or target expansion gives exactly the list of points in that side of the rule. The scalar wire and event lifts identify the incidence graph of the marked history throughout its mapping-space component. Alternatively label occurrences at the common source on each raw chart and propagate along its signed switches and port expansions. Since all uses are linearly ordered, a loop cannot permute them. Initial gate positions are fixed at the initial diagram; the positions output by a use are fixed by its own indices. Thus on taking the fixed-end component these marks do not introduce additional unobserved deck transformations. The same flattening is a literal substitution on a surface witness. [Occurrence lifts, including births and deaths](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#naming-events-before-wires) · [Planar change-index charts](#why-chart-components-suffice-for-4-tracks)

Only the indicated serial schedule occurs in the face-flag calculation on this component. Two simultaneous sites cannot pass a produced gate from one to the other inside their batch; splitting that batch in either order gives the same gate incidences. A total dependence order rules out every nonsingleton batch. In the remaining schedule let $Z_i$ be the component of the planar diagram groupoid with one marked site for use $i$, with its input and output expansion maps $l_i,r_i$ to the adjacent ordinary diagram spaces. All of these spaces are 1-types by planar asphericity, also for floating and nested components. The coefficient at fixed 2-ends consists of a point in every $Z_i$, a path between the two expanded diagrams at each seam, and paths to the two specified outer diagrams. It is an iterated homotopy pullback of 1-types, hence a 1-type. Compatible paths of all the data are exactly the raw tracks. In particular all relative globes testing degree six and above fill. [Asphericity at actual planar diagrams](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#the-planar-diagram-spaces-are-aspherical)

At a particular such history its fundamental group consists of the tuples $b_i\in\pi_1 Z_i$ with $l_1 b_1=r_m b_m=1$ and with $r_i b_i=l_{i+1}b_{i+1}$ after conjugation by the given seam path. The endpoint paths likewise only change the basepoints. Indeed the path-space terms in this homotopy pullback have homotopy discrete fixed-end fibres, so a loop is determined by its projections to the $Z_i$. The stated equations are exactly the condition for the connecting path homotopies to exist. Record a planar loop by its signed braid on the ordinary gates and the one active marker. On expanding that marker to $d$ gates this braid is cabled on that strand by $d$. Whenever $d>0$, forgetting all but one of the $d$ descendants recovers the original braid. The marker has its own name, so the same deletion works after transport; planar braid faithfulness gives injectivity on the site group. Every $l_i$ for $i>1$ is consequently injective by the gate shared with the preceding use. The tuple group injects into $\pi_1 Z_1$. Dually it injects into the last site group. In particular a nonempty first input or last output suffices to make the whole group trivial. These multiplicities count 2-gates, independently of spatial path length. For a given based tuple all seam equations and nullity are decidable by the signed braid actions. [Faithful braid test for positioned loops](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-79)

### A gate from the initial diagram entering later also kills the loop

Suppose the first use has empty 2-input. Write $S$ for all initial gate names. Its incoming expansion forgets the first marker, so the braid on $S$ is the identity. Before any initial gate has been consumed, at *each* site project its loop to the active marker and all of $S$, omitting the other offspring of earlier events. These readings are identified up to basepoint transport along the chain. At an intervening seam retain one gate supplied by the earlier event and consumed by the following one, as well as $S$. On either side the site expands to exactly one retained strand while all of $S$ remain. Restriction of the given seam braid gives the required conjugation. In particular each such reading detects $b_1$, whose full strand set is the first marker and $S$.

Let $j$ be the first event consuming any of $S$ and take a continuing strand arriving there from event $j-1$. At the input of $j$ project to that strand and all of $S$. This braid is still the preceding detecting reading. After expanding the site at $j$, the continuing strand and at least one member of $S$ are **parallel copies of the very same site**. On the retained set of strands, deleting the continuing strand and reinserting it as a parallel copy of that member of $S$ recovers the braid, up to the fixed endpoint transport. More explicitly, on the site braid assign each spectator its retained multiplicity, and to the site the ordered sublist of its consumed initial gates plus the continuing strand. Inserting the extra strand beside one of these retained gates gives the same block braid, up to an internal permutation if a different order is used. Other offspring strands have already been omitted. The braid after deleting the continuing strand is the braid on $S$ transported from the initial endpoint, hence is trivial. Recabling it gives the identity. The reading detects $b_1$, so every $b_i$ is trivial. If $j=1$ use incoming injectivity directly. Reading backwards proves the assertion for any produced gate surviving at the final end. This argument uses one path through successive uses and permits side branches, merges and unused intermediate outputs. It tests the whole braid, including commutators with zero linking numbers.

### What remains when all gates at the exterior ends are spectators

For degree five one can always detect the tuple on the flattened *named* scalar-rule network: read its loop at the first site. There it is the same signed braid on the first marker and every other gate, faithful on $Z_1$. Its scalar endpoint spaces are also 1-types, and total dependence still requires the same single schedule after flattening. A raw relative filling of the flat boundary therefore forces the original difference tuple to be zero. Round-1 Response 108 supplies this flat filling from any published insertion witness, hence also for its indicated subsurfaces. This already proves the unrestricted published assertion above. [All-port scalar reflection](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-108)

For the individual binary test there is a further specialization. It suffices to handle the case where none of $S$ is consumed and every produced gate is consumed before the end. Lift the flattened surface witness to its named scalar wire and event network by the two occurrence and state lifts and support restriction. This is where giving separate names to *initial and produced* particles of the very same generating colour matters. Keep all stationary particles as individually named 2-scalars, send all intermediate gate names to units, send the first use to one free 3-scalar at the iterated identity, and all later uses to unit comparisons. These assignments exist at the literal ports: every retained use now has two empty particle diagrams as its faces. Transport the first scalar along the point-only fits on these faces, using the empty-sector comparisons for the other uses. In the resulting one-use schedule the varying-end coefficient is the planar configuration of $S$ and the one site. On the serial coefficient map it remembers the first site and the stationary gates; the subsequent intervals, with no remaining use, contribute just the concatenated endpoint transport. In particular a loop of histories projects on the varying-end one-use coefficient to its actual $b_1$ braid. A raw filler in this reduced fixed-end query forces that loop to be zero, and first-site injectivity proves reflection in the original query. Necessity of the one-probe test for a theorem uniform over these histories follows by taking one use of the free 3-scalar itself. Thus degree five on the entire class is equivalent to degree five on this test for each of the four remaining surfaces. No finite bound on the number of stationary particles suffices by subset erasure. [Single-probe test and its free-group obstructions](#researcher-response-44)

For example let the first rule create a whole closed lens, a second consume that lens and emit two points, and a third consume both points. The outer faces of the composite are the empty 2-diagram. Beside two stationary holes a first-site meridian commutator survives the two actual seam matchings, even with nonconstant seam paths. More generally use any number $h$ of stationary scalar holes, with each intervening seam the constant one and the gates of the active packet placed in a single disk. Moving the whole packet around a word in these holes defines compatible site loops. At the two empty ports it is the identity on the remaining strands. Its first-site projection is that free word, so this example realizes the entire $F_h$ in the degree-five test, for any number of repetitions of a dependent nonempty intermediate step. It shows why total order alone settles degree six but need not make all raw degree-five boundaries inhabited.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_serial_gate_tracks.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/SerialGateTracks.agda`. The solver replays the commutator through a closed lens and a two-point packet beside two and three stationary lenses. The nonconstant seam comparisons use 432 and 976 signed switches. It verifies both endpoint identities, accepts the transported loops, and rejects the same first-site loop paired with stationary later sites. At a merger with an initial spectator it tests 341 meridian words by deleting and recabling the continuing strand; exactly the 308 nontrivial readings fail the seam condition. Agda checks the three-rule closure at its literal iterated-identity boundary and its placement beside another lens. The solver also accepts a JSON based-track query with the gate-forest graph format, a list of `charts` (each with `diagram`, `slot`, relative `input` and `output`, and signed `loop`) and the signed paths `seams` between successive expanded diagrams. It validates the shared gate, both endpoint equations and each seam before returning nullity. [Serial gate-track solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_serial_gate_tracks.py)

## Researcher Response 53

**The occurrence method for Q3(a) extends to arbitrary 4-attachments over a source-positive 3-skeleton, including 4-generators between identity 3-cells. There is also a finite sufficient test with no positivity hypothesis in any rank.** Both results concern every admissible \(E\), both coherence rules and every \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\). The distinction between lifting *one chosen factorization* and just finding some colouring matters here: with all colour choices, a folded strict 3-bubble can have two factorizations over the same ordered pair of factors.

### A lifting lemma for a free strict extension

Write \(F\) for ordinary strict evaluation. Say a map of strict skeleta has unique factor lifting if every specified binary factorization of the image of a cell lifts uniquely to a factorization of that very cell, and likewise for identities. Suppose this holds through degree \(n-1\), and all lifts of generators in their own degree are generators. Adjoin arbitrary free \(n\)-generators downstairs. Upstairs adjoin copies, preserving their actual attaching terms, subject to this condition:

*For each downstairs generator \(g\) and each strict lift \(a\) of its source, there is at most one copy of \(g\) with source \(a\).* Its target, when supplied, must be a strict lift of the declared target parallel to \(a\). A source may have no copy.

Then the extended strict map still has unique factor lifting and the generator-preimage property through \(n\). Moreover a downstairs \(n\)-cell, at a prescribed lift of its source, has **at most one** lift. If every generating occurrence in a chosen expression has a copy at every lift of its source, that expression lifts from every lift of its source.

To prove this, replay a strict \(n\)-expression from the given lifted source. An identity lifts as the identity there. A variable uses its selected copy, or the replay is undefined. For a top composite replay the first factor, then the second from its resulting target. At a lower cut split the lifted source over the displayed composite of sources by unique lower factor lifting; replay on both parts and paste. Their common lower boundary was fixed by the split. This construction is invariant in both value **and domain of definition** under the strict axioms. For associativity at a lower cut split once into three; for a top association the three replays occur in the same order. A unit split at a lower cut gives the identity at the matching face by unique identity and factor lifting, and replaying a top unit leaves the chosen cell fixed. Identity of a composite follows by splitting its source and composing the two lifted identities. For an interchange with both cuts below the top, the four lower sources are the same entries of the split grid, by unique lower factorization applied on its two decompositions. All four replays are made with these same inputs, and the two results agree by strict interchange upstairs. When the upper cut is the top cut, a defined replay on the side with two successive rows gives a first row of two lifted cells. The second row splits their common intermediate composite back into **their two individual targets**, by lower unique factor lifting. The four lifted cells can therefore be pasted by columns. Conversely the two columns of a defined replay can be pasted by rows, with the same intermediate split. This proves definedness in both directions even when a variable is missing at some other source. Inserting such an equation inside an expression preserves the subsequent sources. These checks cover the presentation of the free strict extension; no interchange of raw witnesses is asserted.

Every expression for a cell upstairs is recovered by replaying its projection, by induction on that expression. Consequently the replay of a specified decomposition of its image gives a decomposition of the given cell upstairs. At the top cut the first lift and then the second are forced; below it their sources and hence their lifts are forced. Replaying an identity or a generator gives the remaining two claims. Lower-dimensional identities padded above the generating skeleton present no additional split. By the positional pullback argument of Response 51, the resulting map of weak computads has the corresponding pullback on raw terms and surface classes at specified strict lifts. In adjoining the copies one uses this pullback **on the preceding skeleton** to give each chosen pair of strict faces its actual raw attaching terms, literally parallel by uniqueness on their common lower boundary. Thus the construction proceeds in declaration order. It proves the lemma without an occurrence-representation theorem for a nonpositive \(n\)-cell. [Strict factor lifting and the syntax pullback](#researcher-response-51)

### The two tests at a written fourth-degree boundary

First suppose the 3-skeleton is source-positive. Use all its occurrence colourings as in Response 51; its strict projection has unique factor lifting through three. For each 4-generator take the two finite occurrence diagrams of its **3-faces**, including the lower diagram padded by an identity when necessary. Glue the diagrams of their common 2-source and 2-target to the corresponding boundaries on both sides, and impose the crossing requirement on the object poles of the new generator. A colouring of this finite graph is exactly a pair of compatible strict face lifts at crossing poles. Indeed strict lifts on the lower positive skeleton are the labellings of the face diagrams, and equality on a common strict face is equality of its entire labelling. The two face maps need not be inclusions. Use the implication-graph test of Response 51 on the map from the source diagram to this graph. It decides in polynomial time in the explicit graphs whether every colouring of the source extends. If so, **select one** such extension per source. This selection is permitted even if the ordinary source contains no generating 3-cell. The lemma at \(n=4\) supplies unique factor lifting and forward existence for all strict 4-cells. At any old query with a colourable ordinary source 3-value, lift a proposed surface filler at the strict lift obtained by replay. Its old faces lift at the two signed strict faces by the raw pullback. Apply Response 49 upstairs and fold. The same conclusion from a colourable initial 2-diagram holds if the 3-generator extension tests of Response 51 pass. This proves the first assertion for arbitrary 4-attachments.

Here is the version for an arbitrary finite computad. Use two colours, all nondecreasing edge copies, and at each 2-generator all pairs of lifted attaching edge words with matching, crossing object poles. Through degree two this map **always** has unique factor lifting. Indeed every 2-generator that has a copy has nonempty ordinary attaching edge words on both sides: an empty word has equal coloured endpoints. Restrict downstairs temporarily to the whole graph and the 2-generators with both words nonempty. The map to this positive 2-skeleton has unique factor lifting by the ordinary positive-polyplex theorem. Its inclusion in the arbitrary 2-skeleton also has that property. At a binary split the count of any excluded 2-generator is the sum of the counts in the factors and is zero; both factors therefore belong to the smaller skeleton. Strict equality between expressions using this skeleton agrees there, since the strict axioms preserve each generating 2-count, including at each intermediate expression in an equality. Identities and the generator-preimage condition follow as well. This reasoning concerns strict 2-cells; the raw attaching edge words of all the copies are still lifted along their literal graph labellings. [Positive strict factorization](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1711.00744) · [Raw lifting along a graph labelling](#researcher-response-5)

At each 3-generator select **at most one** compatible crossing target per lifted source 2-cell. Omit a source if desired or if none exists. Apply the lemma at three. For each 4-generator and each resulting lift of its source 3-cell, ask whether its target 3-cell replays from the *same lifted 2-source*, ends at the *same lifted 2-target*, and has crossing object poles. All three conditions are needed. When they hold the two 3-cells are parallel; adjoin their 4-copy. The lemma applies again. If every 4-generator passes at every available source lift, and the ordinary source 3-cell of the old query has a lift, reflection holds at that query by the preceding pullback and cut argument. In a 3-computad the 4-generator check is vacuous. One can instead start at the target by doing the corresponding replay from targets at the chosen rank; only the order in a strict top composite changes. The cover itself always uses the declared orientations of the generators.

These choices and tests are finite on explicit ordinary expressions. For a fixed source or target 2-face containing a generator without a copy there are no lifts. Otherwise compute its lifts on the finite positive occurrence diagram. This also handles identities on edge words. Given a 3-cell, enumerate the lifts of its source 2-cell and replay a strict expression at each. To test a top attachment only equality of the resulting **2**-boundaries is needed, in the positive lifted 2-skeleton. Thus one may enumerate all partial choices of 3-copies in search of a successful test. In the four effective settings a successful test decides individual inhabitation when the count changes of the retained 4-rules have a common weak sign, in particular for every successful 3-computad test. Fix a source lift, enumerate the compatible target lifts, and apply the bounded search of Response 49. If a raw filler exists downstairs its ordinary value replays and the raw filler lifts at that value. An upstairs raw filler folds. [Bounded search with arbitrary attaching words](#researcher-response-49)

There is a refinement if some 4-generators fail the test. For a strict 3-cell \(u\), let \(N(u)\) count each declared 3-generator with multiplicity. For each subset \(K\) of 4-generators keep it as a possible support only if there are positive integers \(m_g\) for \(g\in K\) satisfying
\[
 N(t)-N(s)=\sum_{g\in K}m_g\bigl(N(\partial^+g)-N(\partial^-g)\bigr).
\]
This includes the empty sum. It is a finite family of integer linear feasibility tests. Necessity follows on a strict expression by top telescoping and additivity at all lower cuts, with identities contributing zero difference. For each surviving support one may choose a *different* table of 3-copies. It suffices to lift the source and pass the 4-generator tests for the members of that support alone. Indeed any putative surface filler has an ordinary strict expression with one such support. In its replay none of the other top variables is required. For the syntax pullback take the upper map to the **whole** downstairs presentation, with no copies of the other top variables; the partial lemma is precisely what permits this. A false positive in the balance test can only make the sufficient condition harder to meet.

### Why choices at a degenerate source matter

On one object and one loop \(f\), take a unary 2-generator \(a:f\to f\) and a 2-generator \(H:ff\to ff\). Write \(P=ff\). Take 3-generators
\[
                      b:1_P\to H;H,\qquad d:H;H\to1_P .
\]
At a fixed crossing copy of \(P\) mark the first of the two edges. There are two lifts of the excursion \(B=b;d\) if all compatible generator copies are used: mark the first or the second wire at the seam of \(H;H\). Call them \(B_0,B_1\). These are distinct ordinary strict 3-cells, as their counts of the two declared birth copies differ. Both are endomorphisms of the identity 2-cell on exactly the same coloured 1-word. In the **ordinary strict** category their top composites satisfy \(B_0;B_1=B_1;B_0\) by interchange and units at cuts one and two. Thus that one upstairs strict cell has two distinct ordered splits above the specified split \(B;B\). This defeats an unrestricted use of the unique-split hypothesis on the all-choices map. It does not give a surface counterexample.

For a degree-four application retain the birth with opposite middle port at each crossing copy of \(P\). A death has a copy for each arriving two-gate row with equal outside ports, returning to the identity at that port. Put \(L=a\mathbin{\triangleright} f\), \(V=L;1_P\), and adjoin the single 4-generator
\[
                           z:1_V\longrightarrow L *_1 B.
\]
The whiskering on the right has both written 2-faces \(V\). At every lifted source its gate \(L\) forces the left crossing position. The selected excursion begins and ends at the required port, so the 4-test passes. This gives reflection at the old boundary and at its unit-padded writings, and the increasing-count decision applies. Its reverse is empty by the 3-count balance. The 3- and 4-sources of the two introductions are identities. The unary gate is genuinely used, and in any category-valued interval at \(ff\) it can be placed on either occurrence; those two placements cannot both cross a single cut. This example therefore requires more than a cut of the edge-word interval.

**Validation.** The checker replays births at empty temporal substrings and deaths on adjacent pairs with their actual coloured middle ports. It verified 1,938 defined instances of strict interchange of independent updates, including coincident empty slots in the two factors, and found both replays undefined on the other 416 instances. It constructs both distinct lifted bubbles, verifies that they fold literally to the same raw term, and checks the unit-padded 4-witness over the original and the selected presentations. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_partial_lifts.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PartialLifts.agda`. Agda checks these written terms in the Gray kernel. The equality of the commuted strict bubbles and the partial lifting theorem are the mathematical arguments above. [Partial-lift checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_partial_lifts.py)

## Researcher Response 54

**A raw obstruction in degree six or higher on an ordinary 3-computad requires a simultaneous three-use chart, or a simultaneous pair of point markers in the same planar face with complementary empty sides.** For the second alternative, in one order $a,b$ of the pair the output 2-diagram of $a$ and the input 2-diagram of $b$ must both have zero generating 2-gates. The alternatives concern charts of the supported computation, including the raw interface paths. The condition applies on arbitrary planar bases, with changing path widths, closed components and compound 3-rules. Consequently all ten individual ordinary surfaces reflect at every old type of degree at least six where both alternatives are excluded.

Here is a finite sufficient test on the named gate-incidence network of Response 52. Check that no three uses are pairwise incomparable by gate dependence. Among incomparable uses check that there is no ordered pair satisfying the two zero counts whose spatial paths are empty at the *same object*. This implies the result, even if the computation has arbitrarily many different serial orders. For example, it suffices that the network be a union of two chains and every rule consume at least one 2-gate, with unrestricted outputs. A computation with just one rule over an empty spatial path and any number of rules on nonempty paths also passes the second test. The sharper chart test distinguishes different faces at the same object. If the 2-generators additionally have a common nonempty spatial input side, or a common nonempty spatial output side, the degree-five result of Response 36 gives reflection at these labels in every degree. [Serial histories and gate dependence](#researcher-response-52) · [Degree five on the one-sided 2-skeleta](#researcher-response-36)

### Which split maps inject on loop groups?

Give the uses and the gates at each cut individual names by occurrence transport. One may do this in the flattened scalar network first, giving each use a name and each output gate its use and port index, as in Response 52. The fixed-end mapping space on these names is the homotopy colimit over ordered partitions of the uses. The coefficient $H_s$ for a schedule $s$ is the iterated homotopy pullback of its planar batch groupoids with a path at each seam and at each prescribed end. It is a 1-type, whether or not any chart group is trivial. A loop in it is determined by its batch braids with the conjugation conditions at the seams. This follows from the homotopy pullback formula because the spaces of paths at specified positions in the intervening planar groupoids are homotopy discrete. The same observations apply after restriction to given incidence data and components. In the higher relative globe test the named lifts are obtained from the common lower faces: occurrence names give coverings with permutation transport, so they detect the higher difference spheres. Equivalently a raw filler in the named computation descends to the literal faces by folding. [Space-valued batch attachments](#why-chart-components-suffice-for-4-tracks) · [Named planar loop groups](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-164)

Suppose a two-use batch at a chart is split into $a$ first, then $b$. Its loop is a pure positioned braid on its gates, including distinct marker strands for $a,b$. The two new readings retain $a$ while expanding the input of $b$, and retain $b$ while expanding the output of $a$. If either expanded side has a gate, restricting that braid to one representative of its block, with all original other strands, recovers the original braid. So a loop in the kernel of the split must have been trivial in this batch. Other batches are unchanged and the seam equations then give injectivity for the entire coefficient.

If both expansions have zero gates, the two readings on signed braids just forget $b$ and $a$ respectively. Forgetting a strand at a gate with incident wires is injective on the *positioned* pure group. Indeed anchored components contribute no moving strand to any sibling factor. A floating component containing this gate has another gate that can serve as a representative, since a progressive closed component with a single gate can only be a point. Choose a representative gate for every direct floating child of each face. Restriction to those representatives reads its entire pure sibling braid, with the other factors acting trivially there; this follows at the grouped reading by restriction of the component cables. If both markers are isolated points but in distinct faces, the two restrictions together still recover every factor: for any face choose the restriction preserving all its direct children. This uses the actual complementary face, including different faces bounded by the same anchored diagram. Conversely if both markers are points in the same face, move the pair by a full twist along a joining arc avoiding the other children. Its restriction to the two marked strands is nonidentity, but forgetting either makes the motion null. Both exterior expansions of the batch kill this loop as well, since each forgets at least one member. Thus it extends by identity loops on all other batches, and lies in the kernel of this split on the fixed-end coefficient. Conjugating to the specified seam readings preserves these conclusions. This proves the stated local criterion in both directions. Failure of a split to be injective alone does not decide whether its total homotopy colimit has a higher obstruction.

### The cubical complex of groups

Exclude simultaneous triples. Each schedule cell is now a cube: a tied pair contributes an interval and distinct tied pairs contribute independent factors. Its corners have distinct serial orders on the marked names. At every cube take one copy for each component of its coefficient, with facets identified by refinement. Choosing basepoints and transport paths in these components gives their fundamental groups and the refinement homomorphisms up to coherent conjugation. The homotopy colimit of the resulting groupoids is the original schedule space, since each coefficient is a 1-type. When the preceding split test holds all these homomorphisms are injective; one obtains a complex of groups on the face subdivision of this cubical space. There can be several cubes of the same schedule shape over one serial history.

Here are the local developments. At a point $z$ of a serial coefficient, the possible lifts of an incident exchange at consecutive positions $i,i+1$ are the components of the homotopy fibre over $z$ of the tied-to-serial refinement. Each such component is contractible by injectivity on $\pi_1$. This is the usual coset description of developed germs, with all contributing tied components included. Germs at overlapping position pairs span no square; without a triple batch they cannot occur in the same cube. Two different germs at the same pair are also incompatible. For disjoint position pairs, **every chosen tuple of lifts spans exactly one developed cube**. To verify this, express the fixed-end serial coefficient using the two-batch segments at those pairs, the intervening other segments, and the endpoint paths between segments. Replacing the segment at one pair by its tied batch is base change along its map to that segment's serial coefficient with the two outside endpoints varying. Replacing several disjoint segments is their simultaneous homotopy base change, since the pullbacks at all outside endpoints are the same. This works when two chosen pairs are consecutive segments sharing an endpoint. The homotopy fibre of this joint replacement at $z$ is therefore the product of the individual fibres. Choosing components gives exactly one joint component. Incidence names or support restrictions do not obstruct the product: every replacement refines to the same named serial data with the same connections at its outside endpoints.

In particular every developed vertex link is simplicial and flag. Its simplices consist precisely of compatible choices on pairwise disjoint position intervals. This is a statement about the developed links, which also accounts for the local braid-group cosets. The cubes have unit Euclidean shapes and bounded dimension for the finite set of uses. The cubical link criterion gives nonpositive curvature in the local developments. At other points of their cubes the normal links are links of simplices in these vertex links: choose a developed corner of the given cube, with the incident coset, and retain its cofaces. The complex-of-groups developability theorem now gives a contractible CAT(0) development. The classifying space of the complex of groups is aspherical: it is the homotopy quotient of the development by its fundamental group, by the stabilizer groupoids on cells. The face-flag model of that classifying space is exactly the coefficient homotopy colimit above. This proves asphericity component by component, also if an individual schedule admits no such lift. The fixed-end globe comparison finishes the degree-six-and-above assertion. [Bridson and Haefliger, II.5.20 and III.C.4.17–18](https://webhomes.maths.ed.ac.uk/~v1ranick/papers/bridsonhaefligerx.pdf)

For contrast within the test, two different free 3-scalars at the same object with one occurrence each give the raw factor $\operatorname{Conf}_2(\mathbb R^3)\simeq S^2$. Their joint chart has two point markers and both orders satisfy the zero-side condition. The two serial fixed-end coefficients are contractible, and the joint coefficient is the planar two-point configuration, homotopy equivalent to a circle. Its suspension is the displayed 2-sphere, which supplies an unfilled old degree-six boundary. This is a raw obstruction. If one change instead acts on a gate inside a fixed closed lens, its marker has incident wires, and it can form an independent chain of such changes while the free scalar executes outside. With width at most two the result gives raw filling in every degree from six on, in the presence of the nontrivial planar braid groups of the lens diagram. [Pure scalar configurations and fixed ends](#researcher-response-44)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_two_chains.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/TwoGateChains.agda` with an existing directory. The network test agrees with direct two-chain covers on 1,024 five-use networks. For the local split test the checker builds positioned exchange graphs and uses the Artin action on actual return braids, including products. Its 60 oriented chart tests across five diagrams agree with the kernel readings on 4,034 return words and products. These include points on opposite sides of an anchored through-wire, points outside and inside a lens, and a marker on a lens wire. For the link test it constructs the two spatial orders of each joint marker diagram of a left and right chain on three wires, expands their four one-marker sides, and identifies equal charts. It then builds the whole cubical incidence table before testing the cliques in each measured link. For two length-four chains there are 70 vertices, 219 edges, 215 squares, 70 three-cubes and four four-cubes. All 70 links pass. Agda checks a free scalar beside a closed lens whose wire undergoes two gate-dependent changes. The JSON mode tests a named network and optionally the split at a specified positioned chart; a reported potential singular split still requires an actual common face. [Width-two schedule solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_two_chains.py)

## Researcher Response 55

**On an arbitrary ordinary 3-computad, a raw obstruction in degree six or above supported on at most two 3-rule uses requires a genuine 3-scalar.** In ordinary planar evaluation both 2-faces of that use have zero gates on an empty spatial path. There must also be a joint chart where the other use is a point marker in the same face and has a zero-gate side. In particular all ten individual ordinary surfaces reflect in these degrees at weight at most two if neither use is such a scalar. This improves the two-use case of [Response 54](#researcher-response-54), whose split-injectivity criterion can fail even for a proper death and a proper birth.

### Recovering the joint chart path

The additional case is a death consuming a nonempty closed diagram \(P\) with zero-gate output, and a birth with zero-gate input creating a nonempty closed diagram \(Q\). Both have empty spatial paths. There can be stationary wires, other gates, or nested components in the containing diagram. Name gates and uses by occurrence as in [Response 52](#researcher-response-52). First allow the two outside ends to vary. The refinement from a simultaneous chart to the **birth-first** serial coefficient is fully faithful as a map of groupoids.

Indeed take an arrow between the refinements of any two simultaneous charts. At their canonical serial representatives the seam paths are identities. The arrow is given by a path \(u\) in the chart with the birth marker and the gates of \(P\), and a path \(v\) in the chart with the death marker and the gates of \(Q\). Expanding the birth in \(u\) to \(Q\) and the death in \(v\) to \(P\) gives the same positioned braid between the fully expanded readings. This is precisely the seam compatibility since the ordinary diagram space is a 1-type. Restrict the common braid to the spectator gates and one specified gate of each of \(P,Q\). This gives a candidate joint path \(w\), including for arrows between different joint readings.

There is a positioned realization of this restriction. In \(u\), retain the birth marker and all spectator gates.
