Erase the internal wires of \(P\), retain its chosen gate as a point at its ambient object and erase its other gates. At the initial reading \(P\) occupies its own insertion disk with no exterior ports. Under each exchange its wire incidences persist, and spectator wires and gates remain outside the bounded faces of its connected components. Equivalently, project each horizontal slice onto the spectator wires in their order. The projected offset of a retained row counts exactly the retained wires to its left. An exchange of two retained rows still satisfies the same left-right disjointness inequality after projection. An exchange involving an erased row leaves the projected reading fixed. The new point occupies the gap of its floating component, at the ambient object after its internal wires are erased. At the two grouped endpoints this construction puts it at precisely the original death marker. Thus the projected switches give a path of joint charts with the required two endpoints. It is unnecessary for the separate components of \(P\) to remain together along \(u\).

To recover \(u\) from \(w\), keep every strand of \(P\) and just the chosen strand of \(Q\) in the common expanded braid. On the \(u\) side this recovers \(u\). On the \(v\) side it duplicates the death strand of \(w\) by the whole ordered packet \(P\), since deleting the extra \(Q\)-strands commutes with cabling the disjoint death strand. The other restriction recovers \(v\) by duplicating the birth strand by \(Q\). These are equalities of paths in the positioned groupoids by planar braid faithfulness. The same double restriction recovers any joint preimage, proving uniqueness. The argument allows compound ports with internal bounded faces and disconnected ports. Choosing names first also covers repeated generator colours. [Positioned braid faithfulness](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-79)

### Consequences at fixed ends

A fully faithful map of groupoids identifies its domain up to equivalence with a union of components of its codomain. This property persists under homotopy pullback, so one can prescribe both outside 2-ends, including their paths. With two named uses the schedule space is the homotopy pushout of the two serial coefficients along the joint coefficient. On the birth-first side the joint coefficient identifies exactly with its image components. The pushout is consequently equivalent to the death-first coefficient together with the disjoint birth-first components outside this image. Each is a 1-type. This argument does not assume that the map on the death-first side is injective, or that every birth-first chart admits a joint refinement. If the gate incidence forces a single order, its single serial coefficient already is a 1-type.

There is a degree-five conclusion on every component meeting the joint chart or containing a death-first history. Each such component is contractible at fixed ends. A loop at the death-first vertex is determined by its two site braids. Expansion of the first site to the prescribed initial diagram detects its entire braid by retaining one gate of \(P\). Expansion of the last site to the final diagram does so by retaining one gate of \(Q\). The end conditions force both braids to be trivial, independently of the seam path. The unmatched birth-first components have only the 1-type conclusion from this argument.

To deduce the weight bound, apply Response 54 whenever its criterion holds. In every remaining two-use chart there is an order with empty output on the first marker and empty input on the second. If neither marker is a genuine 3-scalar, their opposite ports are both nonempty, exactly the case just proved. The named coverings detect the higher difference spheres as in Response 54. The conclusion concerns existence of raw fillers, so it gives reflection for each of the ten individual surfaces in those degrees. Two genuine free 3-scalars in a common face do give the raw two-point configuration sphere of Response 54. Whether an individual surface fills a particular obstructed raw boundary is a further question.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_birth_death_packets.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/BirthDeathPackets.agda` with an existing directory. The solver checks both positioned serial paths at their written ends, tests equality after the two seam expansions, then constructs a joint path and recables it to both given classes. Across singleton, lens and disconnected ports, including placements in a bounded face at a different object, it reconstructs 432 lifts and rejects 36 incompatible pairs. Independently enumerating both three-strand path groups through length five at two two-point packets gives 263 braids per side. Among 1,517 endpoint-compatible pairs, exactly five have equal four-strand seam images, equal to the five predicted joint images in that bound. Agda checks a raw exchange between the two orders for a death consuming a lens and a point and a birth creating two lenses. JSON input accepts a pair of serial paths at two named joint readings and returns a joint word or null for incompatible seam classes. [Birth and death path solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_birth_death_packets.py)

## Researcher Response 56

**A degree-six-or-higher raw obstruction at weight at most two on an arbitrary ordinary 3-computad requires two genuine 3-scalar occurrences in the same planar face.** The occurrences may have the same generator label. In every other case the raw higher boundaries fill, hence all ten individual ordinary surfaces reflect there. The additional case beyond [Response 55](#researcher-response-55) is one genuine 3-scalar beside a rule on an empty spatial path that creates or consumes a nonempty closed diagram. A single probe calculation gives the based degree-five group there as well.

Fix names and the two outer 2-ends. Call the scalar the probe. Write \(P,Q\) for the input and output diagrams at the other site. In this calculation either of them can be empty. Omit the probe from a history, retaining the other site, the ordinary diagram and both end paths. Its one-use coefficient \(B\) is a 1-type. When at least one of \(P,Q\) contains a gate, each component of \(B\) is contractible. Indeed a loop is determined by the site braid, and expansion at the nonempty end followed by restriction to one port gate recovers that braid. The fixed-end condition therefore kills it.

Over a chosen point of \(B\), the fibre of the schedule pushout is the homotopy pushout of the spaces of probe positions *before*, *at* and *after* the retained site, with maps from the middle to the two sides. To see this with nonconstant end and seam paths, use the single-site marked diagram together with the two paths from its expansions to the fixed ends as a representative of the base point. In the joint chart one adds a point to the marked diagram. In either serial ordering one adds a point to the appropriate expanded diagram. Forgetting the point at the probe-only batch is its expansion at both faces. Composing the two seam/end paths on that side therefore recovers the one-use end path; the unused choice of subdivision of this path has contractible space of choices (equivalently compose two groupoid arrows and vary the intermediate object by isomorphism). The fibre is the homotopy fibre of point-forgetting at that expanded diagram. The end paths transport the choices of faces. These equivalences intertwine refinement with actual port insertion. Taking the pushout commutes with taking this fibre because homotopy colimits of spaces over a space are computed fibrewise, or by transporting all three coefficients over the path groupoid of \(B\).

At a fixed diagram these probe positions form one punctured plane per face with the probe's object label, one hole per directly enclosed floating component. This is the one-point case of the sibling braid calculation in [Response 44](#researcher-response-44). Apply it to the joint chart treating the retained site as an ordinary point, and separately to the two expanded diagrams. Outside the face of the site the two maps are identities after transport. A bounded face introduced inside \(P\) or \(Q\) contributes its own disjoint punctured plane on that side if it has the probe's label. No joint position maps into its interior. This matters for diagrams containing lenses within lenses.

In the face of the site, let \(h\) count the other directly enclosed components and let \(m,n\) count the *outermost* floating components of the two closed ports. Nonempty closed ports have at least one such component. Write \(U_j\) for a plane with \(j\) punctures. The remaining connected piece is
\[
                   U_{h+m}\ \longleftarrow\ U_{h+1}\ \longrightarrow\ U_{h+n}
\]
taken as a homotopy pushout. Choose the same access paths to the \(h\) stationary holes outside the site disk on both sides. Their meridians are fixed. The site meridian maps on each side to the peripheral loop about *all* outermost children in the inserted disk. A small loop outside the insertion disk moves about the entire packet under refinement; a cut along disjoint arcs to the child disks writes this loop as their ordered product. With the positive crossing comb used in the solver the last child's factor comes first. Gate multiplicity inside a connected child does not duplicate its meridian.

Retract each punctured plane to a rose on these meridians, representing the two maps by the described based words. The double mapping cylinder, after contracting its basepoint interval, has one equality 2-cell for each shared spectator and one for the equality of the two port products. Eliminate the duplicate spectator generators with their equality cells. If \(m+n>0\), a port meridian occurs exactly once in the remaining attaching word and in no other relator. Changing that generator in the free basis to the attaching word and contracting the resulting disk gives a wedge of \(h+m+n-1\) circles. This proves a homotopy type, including the absence of higher homotopy. If \(m=n=0\), the attaching word is empty and the **fibre piece** is a wedge of \(h\) circles and a 2-sphere; the forgotten one-use base can then have its own loops. For two free scalars the full space in that common face is the ordered two-point configuration in the product of the punctured plane with time, as computed in Response 44.

Thus for a genuine probe and a nonempty port every fixed-end history component is a graph up to homotopy. In the site face its based degree-five difference group is free of rank \(h+m+n-1\); in the new bounded faces one counts their own direct children. Already with no spectators a birth of three separate points gives rank two, whereas a birth of one lens enclosing these same three points gives rank zero in the exterior component. For the stated weight bound apply Responses 54 and 55 first. Their only remaining candidate with precisely one genuine scalar has a second point site in the same face with a zero side and a nonempty opposite side. The graph computation handles it. For distinct faces, Response 54 applies even if both uses are genuine scalars. The two-scalar configuration sphere in an empty face shows that the same-face exception really is necessary for the *raw* conclusion.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_probe_packets.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/ProbePackets.agda` with an existing directory. The checker extracts the direct-child sets of 32 positioned diagram spans, including nested lenses, and executes the peripheral braid both by cabling the whole site and by circling individual child diagrams. All eight comparisons agree in the faithful Artin action, while reversing the child-product order differs whenever there are at least two children. It checks 592 based word reductions against insertion of the port relation. Agda checks the raw comparison exchanging a genuine scalar and the birth of a lens and a separate point. JSON input computes the site-face component from the two diagrams and reduces a supplied meridian word there. [Probe and packet solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_probe_packets.py)

## Researcher Response 57

**In Q3(a) one can use all compatible 3-generator lifts simultaneously, even at identity faces.** On an arbitrary finite 3-computad let \(s,t\) be the two old 3-terms of a fourth-degree query. If their ordinary strict values differ, neither theory has an inhabitant. If their common strict value has even one lift to the cut computad with *all* compatible copies of the 3-generators, reflection holds at this query for every admissible \(E\), either coherence rule and \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\). In the four effective settings this gives a decision of individual inhabitation. Positivity and a fixed choice of birth at each source are unnecessary. Below we also give a finite sufficient test with arbitrary 4-attachments.

There is an unconditional consequence for Q1(a) in higher dimensions. For \(h\ge2\), any counterexample in degree \(h+1\) on a finite \(h\)-computad has a counterexample with the same lower skeleton in which **every generating \(h\)-cell occurs exactly once in each of the two old faces**. This includes degenerate attachments at any rank. The reduction allows the two orders of these occurrences to differ. The proof uses the order respected by *conversion* in the faces; a cell between the faces can change that order.

### Splitting an operation at the actual lifted surface cell

Use \(L\) for the chosen binary surface on \(E\) and \(H\) for its raw syntax. Work through rank \(h\). Suppose \(Y\to X\) is a generator labelling preserving the written attachments, with the unique strict factor lifting and generator-preimage properties of Response 53 **through \(h-1\)**. In degree \(h\) allow any collection of copies at compatible lifted raw faces. We need two facts:

1. Given a surface lift of \(o(\sigma)\), where \(o\) is a rank-\(h\) surface operation on its ordinary positional tree and \(\sigma\) a labelling downstairs, there is a lifted labelling of that tree whose evaluation is this very surface lift. The lifted labels lie over the corresponding given surface labels.
2. Every surface lift of the class of a raw \(h\)-term \(s\) has a raw representative that folds **literally** to \(s\). Its lower strict boundary is that of the surface lift.

We prove these statements without unique factorization of the all-copies *strict* map in degree \(h\). For a term of rank \(h\), read the ordered list of its occurrences of ambient rank-\(h\) variables. For a head take the lists of the top arguments in their fixed tree-slot order, concatenating recursively; lower arguments contribute nothing. An ambient variable contributes its one entry. This list counts precisely the ordinary top generating occurrences: the ordinary generic value of a head uses each top slot once, and substitution adds the counts. At a word-level binary product the list is the concatenation of the lists of the moving top operands in written order. A rank-\(h\) identity on a lower operand has empty list.

The list descends through interchange-free binary conversion. More precisely, freely replace each \(h\)-generator with parallel copies at its *unchanged* attaching type. At a specified ordered list of copy names over the old list, there is exactly one renamed surface class over the old class with that list. To verify this, first rename a representative by replacing its consecutive top occurrences; its types and incidence below the top rank are unchanged. Lower classes are unaffected by top declarations: truncate at the lower rank and put a unique cell over each higher parallel boundary to interpret proofs using higher declarations, as in Response 5. In a word equality (i), associativity, units and identity of a composite preserve the ordered list of top leaves; this also follows by pruning identity branches and flattening each equal-cut product in the word algebra. Each top variable used by a word on its *generic tree* occurs there just once, since its ordinary strict cell uses each such position at most once. Thus on either side of a specialized word equality the chosen list splits into the same named sublists for the top arguments. The equation applies to that renamed tuple; restrict to the word support if an unused slot would have no copy. A rank-\(h\) equation (ii) involves no top slot: its faces are words of lower rank and the coherence-case head must use the whole arity. Congruence in the generic type keeps the slot order. Congruence in a substituted top argument transports its own sublist, and substitution into a term concatenates these sublists in the order of occurrence. These checks transport a conversion proof, in either direction, to the renamed representatives. They also show that two representatives upstairs folding to equal classes and bearing the same list are equal: transport the folded equality with that list from one end to the other, where replacement in the final representative is literal. Equivalently the construction is the family interpretation through rank \(h\), indexed by the ordered lists of parallel copies, with unique cells at higher boundaries. This accounts for conversion inside a derivation as well. For raw terms the renaming assertions are immediate from term formation. [Globular support, clamping and truncation](#researcher-response-5) · [Ordered leaves of interchange-free words](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-170)

Now take a representative of the given lift upstairs. Number its top occurrences with distinct names. Downstairs replace the generating data in degree \(h\) by one parallel generator for each of these names at the original attaching type. Upstairs use the whole previous lower skeleton and assign to each new name only the **one** copy with the lifted faces occurring at that occurrence in the chosen representative. The resulting named map has at most one copy per source at rank \(h\). Response 53 gives unique strict factor lifting on this map, hence the pullback for both \(L\) and \(H\) at specified strict lifts. The chosen representative itself lives in the named upper presentation. Its named projection equals the desired named expression downstairs by the preceding renaming calculation. Only this auxiliary map depends on the representative.

For (1), in the named expression \(o(\sigma)\) the top positions of the generic operation occur once each at its ordinary total. Number the occurrences in the actual labels by their consecutive sublists in that generic occurrence order. The resulting expression therefore evaluates at the named labelling. Split the strict value of the named upper representative over that ordinary positional paste by unique factor lifting. This gives compatible strict lifts at all slots, also at stationary slots. Lift each of the named surface labels there by the pullback. Applying \(o\) gives the same named surface cell upstairs by injectivity of the pullback, since it has the prescribed projection and strict value. Forget the numbers. For (2), use the named raw representative of \(s\) downstairs given by its ordered list. Its class is the named projection of the upper representative. Lift it raw at that upper strict value. The two pullbacks identify its surface class with the chosen one; forgetting the numbers folds it literally to \(s\). This argument proves existence at the actual surface cell even when one unnumbered strict value admits several splits. [Partial strict lifting lemma](#researcher-response-53) · [Pullback on weak attaching terms](#researcher-response-51)

### Lifting existence one degree higher

Allow a further degree of generators and use the same map on the lower skeleton. Suppose each downstairs \((h+1)\)-declaration has the following property: for **every surface lift of its written source**, a surface arrow in \(Y\) reaches some lift of its target at the resulting parallel boundary. Then this holds for every surface \((h+1)\)-term. There is no claim about preserving a selected strict value or lifting a particular top witness.

Here is the model argument. Below the tested rank use the downstairs surface classes through degree \(h\). Between parallel \(h\)-classes put a single \((h+1)\)-cell precisely when the displayed universal-existential condition holds, and use a unique cell at every compatible higher boundary. Use the same lower skeleta when speaking of lifts of lower classes. They compute the low classes of each full presentation by the truncation argument above. Lower operations are the given ones. For an operation of degree \(h+1\) take its ordinary positional tree \(P\); the globular positional calculation of Response 5 presents operations in this form, also those using conversion on the bound arity. Its negative and positive face operations lie over the signed \(h\)-boundaries of the ordinary total. Given a lift of the evaluated source, apply (1) to its face operation, splitting it into a labelling of the negative \(h\)-boundary tree. For a boundary of height less than \(h\) use the lower pullback, including identity padding. In particular all slots of dimension less than \(h\) in \(P\) have now received consistent lifts.

Passing from that boundary to the whole tree adds the stacks of \((h+1)\)-variables. Within each stack start at its already labelled \(h\)-source; each successive globe introduces its new parallel \(h\)-target and the variable between them. This follows by suspending the lists of adjacent versions at the top of the tree; shorter branches are present on both signed boundaries already. The relation for the corresponding input \((h+1)\)-cell supplies an upper target lift **and** an upper surface cell at these faces. Use them as the next two labels. The lower incidence was fixed on the negative boundary and the new target is the source for the next member of its stack. When \(P\) has no top slot the initial split already supplies the whole tuple. Apply the given operation in \(L(Y)\) to this labelling. Its source is the chosen upper cell by the split, and its target folds to the required class by the face formula. Thus every surface operation is defined. In the tested rank any two computations at the same boundary agree by thinness; in higher ranks existence is automatic. Together with the lower surface laws this verifies the model, so assigning the declarations proves the assertion.

At a *raw* query with a raw source lift, a downstairs surface witness consequently gives an upstairs surface witness to some target lift. By (2) this target has a representative that folds to the original written raw target. Its lower faces are literally those of the chosen source lift: their folds are the same raw terms, and their strict values agree because the surface lifts are parallel. The lower raw pullback gives this literal equality. Whenever reflection is known upstairs, the raw filler there now folds to one at the original query.

Take in particular an \(h\)-computad with a failed old \((h+1)\)-type and use the same lower skeleton with a fresh parallel generator for each ordered top occurrence of the old source. The source has its specified raw lift and the condition on \((h+1)\)-generators is vacuous. The argument gives a renamed old target and a surface witness upstairs. The ordinary strict value of the witness is an identity above the generating dimension. Thus the two faces have the same counts of every *named* generator, one of each. A raw filler would fold to the original failed boundary. This proves the unconditional reduction stated above. In fact, fixing the numbering on the source gives a finite disjunction for an individual query, separately for raw and surface inhabitation. Apply the same argument to the raw operad for the raw direction (its list transports are literal); in the opposite direction simply fold. If the multiplicities agree and are \(m_g\), enumerate at most \(\prod_g m_g!\) assignments to the target, using each name of its generator exactly once. One can discard an assignment whenever its named ordinary strict value differs from that of the numbered source. An inhabitant downstairs exists exactly when one of the retained named boundaries has an inhabitant in the same theory.

### The all-copies cut test

In degree four take \(Y_{\le2}\) to be the two-colour cut construction of Response 53. Its map has the requisite unique factor lifting through degree two on **every** computad, including ones with empty attaching words. Adjoin every compatible crossing copy of every 3-generator. For each 4-generator enumerate the raw lifts of its source and target 3-terms, and adjoin a copy for every parallel pair with crossing exterior poles. Enumeration here can use the *written expressions*: at a variable try its finitely many copies, at a raw head try the possible lifted argument tuples, checking faces literally. It enumerates exactly the raw lifts, since folding preserves the head and substitution. Test that every raw source lift has a parallel raw target lift. At crossing poles the added copy serves this pair. At stationary poles both lifts lie on the graph of that side, where parallel 3-terms compare in degree four by graph-line lifting. The relation asks only for such an arrow; a stationary top copy is unnecessary. Conversely, if no parallel raw target lift exists, (2) and the lower pullback rule out even a parallel surface target lift. Thus this finite check is exact for the stated condition on the declarations of the all-copies cover. By (2) it gives the model hypothesis, and any old query with a raw source lift reflects by Response 49. The support restriction by 3-count balance of Response 53 also applies by first restricting to the possible 4-declarations in a witness: ordinary closed support restricts the syntax, or lift across the inclusion with just those declarations by the partial lemma of Response 53 at rank four with *identical lower skeleton*. One may make the cut test separately in each such restriction. [Cut reflection and bounded local search](#researcher-response-49) · [Counting top declarations](#researcher-response-53)

For the assertion using a common ordinary strict label on a 3-computad, it remains to check that a strict lift of that label supplies a raw source lift. Through rank three the all-copies strict map has **existence** of lifting a specified decomposition of an already lifted 3-cell. In fact start with any decorated expression representing this lifted cell and transfer its decoration across a chain of ordinary strict axioms to the desired expression of its projection. At each reassociation or interchange keep the assigned lifted pieces and repaste them. At an interchange with top composition, the intermediate splits into the two actual targets by unique factor lifting through degree two, exactly as in Response 53. Units expand by the identity at the matching lifted face. Expanding the identity on a lower composite splits its lower label by the same lower lifting property. Strict equality of lower labels is respected there. Thus an expression with the desired decomposition receives some compatible decoration evaluating to the original strict lift. No uniqueness of the transported assignment is needed.

Choose the desired expression by expanding the strict positional pastes of the written 3-source. Each of its top variable occurrences has now received a particular coloured copy. Give those occurrences distinct names on both sides as in (1). The decorated expression yields the specified strict lift in the resulting named map: each variable is assigned its one chosen copy, and the intermediate lower faces match as before. The named raw pullback therefore lifts the written source; forget the names. This proves the common-label criterion. With 4-declarations one can simply test for a raw source lift directly. Under a common weak sign of the 4-rule count changes the finite decision follows by fixing a source lift, enumerating the parallel raw target lifts and applying Response 49 to each. A surface filler downstairs implies a surface filler at one of these pairs by the model; a raw filler upstairs folds. The dual model starts with a target lift and fills stacks from their positive boundaries, if every target lift of each declaration has a parallel source lift. In particular no 4-rule count assumption is needed on 3-computads.

### Two different births required at one source

On one loop \(f\) let \(P=ff\), take \(a:f\to f\), \(H:P\to P\), and take the birth \(b:1_P\to H;H\) and death \(d:H;H\to1_P\) of Response 53. Let \(L,R\) be the unary gate \(a\) placed on the left or right wire of \(P\), respectively. For \(J=L,R\) adjoin two directed 3-generators between \(H\) and \(H;J\), one in each direction. Form a 3-endomorphism \(C_J\) of \(1_P\): take the birth, apply the forward and backward changes on the **first** \(H\) with the second \(H\) fixed, then take the death. In any cut lift, the attachment \(H;J\) forces its outgoing port to be on the side of \(J\). This is exactly the middle port of the born row. Conversely at either crossing copy of \(P\), choosing that middle port supplies the indicated lift. The composite \(C_L;C_R\) thus requires two different copies of \(b\) at the very same lifted source \(1_P\). It has a raw lift in the all-copies presentation and none for any fixed choice of target per birth source.

For an old 4-boundary add 3-endomorphisms \(U,V\) of \(L\) and a 4-generator \(U\to V\). Follow \(U\) whiskered by the empty temporal slot with \(L *_1 (C_L;C_R)\); use the same writing with \(V\) as target. Every source lift of the 4-declaration has its parallel target copy. The new criterion applies at the displayed boundary, and its reverse is empty by the count of \(U\). The two occurrences of the birth cannot be separated merely by first choosing a different crossing copy of \(P\): the neighbouring \(L\) forces the left port and both excursions return to the identical empty slot there.

**Validation.** The checker constructs the two updates and their actual fixed-factor whiskerings at both candidate ports. For all 63 words in the two probes of length at most five, the only admitted sequence of middle ports is the word itself. Only 11 of these words admit a fixed birth choice. It builds the 4-witness through both excursions at the written faces and verifies literal folding to the original witness. In ranks two through five it also numbers three uses of the same variable attached at an identity, reads the three distinct names on both temporal bracketings, and checks the raw associativity comparison and its literal fold. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_named_lifts.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/NamedLifts.agda`. Agda verifies the raw terms in the Gray kernel; the lifting and reflection statements use the arity and conversion arguments above. [Named lift checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_named_lifts.py)

## Researcher Response 58

**One free 3-scalar beside an arbitrarily long gate-dependent chain of point sites has at most one potential 2-cell relation in its probe fibre. Its attaching word decides asphericity.** Each chain rule may replace any closed planar 2-diagram by another at an empty spatial path. Assume that each consecutive pair shares an actual generating 2-gate, and prescribe the named history with its positioned seam paths as in [Response 52](#researcher-response-52). If the first input or the last output is nonempty, all raw boundaries there of degree six and higher fill, hence all ten individual ordinary surfaces reflect. Even when both ends are empty, a nontrivial attaching word which is not a proper power suffices. A full twist at a seam can change the answer while fixing all the incidence names and written ports.

### Sweeping the probe through the chain

Forget the distinguished scalar use. The fixed-end coefficient of the remaining chain is a 1-type by Response 52. Over a chosen point of this base the schedules for the probe are its serial placements in the gaps of the chain and its ties with a single site. They form a subdivided interval, since no two chain sites can tie. At a gap the fibre coefficient is the space of positions of a probe point in the corresponding planar diagram. At a tie it is the space of such positions with the other site marked. Its two maps are the point-position maps under input and output insertion. Indeed in the gap the unmarked interface path factors at the extra probe-only batch. After forgetting its point the space of intermediate objects and path factorizations of this fixed path is contractible. At an outer gap use the prescribed end path in the same way. This identifies the forgetful fibres at all vertices and ties, compatibly with refinement and transport along the given seams. Taking their homotopy colimit computes the fibre, as homotopy colimits of spaces over a groupoid are fibrewise. The long exact homotopy sequence now identifies every based homotopy group in degrees at least two of the total space with that of the corresponding fibre component. This last step allows loops in the forgotten chain. [Single-site point-position maps](#researcher-response-56)

Sweep from the initial gap to the final one. At each gap take one punctured plane per face bearing the probe's object. At a site, all joint faces except its containing face map equivalently to their corresponding outside faces on both sides. In the containing face the maps fix the other hole meridians and send the site meridian to the product about the outermost children of the inserted port. Bounded faces internal to an input port finish at that gap; those internal to an output port start there. A seam transports the faces and their based meridian frames by its actual path. In particular distinct continuing faces never merge.

If the output port is nonempty and the site face admits the probe, the map from that joint face to the new exterior face is inclusion of a free factor up to homotopy. One may use the product of the new child meridians in place of the first new meridian in a free basis, retaining the other new meridians and all the spectators. Thus the next step wedges on one circle for each new exterior child beyond the first, irrespective of the map to the accumulated old space. On the other continuing faces it does nothing up to equivalence; newly available bounded faces enter as separate graphs. This also proves the graph conclusion when the site's object excludes the probe. Each nonfinal output is nonempty by gate dependence, so until a possible empty final output all accumulated components are graphs. If the first input is nonempty the reverse sweep has nonempty ports in its sweep direction throughout, proving the same conclusion even for an empty final output.

At a final empty output the map from the joint exterior face to the last gap fills just its site puncture. Its mapping cylinder relative to the joint face therefore attaches one disk along the site meridian, up to homotopy. When this face admits the probe, let \(W\) be the image of the *input* packet's peripheral word in the accumulated free group. The resulting component is a rose with a single disk attached along \(W\). Other components remain graphs. If \(W=1\), this gives a wedge with a 2-sphere and hence raw degree-six obstructions. For a nontrivial cyclically reduced word that is not a proper power, Lyndon asphericity gives the vanishing of every higher homotopy group. The word test is exact for this presentation complex. If a nontrivial proper power occurs, the maximal root has finite order greater than one in the one-relator group, which precludes a finite-dimensional aspherical complex (restrict the cellular resolution to that finite cyclic subgroup). A simply connected 2-complex with zero second homotopy is contractible by cellular homology and Hurewicz, so failure of asphericity here already gives second homotopy. This criterion does not require a realizability assertion about proper powers. [Louder and Wilton, §5.2, on Lyndon asphericity](https://discovery.ucl.ac.uk/id/eprint/10131842/1/Louder_Negative%20immersions%20for%20one-relator%20groups_AAM.pdf) · [Karrass, Magnus and Solitar on the order of a relator root](https://doi.org/10.1002/cpa.3160130107)

### The word at a written seam

In the positive access comb of Response 56, let \((a_1,\ldots,a_m)\) be the input child meridians expressed in the accumulated group. A nonempty output of size \(n\) introduces \(n-1\) new free generators and determines its first meridian by \(b_n\cdots b_1=a_m\cdots a_1\). Spectators keep their words. A positive adjacent seam crossing changes the ordered pair \((x,y)\) to \((x^{-1}yx,x)\); the negative crossing uses the inverse change. This is pullback of a loop at the *next* reading along the seam. It follows by conjugating the probe's two actual meridians with the adjacent half twist and preserves the reversed peripheral product. For a planar child containing many gates restrict the positioned seam braid to one gate of each continuing child in its face, transporting those same choices to the other endpoint. At a grouped reading this is the sibling braid of [Response 44](#researcher-response-44). Cyclic reduction and testing periods of the resulting finite terminal word decide the criterion.

For example, start beside a stationary lens. Create a lens with an inner point, replace it by a new lens with an inner point and a separate point, then consume both. Take the probe outside. At the split the new branch meridians are \(b^{-1},b\), and the stationary meridian is \(a\). With straight seams the final word is empty and the exterior fibre is a two-circle rose wedged with a sphere. A full twist of the separate point about the stationary lens at the second seam gives \(b^{-1}a^{-1}ba\). The exterior fibre then has the torus presentation and is aspherical. Thus a calculation from unsigned gate incidence or from the abelianized terminal word alone would give these histories the same answer. This distinguishes raw obstructions; the sphere case still requires a surface test.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_chain_probe.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/ChainProbe.agda`. The solver checks positioned gate incidence, replays the signed seams and reads the exterior child braids before computing the terminal relation. It verified 255 comb transports by conjugating actual four-strand probe loops. In 85 closed lens chains with the same ports and up to three signed full twists, 25 words vanished and 60 were nonempty nonpowers. Independently it computed the terminal peripheral braid before imposing the birth equation and recovered each reported word. Agda checks the closed chain with a probe and stationary lens in the raw Gray kernel. The JSON solver handles the exterior face on an empty spatial path with grouped child readings at seam endpoints; the face-by-face argument above also covers bounded faces. [Chain probe solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_chain_probe.py)

## Researcher Response 59

**In degree five and above the two published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange reflect on every probe-and-chain history of Response 58, including its sphere cases.** For each of the four remaining individual binary surfaces, reflection in a given degree on this class is equivalent to reflection on the scalar-rule subclass in that degree. Here a genuine 3-scalar is distinguished as the probe, and the other uses are point-site changes of arbitrary closed planar diagrams, totally ordered by generating-gate dependence. The result concerns supported histories on ordinary 3-computads, with unrestricted stationary lower diagrams and arbitrary seam paths. It follows by proving that flattening *all* planar gates detects the based raw homotopy groups in positive dimensions.

Use the literal flattening of [Response 52](#researcher-response-52). Each ordinary 2-generator becomes a scalar at the point, with the point-only comparisons at its actual spatial poles, and each declared 3-rule has a rule at its flattened ports. Flat charts have a point per gate and a point per active site. The original shared gate between every two consecutive chain sites still exists in the flat history. Name uses and gates in these fixed-end components. This is compatible with flattening, including for two uses of the same generator. A loop permuting names can be null after flattening only if its permutation was trivial, as the named covers have the same occurrence transport. For a named loop or a higher sphere one can therefore work upstairs.

### A left inverse on each component of the probe fibre

Forget the probe as in [Response 58](#researcher-response-58), and fix an unmarked history, including the seam paths. Consider the map of its fibre to the fibre at the flat history. The flat fibre is the interval diagram of punctured planes with every gate treated as a hole. We construct a homotopy left inverse on **each chosen source component**. This is a construction on the fibre at the given history. Only the initial all-gate flattening needs to be a computad substitution applicable to surface witnesses.

In the original interval diagram a component follows a single probe face over an interval of consecutive gaps. At a point insertion there is a bijection on the continuing faces, since the entire port occupies a disk in the containing face. The input's internal bounded faces can end and the output's internal bounded faces can start, each separately. Seams carry faces by isomorphisms. This proves the interval description, including a face available at just one gap.

For a face available throughout, keep one named gate of each directly enclosed floating child. Choose it on the child's connected wire-and-gate component. A rule whose site is a point cannot consume part of such a connected component: the port has no incident wires to its exterior. Thus a child's gate can be transported until the entire child is replaced. Uses in a bounded face of the child leave this gate in place. At a joint chart keep the site as a puncture exactly when the use acts in the chosen face. Forget every other puncture from the flat probe plane. At each gap or tie the composite from the original probe face is an equivalence onto the plane with these retained punctures. In fact its free meridians go once about the respective children; this is the one-point configuration fibre equivalence of [Response 44](#researcher-response-44). For a use in this face, the retained site expands to the representatives of the direct children of its port; for any other use it and both its port lists have no retained punctures. Restriction therefore commutes with the insertion maps, up to the disk choices. It commutes with seam transport by following the same gates in the seam braid. These equivalences compare the two interval diagrams on the chosen component.

Suppose instead that the face starts or ends inside the chain. It has an enclosing floating connected component, introduced or consumed as part of a closed port. Choose **one more gate**, on this enclosing component itself, distinct from every direct-child representative. This gate persists throughout the life of the face, also when sites act in other faces of the same enclosing component. On the life interval project the flat plane to the **sphere** punctured at this extra gate and at the chosen representatives: forget the other gates, then include the plane in its one-point compactification. The extra puncture plays the role of the exterior of the original face. More explicitly, a compact disk core of the original face with disks about its direct children omitted has the corresponding child puncture in each inner complementary disk, and the enclosing gate on the exterior side. One can take the boundaries off the gate and wire drawings. The inclusion into this punctured sphere is a homotopy equivalence, since each complementary component now has just one puncture. Equivalently its child meridians freely generate, with the remaining puncture meridian determined by the total peripheral relation. The earlier naturality argument applies within the life interval; the enclosing gate is transported too.

At an end of this interval *inside the chain*, all the selected punctures on the available side, including the enclosing gate, belong to the single appearing or disappearing port. Extend the projection over the rest of the flat interval as a constant at that endpoint. For the boundary tie this requires a nullhomotopy of the map from its flat probe plane to the punctured sphere at the first or last available gap. All spectator meridians map trivially. The site meridian goes about the **entire set** of selected punctures in one disk. In the sphere this loop bounds the other disk. Since the joint probe plane retracts onto a rose on those meridians, this gives the required nullhomotopy on the entire coefficient. There are no further coherence conditions between adjacent edges of an interval. Perform this construction independently on both sides when necessary.

Taking the homotopy colimit now gives a map from the full flat fibre to the colimit of the retained punctured spaces over the life interval. Its composite on the original component is an equivalence by the objectwise comparisons above. Inverting this equivalence gives the asserted left inverse. It applies to loops as well as spheres, with all based homotopy groups transported along the chosen identification of basepoints. In particular it detects linear combinations of sphere classes based along different loops in the rose of Response 58. Merely counting the spherical summand would not suffice for that conclusion.

### Passing back to the old boundary

The two unmarked chain bases are 1-types. Their map is injective on fundamental groups: a compatible tuple is detected on its first site by Response 52, and flattening that site is the faithful positioned gate braid reading. For a loop upstairs that becomes null in the flat total space, its base loop is therefore trivial. The loop comes from the original fibre; its image in the flat fibre must be trivial since the flat base has zero second homotopy. The left inverse forces the original loop to be trivial. In higher degrees the two long exact sequences identify the total groups directly with the corresponding fibre groups. This proves detection in all positive based degrees. It does not require distinct probe faces to land in distinct flat components.

A surface filler at an old degree-five-or-higher boundary flattens to one at the flat boundary. For the published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange, the scalar-rule reflection theorem gives a raw filler there, forcing the original difference class to vanish by detection. These six surfaces therefore reflect even at the straight-seam sphere of Response 58. For any other individual surface the same argument needs only reflection at the corresponding scalar-rule probe-and-chain tests. Conversely those tests belong to the given class. [All-port scalar reflection and its subsurfaces](#researcher-response-48)

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_probe_retraction.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/ProbeRetraction.agda`. In a bounded face it imposes the total peripheral relation using the enclosing puncture. It checks 318 positioned lifts at an interior change turning a point into a lens and a separate point. All six boundary projections for the two enclosing-gate choices and the port representatives kill the full packet meridian; the corresponding plane projection to just the children detects a nonempty word in each case. The checker also sweeps the flattened open/change/close example of a face available only in the middle, verifies its terminal relation under the map to the three interior meridians, and checks a child motion transported around an exterior spectator. Agda verifies the substitution on a probe at the actual inner object of a lens with different inner and outer objects, including its spatial unit comparisons. **For Q1(a), every proposed surface inhabitant has a finite universal occurrence computad, even when generators have composite or identity attaching faces in arbitrarily many consecutive degrees.** The old query lifts to a literal raw type on that computad. There is also a reduction which keeps the lower skeleton fixed: in a counterexample one can require each output-degree generator to occur once and each generator one degree below to have a unique place of production and consumption. ### Unique splitting at a syntactic value

Write \(H\) for raw syntax and \(L\) for surface classes, and let \(O\) denote either. Computad maps here send each generator to a generator and preserve its written raw attaching type. For such a map \(f:Y\to X\), a globular operation \(o\) over its ordinary positional tree \(P\), and a globular labelling \(\sigma:P\to O(X)\), evaluation induces a bijection
\[
 \{\rho:P\to O(Y)\mid f\rho=\sigma\}
 \longrightarrow \{v\in O(Y)\mid fv=o(\sigma)\}.
 \tag{1}
\]
Also, the preimages of the class of a generator in its own degree are precisely its generating copies. Here equality on the right of (1) is equality *in the indicated syntax*. At identity attachments the corresponding claim about lifts of ordinary strict factorizations can fail. [Strict factor lifting and its limitation](#researcher-response-53)

We use the globular-operad description on globular inputs. Each operation lies over the total of its positional tree, possibly padded by identities. Its boundary operations are over the signed boundary trees. Multiplication substitutes slot trees and identifies their common face trees. This description includes operations with conversion on the bound arity. In a free algebra on a computad, after fixing its lower degrees, expressions in degree \(j\) are generators and applications of these operations to compatible tuples. Their presentation uses operad equality, units, substitution and congruence. Attaching a generating disc prescribes its two faces. Induct on the degree \(j\). In degree zero an object lifts by choosing an object over it. Work first with a map for which each degree-\(j\) generator has at most one copy at any given lifted source *class* in degree \(j-1\). There is a partial evaluation of a downstairs expression from a specified lift of its source. For a variable, use that copy if present. For \(o(\sigma)\), split the lifted source over the source operation of \(o\) by (1) in the preceding degree. This labels the negative boundary of \(P\). At each stack of \(j\)-globes in \(P\), start at the resulting initial face and evaluate its arguments successively, assigning each lifted target to the next source. Shorter branches have already received their labels on the negative boundary. Different stacks meet only below their moving faces. The resulting tuple is globular, and applying \(o\) gives the answer. If \(P\) has height below \(j\), the source split already supplies the entire tuple.

For precision, this partial evaluation respects *both* definedness and value in the presentation just given. For substitution, compare an outer operation applied to inner operations with their operadic composite on the substituted tree. Successful evaluation in stages supplies labellings of the inner trees. Where they meet, their lifted boundary values agree by incidence of the outer tuple. Those boundaries evaluate the same boundary operation on the same downstairs labels, so the lower-degree uniqueness in (1) identifies the boundary labellings. They glue, including when the same smaller tree serves as both faces of a padded slot. In the other direction, restrict a successful labelling on the substituted tree to each inner tree. Its input boundary is the unique split of its evaluated source, and along each stack the argument evaluations start at exactly their prescribed faces. This recovers each restricted labelling in stages and hence the outer tuple. The two final values agree by multiplication. A unit disc just reads its slot. Equal operad elements have the same positional tree, boundary elements and evaluation on any tuple. Congruence in an argument follows by running it from the same lifted source. This proves invariance under all the presentation relations.

Evaluating the projected expression of an upstairs cell from its own source recovers that cell: this follows by induction on the expression using uniqueness on its negative boundary and then the indicated argument lifts along the stacks. Consequently an upstairs value above \(o(\sigma)\) makes the specified downstairs expression succeed, yielding a tuple above \(\sigma\). Any candidate tuple is recovered by the same boundary split and successive evaluations, so it is unique. Applying the variable case gives the generator-preimage assertion. This proves the induction step under the restriction on copies.

To dispense with the restriction, use the parallel renaming calculation of Response 57. With the lower skeleton unchanged, a class has a unique lift at any prescribed ordered list of names for its top generating occurrences. This applies to raw syntax as well. In degree one its proof reads the edge occurrences of a word; the unit contributes the empty list. The order used for a general operation is the order of its top positions *in that operation*. Each such position occurs once by its ordinary positional value. Thus evaluating at a tuple concatenates the occurrence lists of its top arguments in this order. Given \(v\) on the right of (1), give its degree-\(j\) occurrences distinct names, recording the actual upper generator at each. Form \(Y^\#\) from the lower skeleton of \(Y\) with one generator per name at its recorded raw attachment. Form \(X^\#\) the same way with the projected attachments. The map between them now has just one possible copy per name. The two maps forgetting names are parallel renamings. Lift \(v\) at its ordered list of names. Partition that list into the consecutive blocks for the top arguments of \(o(\sigma)\), including empty blocks. Rename those arguments in \(X^\#\), keeping all lower labels. By uniqueness of parallel renaming the resulting evaluation is the projection of the named lift of \(v\). Apply the restricted case of (1) and forget names to obtain a tuple over \(\sigma\). Two candidate tuples in \(Y\) have the same upper occurrence lists in these blocks, since their evaluations are both \(v\). Each can therefore be renamed in the *same* \(Y^\#\), giving the same named evaluation and the same projected named tuple by parallel renaming. Restricted uniqueness identifies them, including on stationary slots. For a generator use its singleton list and the restricted generator case. ### A finite representing diagram and a literal lifted query

For every such map the raw-to-surface comparison gives a pullback of globular sets
\[
 H(Y)\ \cong\ H(X)\times_{L(X)} L(Y).
 \tag{2}
\]
Indeed take a raw expression downstairs and a lift of its surface class. At a variable the preceding generator calculation supplies the unique raw copy (different copies have different singleton lists). Use induction first on degree and then on expression size for arguments in the same degree. The tuple satisfies the *literal* incidence equations: its actual raw faces and the indicated specializations of the raw attaching expressions have the same raw projection and the same lifted surface class. Uniqueness in lower degree identifies them. Applying the original raw head constructs the lift. Any other raw lift folds to that very expression, so has the same head, the same split surface tuple by (1), and the same raw arguments by induction. This proves (2).

For a fixed \(v\in L(X)\), consider the functor on raw computads over \(X\) assigning to \(Y\) its set of lifts of \(v\). It is represented by a finite computad \(D_v\to X\) and a distinguished surface cell there. Here are the construction and finiteness argument. First do the raw version using (1) for \(H\). A variable in degree zero is represented by one vertex. For a variable of higher degree, take the representing diagrams for its written source and target. Identify their corresponding source diagrams and their corresponding target diagrams one degree lower, via the two boundary maps (no identifications are required between endpoints of a 1-generator). For an operation application take the colimit of the representing diagrams of its labels over the *whole globular incidence diagram* of its tree. A map out is exactly a compatible tuple of lifts and (1) gives the desired bijection. Induction on degree and then expression size suffices. The finite colimits used here identify the specified generator names in a disjoint union rank by rank; the images of their written raw attaching terms agree by the attaching-map condition. This construction permits a boundary map to identify positions.

For \(L\) use the same diagram for each variable by the generator-preimage assertion. Use the same colimit construction for an operation application, now invoking (1) for \(L\). The identity map out of the resulting diagram supplies the universal cell. The representing property proves independence of the chosen expression up to unique isomorphism preserving that cell over \(X\). Formula (2) identifies the diagram of a raw term with that of its surface class, with the indicated universal elements corresponding. In particular, if \(v\) inhabits the surface conversion of an old type \(s\to_A t\), lift \(s,t\) by (2) at the faces of the universal element. A counterexample therefore lifts to a counterexample of this form. Conversely each of these diagrams is a finite computad and so is itself in the scope of Q1(a). No asphericity or existence of a raw filler on the representing diagram follows merely from representability.

### A reduction retaining the lower skeleton

More concretely let the output degree be \(h+1\), \(h\ge2\). Keep the entire skeleton through \(h-1\). In a written surface witness, name the \(h\)-occurrences of its old source distinctly. At an operation split its named source over its negative boundary by (1) for the parallel renaming in degree \(h\). The lists at the initial \(h\)-slots partition all current names, since the source operation uses each such position once. Process each stack in order, using globally fresh names for outputs; shorter branches carry their assigned names forward. The common incidences below degree \(h\) stay fixed. The total target uses each terminal slot once. This proves recursively that the resulting live lists are disjoint, and that each consumed name disappears permanently. The final list renames the old written target by (2). Its lower boundary is the original one. Folding sends the entire query and witness back to the original ones.

Thus each output-degree generator occurs exactly once in the displayed witness. Every \(h\)-name occurs once at its production (initial old source or a generating target) and once at its consumption (a later generating source or final old target). For a fixed bound on their number of uses there are finitely many of these two-rank presentations up to names over a fixed finite input and query: choose the ordered sequence of generator labels, choose the ordered distinct live inputs for each written source, assign canonical fresh names to its written target, and choose a matching of the final live names to the old target. ## Researcher Response 61

**The fixed-history fibre of any number of free scalar probes beside a gate-dependent chain is an ordered configuration space in an open 3-manifold.** The chain sites can have arbitrary spatial wire ports and arbitrary planar 2-faces. Two named probes in the same connected component give a raw degree-six obstruction. If they are all in separate components, the higher obstruction groups are exactly the products of the one-probe groups in those components. The relevant component records which positions can connect *through time*: several distinct faces of a single cut can belong to it.

### The space of probe positions through time

Fix the named unmarked history, its fixed ends and its seam paths. Its coefficient is a 1-type by [Response 52](#researcher-response-52). Choose distinct interior times for the uses in their forced order. Draw each cut as a planar wire-and-gate diagram in an open rectangle, with the boundary wires running to its ends. Between sites use the given positioned planar motions, including the signed exchanges, to transport the drawing. Near a site use a small closed disk with the prescribed boundary wires on its rim. On the preceding and following sides draw its two ports inside this disk with the same exterior. At the time of the change occupy the **entire closed disk**. Define \(M\) in the product of the open time interval and the rectangle by omitting the drawn wires and gates at regular times and also this disk at each critical time. Using piecewise linear drawings constant in a collar on each side of a change makes the obstacle relatively closed. Thus \(M\) is an open subset of three-space. Each component has a consistent object label, since the diagrams on both sides of a disk agree on its exterior.

Over the fixed history the fibre is the product, by occupied components \(C\) of \(M\), of \(\operatorname{Conf}_{S_C}(C)\), where \(S_C\) contains the probes assigned there. To verify the formula, order the probe times weakly relative to one another and to the fixed site times. The admissible orders are exactly the refinements and ties of the named probe schedules. In a gap, after transporting the unmarked diagram along its chosen seam, each equal-time block is a configuration of distinct planar points away from its wires and gates. Distinct time blocks impose independent conditions. Indeed the choices of subdivisions of the unmarked interface path at these probe-only batches form a contractible space, as in [Response 58](#researcher-response-58). At a site, a tied block occupies the faces of the marked gate. Replacing its drawn star with the closed disk gives an equivalent configuration space: in each sector of the star push a collar out to the rim, keeping incident wire ends in their order. For a point site use an annular collar. The same isotopy of the open faces works on any finite tuple of distinct points. The map to either adjacent gap is then the inclusion of configurations outside the disk in the chosen port drawing. This represents actual port insertion. Points in an internal face of a port have positions only on its own side.

These coefficient equivalences identify the gluing maps as well. Separating a time block simply relaxes distinctness between the two new blocks; moving off a site uses the preceding disk inclusion. For a direct realization, trivialize the drawings over each regular time gap and use the constant port drawings in the two end collars. Subdivide the products of position rectangles compatibly with the drawings, disks and all pair diagonals, taking common subdivisions at the collars. Over an open face of the time-order subdivision the admitted position cells are precisely the preceding coefficient. A refinement admits each cell from its coarser time face by the indicated inclusion; time transport between the collars uses the seam motion. Barycentric subdivision of the allowed product faces computes the complement, by discarding the barycentric weights of forbidden faces and renormalizing. It also computes the homotopy colimit of these coefficient diagrams, by subdivision along the flags of time faces. One can subdivide a regular gap further at the elementary exchanges, where planar isotopy identifies the coefficients on the two sides. This proves the formula for the actual schedule fibre. The same construction commutes with forgetting any named probe. [Time-face realization for stationary diagrams](#researcher-response-44)

### Reading higher obstructions

The long exact sequence over the unmarked 1-type identifies all based groups of degree at least two with those of the fibre. If two probes lie in one component, put them close together in a small ball disjoint from the other probes there, hold the first still and move the second on a small sphere about it. Such a base position can be reached by a path of the configuration. Project to these two coordinates and normalize their difference vector in three-space. The resulting map on this sphere has degree one. Thus the class is nonzero even in the full total space. This produces an unfilled raw relative globe; a surface filling is a separate question. With at most one probe per component the fibre is a product of the corresponding open 3-manifolds themselves.

There is an effective sufficient test for the latter factors, including non-point sites. Build the graph with one vertex per planar face at each gap and one edge per joint face at each site, joining its two expanded faces and transporting them along seams. Its connected components are those of \(M\). The vertex and edge spaces of this graph of spaces are punctured planes. A face map is injective on fundamental groups unless the site is itself a directly enclosed point child in that joint face and the chosen port is empty. Indeed choose one gate on the connected core of each directly enclosed floating child. If the marker lies on a core with incident wires, that core has another gate: a progressive closed component with just one gate has no incident wires. Choose representatives outside the site disk. The map from the expanded face to the plane punctured at these gates detects all the original child meridians, even if two joint faces now map to the same cut face. For a point child and nonempty port choose a gate of its port instead. In the exceptional empty case the site meridian dies. Thus where all incident maps in an occupied component inject, its graph of groups has a tree of contractible universal covers of the vertex and edge spaces as development. The component is aspherical. If every constituent face there is simply connected, the space is exactly the incidence graph up to homotopy. Together with the collision test this proves reflection for all ten individual surfaces in degrees six and above at the resulting aspherical allocations.

For example, use two parallel spatial wires, create a gate joining both wires and then consume that same gate. The probe's middle gap before the creation and after the consumption is connected. At the intermediate cut it has distinct faces below and above the two-wire gate. Both paths around the gate reconnect, giving a circle in the incidence graph. The two outer strips are separate contractible components. In middle-strip coordinates the obstacle can be represented by a crossbar spanning the width at one drawing height for one closed time interval: the middle complement is an interval times a plane minus a segment. The disk collar model gives the same type. With one probe in each of the three components all higher raw obstructions vanish. With two in the middle there is a collision sphere, even if one probe starts in the upper intermediate face and the other in the lower one.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_probe_movies.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/ProbeMovies.agda`. The JSON solver constructs the cut and tie faces from positioned gate and wire incidence, transports them across the signed seams, counts direct holes and tests allocations. It finds the middle cycle and the two outer trees, and verifies transport across an extra spectator. Independently, it builds the coordinate-order cells of the actual open configuration complement, computes relative cellular boundary matrices over the field of two elements and checks that their squares vanish. Compact-support duality gives middle-strip Betti numbers \((1,1)\) for one probe and \((1,2,2,1)\) for two, with zeros thereafter. For three probes in an open ball the ranks in degrees zero, two and four are \(1,3,2\). Agda checks the supported two-use chain with two occurrences of a probe whiskered into its middle spatial gap. [Spacetime configuration solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_probe_movies.py)

## Researcher Response 62

**In Q3(a) a finite search can certify reflection even when the fourth-degree rules can be used arbitrarily often and a cut chosen on either face of a rule can fail to extend.** Start with the occurrence diagram of the *old source*. Adjoin a generator diagram along a map from its written source diagram, and keep the image of the old source at each step. If each fourth-degree generator diagram gets all its vertices from its source diagram, there are only finitely many states after identifying parallel copies as specified below. This also works when only the total number of vertices introduced on a successful computation is bounded. For Q1(a) the construction gives an exact finite disjunction of old queries in any degree under the same bound, independently of the cut theorem. It applies to every admissible \(E\), either coherence rule, and \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\).

### How the diagram grows from one face

Work over an input \(X\) with maps of computads that send variables to variables at their literal raw types. Write \(D_e\) for the finite diagram representing lifts of a cell \(e\), either raw or surface. An old term and its surface class have canonically the same diagram by the raw-to-surface pullback. Write \(s g,t g\) for the two *written terms* in the declaration of a generator \(g\). Taking its source gives a map \(i_g:D_{s g}\to D_g\) over \(X\). Given a written expression for an \(n\)-cell \(v\), \(n\ge2\), there is a sequence starting at \(D_{s v}\) and ending at \(D_v\), with one pushout
\[
                    C' = C\amalg_{D_{s g}}D_g
\]
for each occurrence of an ambient \(n\)-generator \(g\) in the computation. The entrance map on the right is \(i_g\). All other steps only evaluate operations on already lifted data. There is a dual sequence from \(D_{t v}\) using written targets. These are claims about the actual maps of occurrence diagrams, including maps identifying positions. [Unique splitting and representability](#researcher-response-60)

To prove this, give a lift of the source to an arbitrary computad \(Y\) over \(X\). At an operation, split the source over its boundary operation by (1) of Response 60. This supplies the labels of the negative boundary of the positional tree. Follow each stack to its terminal face, using the preceding target as the next source. Fix any order between stacks, which already agree on their lower incidences. A shorter branch carries its given label through. Recursively process each top argument; an operation with no top slots requires no further choice. At a generating occurrence the current lifted source requires precisely a copy of \(g\) at that source. Indeed the preimages of a generating cell are its generating copies. For the surface syntax the current class over the written \(s g\) has a unique literal lift of that raw term by (2) of Response 60. Thus the condition on a copy imposes exactly the indicated source map even at a compound attachment.

At each stage the partial histories in \(Y\) are represented by maps \(C\to Y\). Their current source at the next occurrence is natural in \(Y\), so evaluation at the identity of \(C\) specifies its map \(D_{s g}\to C\). Extending such a history by a copy of \(g\) is represented by the displayed pushout, by its universal property. After all stacks are evaluated, (1) identifies the completed histories bijectively with lifts of the specified expression: a lift determines all argument labels and recursive choices uniquely, and a compatible run evaluates to that lift. This proves the assertion for either syntax. The target version splits positive boundaries and processes stacks from their last face. In particular, for a cell with no generating occurrence in its own degree, **both boundary maps to its diagram are isomorphisms**. Such a cell transports lifts of its entire source diagram bijectively to lifts of its entire target diagram, naturally over \(X\). If two such parallel cells are joined by a cell with no generating occurrence in that higher degree, their transports agree: the two boundary isomorphisms into the joining diagram commute with both lower face maps by globularity.

For a fixed bound on the number of top uses, enumerate all sequences of that length or less, taking *every* computad map \(D_{s g}\to C\) over \(X\) as a possible entrance. The sequence above occurs in this enumeration. Per step the number of new generators of rank \(j\) is at most \( |(D_g)_j\setminus i_g((D_{s g})_j)|\), where the subscript counts generators. Existing ones can become equal in the pushout. For instance the source of a declaration \(f\to1_x\) at a loop has two occurrences of \(x\) on its universal edge. They map to one vertex of the declaration diagram. Attaching it to a path identifies the two ends of its selected edge. The old-source map into the next diagram therefore has to be updated before computing subsequent matches.

### Finite states with unboundedly many top uses

For an old \(n\)-query \(s\to_A t\) let \(b\) bound the number of vertices of every intermediate diagram in the source construction for a successful witness. One checkable choice in either syntax is
\[
 b=|(D_s)_0|+\sup_m\sum_g m_g\, |(D_g)_0\setminus i_g((D_{s g})_0)|,
 \qquad
 \sum_g m_g\big(N(tg)-N(sg)\big)=N(t)-N(s).
\]
Here \(m_g\) are nonnegative integers and \(N\) counts ambient generators in degree \(n-1\). The balance equation follows by applying ordinary strict projection and taking generating counts on both faces. Internal faces cancel and each top input occurs once. Infeasibility gives emptiness in both theories. Any finite upper bound on the displayed linear objective suffices. In particular rules surjective on vertices may run without any bound on their multiplicities. The prefix bound is valid even when identifications occur later, because each summand bounds the *gross* addition of vertices. One can run the dual test from \(t\).

Keep every vertex of a reached diagram distinct. In ascending degree identify generators with the same name over \(X\) whose two written attachments are literally equal after the lower identifications. This gives a quotient computad over \(X\) and sends the distinguished old source lift to a raw source lift there. Use this quotient initially and after each pushout. A state consists of this computad over \(X\) and the distinguished raw lift. Cap its vertex count at \(b\) and close under all entrance maps and generator choices. There are finitely many states up to renumbering. Given the finitely many possible lower generators, each written attaching expression has finitely many literal lifts: at a head keep its written head and enumerate the lifts of its arguments, and at a variable try each generating copy. Thus each original generator has finitely many pairs of possible lifted faces, with at most one copy per resulting written type in the quotient. Induction up to rank \(n\) gives a finite bound. The same argument bounds the choices of distinguished lift of \(s\). The finite diagrams, maps and quotients here require only raw syntax on the given terms. In particular finding states does not require deciding conversion on arbitrary presentations.

In every state enumerate all raw lifts of \(t\) literally parallel to the distinguished lift of \(s\), giving a finite family of old queries. **Downstairs inhabitation is equivalent to inhabitation of at least one family member, separately in each theory.** To prove the forward implication, follow the source construction of the given witness. Inductively map its actual intermediate diagram to a reached state. Compose the next entrance with that map; the two pushout maps give a map from the actual next diagram to the computed pushout. Its quotient again receives the actual diagram. The vertex cap does not obstruct this run: at each step the actual intermediate diagram maps *surjectively* onto the computed state on generators, initially by the quotient. A pushout on the same generator diagram preserves this surjectivity, since every new generator comes from one of its two inputs. At the final state send the universal witness along this map, with the two literal old boundary lifts given by (2) of Response 60. Conversely any witness at any enumerated pair folds, including at a state reached by entrance choices which would not have replayed a particular downstairs expression. This converse is the reason no test of syntactic realizability of the state history is needed. For comparison, the strict occurrence results used for source-positive polygraphs rely on the combinatorics of the free strict category. Here splitting is at the actual raw or surface cell. In degree four apply the graph cut test of Response 49 to each state with at least one enumerated pair. All pairs in successful cut states reflect. If every such state passes, this proves reflection at the original boundary, including when the initial presentation has only one object. In the four effective settings one also obtains individual decidability when each required cut test has a positive weight on the 3-generators for which every 4-change weakly increases weighted count, or every one weakly decreases it. In the increasing case the target weight divided by the smallest weight bounds the number of generating 3-events in every history on a successful route, and in the decreasing case use the source weight. Native switches preserve this weight. The other two bounds of Response 49 now bound all local path lengths and exterior indices, so the finite reachability test there applies. Different states may use different cuts and weights. [Cut reflection and the finite history calculation](#researcher-response-49)

### A rule with incompatible endpoint choices

Use one object, one loop \(f\), and \(H:ff\to ff\). Fix \(P=(ff)f\). Let \(L\) be \(H\) on the first two edges, and \(R\) be \(H\) on the last two edges, using the raw associator and its reverse to put that gate at the same written poles \(P,P\). For each \(J=L,R\) add a separate 3-birth \(1_P\to J\) and death \(J\to1_P\); let \(B_J\) be their temporal composite. Write the position of a cut as the index of the unique edge of the three-edge path that crosses it. On the source diagram of \(B_L\to B_R\) the possible positions are 1 and 2, on its target they are 2 and 3, and on its entire declaration only 2 works. A source cut at 1 cannot extend through the declaration, and a target cut at 3 cannot extend backwards. The universal extension tests in the two directions in Response 57 therefore both fail on this rule. Starting instead from two copies of \(B_L\) and taking the two required uses of the rule gives four pushout histories when arbitrary entrance maps are allowed. All four resulting diagrams admit the middle cut. At the history selecting the first source occurrence and then the second, the pushout diagram is isomorphic over the input to the literal occurrence diagram of the raw two-step witness.

For an unbounded example use the two rules \(p:B_L\to B_R;B_R\) and \(q:B_R;B_R\to B_L\). Add a unary \(a:f\to f\) and a birth and death for the gate on the *middle* edge alone, giving an excursion \(M\) at \(1_P\). Start at \(M;B_L\).
