The composite of the placed \(p,q\) can repeat arbitrarily often, and one direction grows the number of 3-events while the other shrinks it. Both declaration source maps cover every vertex of their diagrams. From this source the quotient search has three states: the initial one, the one with the expansion, and the one with both changes. In each state the three edges form the same line. The left two-edge gate, right two-edge gate when present, and middle unary gate all cross its middle cut. More explicitly each entrance for an available rule must place its full three-edge path on that line, fixing the placement of every gate. After the first expansion both ports of the expansion and contraction are present; identifying equal labelled attachments leaves at most one copy of each rule. This accounts for all five transitions, including repeats. It proves reflection for **every written parallel target** from this source. Weight two on each left birth or death, one on each right birth or death, and one on each middle birth or death is positive and conserved. Thus the individual old queries from this source are decidable in the four effective settings. The argument uses only the unary gate at its actual middle position, although the input loop permits that gate on any of the three edges.

**Validation.** The checker builds the raw occurrence diagrams, enumerates attaching maps, computes the quotient types after each pushout and tests every vertex colouring. It verifies both vertex identifications on a two-edge path in the \(f\to1_x\) example, the four bounded histories and the isomorphism at the specified witness. In the expansion/contraction example it exhausts the state graph and verifies that exactly two of its states admit a parallel raw lift of the target \(M;(B_R;B_R)\). Agda checks the raw two-use witness and its literal fold, as well as the repeatable expansion/contraction composite, in the Gray kernel. The all-\(E\) reflection statement uses the splitting and cut theorems above. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_occurrence_attachments.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/OccurrenceAttachments.agda`. [Occurrence attachment checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_occurrence_attachments.py)

## Researcher Response 63

**All six reflection conclusions of Response 59 hold with arbitrarily many free scalar probes. For each of the four binary surfaces containing interchange, degree \(n\ge5\) on this class reduces to scalar-rule chains with at most \(n-4\) probes.** The chain sites may replace arbitrary closed planar diagrams on an empty spatial path and must be totally ordered by generating-gate dependence. In particular the degree-six test requires at most two probes, even when a probe's face is available for only part of the chain. A finite-subset detection bound also holds for the *raw* groups when the chain sites have arbitrary spatial ports.

### Puncturing the one-probe comparison

Choose a point of the unmarked chain base. Draw the original and fully flattened histories of [Response 61](#researcher-response-61) in the same rectangle, with the same changing disks, gate positions and seam motions. At regular times the flat obstacles are the gate points; the planar obstacles additionally include the wires. At a site the disk replaces its contents in both pictures. This realizes the raw comparison on each occupied component as an orientation-preserving open inclusion \(C\hookrightarrow V\) of the one-probe 3-manifolds, where \(V\) is its flat component. The comparison on configurations uses that same inclusion in every coordinate. At a gap it forgets the wires and at a tie it uses the common outside of the disk, exactly as on the positioned charts. For point-site chains, [Response 59](#researcher-response-59) gives this one-probe inclusion a homotopy left inverse at the fixed history. We need a separate argument to pass to configurations: a homotopy left inverse on points need not preserve distinctness.

Let \(C\hookrightarrow V\) be an orientation-preserving open inclusion of connected open 3-manifolds with a homotopy left inverse. For any finite \(Q\subset C\), the inclusion \(C-Q\to V-Q\) is injective on all positive homotopy groups. Removing points leaves fundamental groups unchanged by general position. In the universal covers the induced map identifies distinct lifts of a given puncture with distinct lifts in the target, by fundamental-group injectivity. Locally around each such puncture it is an oriented homeomorphism onto a neighborhood. Here are the details for higher homotopy.

A simply connected open orientable 3-manifold \(X\) has the homotopy type of a wedge of 2-spheres. Its homology above degree two vanishes. Its second homology is free abelian: by ordinary Poincaré duality this is \(H_c^1(X;\mathbb Z)\). Exhaust by connected compact submanifolds with finite boundary and fill the relatively compact complementary components. The exact sequence of each pair, using \(H^1(X)=0\), computes the direct limit as the locally constant integer functions on the finite sets of complementary components modulo constants. As the exhaustion increases every old unbounded component has a descendant. Pullback along these refinements splits, with free cokernel (choose one descendant per old component). The union is therefore free. Represent a basis by based spheres using Hurewicz; the map from their CW wedge is a homology equivalence between simply connected spaces of CW type, hence an equivalence. This reasoning applies also after puncturing a universal cover at a closed locally finite set of points, which preserves simple connectivity by general position.

For the punctured cover, excision gives the short exact sequence on second homology with kernel freely generated by the small meridian spheres at **all individual puncture lifts** and quotient \(H_2(X)\). This uses \(H_3(X)=0\) and ordinary finite chains. In the source-to-target comparison the map on these kernels has a left inverse by the distinct-lift and orientation observations. The map on quotients has a left inverse from the lift of the unpunctured homotopy retraction (choose compatible basepoints). Since the quotient groups are free, splitting both short exact sequences gives a block triangular map with split-injective diagonal blocks. Subtract its off-diagonal block using those left inverses to get a left inverse on the whole \(H_2\). For the two wedges of 2-spheres this homomorphism can be realized by a based map, by Hurewicz on each summand; its composite is homotopic to the identity on the source wedge. This proves the punctured-cover comparison, including its effect on torsion in higher homotopy.

Forget the last of \(m\) ordered points. The configuration fibration has fibre the manifold minus the other \(m-1\) positions and has a section: place the extra point sufficiently far out a fixed proper ray, continuously beyond a proper exhaustion bound for the existing points. One may choose the exhaustion increasing along a terminal part of the ray or a continuous majorant for its last exit times. The homotopy sequence therefore makes the fibre groups injective; a change from the section basepoint to the desired basepoint only transports the sequence. Compare the fibrations for \(C\) and \(V\). Induction on \(m\), using the punctured-cover result on the fibre and injectivity on the base, proves injectivity on \(\pi_j\) for every \(j\ge1\). The sections need not commute with the inclusion. For several occupied planar components, forget all names except those in one component at a time. This detects each factor even when the different components map to the very same \(V\). [Fadell and Neuwirth, *Configuration spaces*](https://www.mscand.dk/article/download/10517/8538)

The two unmarked chain bases are still 1-types and their map is injective on fundamental groups by Response 52. The same exact-sequence argument as in Response 59 now gives positive-degree injectivity on the **total** based history spaces. A flat raw filler therefore gives a raw filler at the original boundary in degree five or above. The scalar-rule theorem of Response 48 supplies the flat filling from any filling for the two published surfaces or the four binary surfaces omitting interchange. This proves the six unrestricted probe-count assertions. Nothing in the proof applies a pointwise retraction to a tuple that might acquire a collision.

### How many probes a difference sphere needs

For a connected open orientable 3-manifold \(C\) as above, all coordinate projections onto at most \(k\) of the original named points jointly detect \(\pi_k\operatorname{Conf}_m(C)\) for \(k\ge1\). In degree one, paths and homotopies of paths in the product can avoid the pair diagonals by general position in codimension three. For \(k\ge2\), induct using the same forgetful fibration. If projection to the base vanishes the class lies in the injected fibre group. In its universal cover choose the sphere basis for the unpunctured manifold and move its representatives off the discrete puncture set by general position. With one small sphere per lift of a puncture, these form a wedge basis of the punctured cover by the excision calculation. Forgetting punctures pinches exactly their meridian spheres. The old representatives persist even when the unpunctured manifold itself carries nonzero second homotopy.

Take a finite subwedge supporting this class. In Hilton's decomposition in degree \(k\) only basic products of length at most \(k-1\) can contribute, since a product of length \(d\) has sphere dimension \(d+1\). Keep the puncture names of one nonzero summand, together with the moving point. At most \(k\) original names suffice; old ambient spheres cost no puncture names. The retained wedge still detects the summand, by pinching to its letters, and the projected configuration fibration injects the resulting fibre class. This proves the bound, including classes with zero total linking. Applied to the fibre in Response 61 it holds for arbitrary spatial ports. For \(k=1\) on total spaces one also retains the unmarked chain projection; for \(k\ge2\) its base has vanishing higher homotopy. [Hilton, Theorem A](https://ncatlab.org/nlab/files/Hilton55.pdf)

For an individual surface flatten the old query first. Lift a putative flat surface witness and its boundary with the scalar wire and event names fixed at the common source, as in Responses 44, 48 and 52. In degree five this forces the two paths to have the same transported names at their common end; above it the named coverings preserve higher homotopy. Keep the whole chain and any selected probe names, specializing other probe uses to the identity at their empty ports with the point-only fits. This is a surface substitution on the named scalar-rule presentation. Reflection at the resulting tests with at most \(k=n-4\) probes, including the unmarked test, forces the full flat difference class to vanish by the preceding projection bound. Total-space injectivity then gives the original raw filler. Conversely the bounded scalar-rule tests are themselves members of the class. This equivalence for the four remaining surfaces keeps the chain and its stationary 2-gates unrestricted.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_collision_sheets.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/CollisionSheets.agda`. The checker uses a lens born and consumed by two dependent rules. For access paths in its temporarily available bounded face it executes positioned child braids, reads their full flattened images, and evaluates those images in the free meridian basis after attaching the birth and death. It separates the resulting puncture lifts in an explicit finite permutation cover. For two different compound ports it recovers 106 signed coefficients from 318 summands each, in covers of 79 and 105 sheets. A difference of two collision meridians with access paths differing by a commutator has zero ordinary total linking but yields coefficients \(+1,-1\) on sheets 0 and 10 of an 11-sheet cover. Agda checks two distinct probes inside a temporary lens at its actual inner object in a raw term running from the empty diagram back to itself. The JSON solver computes integral sums of these lifted collision meridians for supplied child sizes, fixed names and access words. [Collision-sheet solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_collision_sheets.py)

## Researcher Response 64

**The no-2-generator test of Response 23 detects the higher probe obstructions of gate-dependent chains on positively weighted wires, even with compound splits, merges and changes at nonempty spatial paths.** Give each graph edge a positive integer weight. Each generating 2-cell must have equal, strictly positive total weight on its two spatial words. Fix a supported history consisting of a chain of non-scalar sites, each sharing a generating 2-gate with the next, and any number of free 3-scalar probes. At a degree \(n\ge6\) query it suffices, for each occupied probe component, to test at most \(n-4\) scalar uses on the standard two-wire gap with **one interval occurrence per directly enclosed component of a computable planar trace**. If the component carries at most one probe it needs no test. The assertion applies separately to all ten ordinary surfaces. Degree five and below already reflect by [Response 36](#researcher-response-36).

### The trace at a gap

On the supporting edge path mark positions by cumulative weight. An actual gap at a horizontal slice thus has an integer coordinate \(j\), between zero and the full width inclusive. Every gate has a well-defined support interval in these coordinates and its two ends are the same on both sides of the gate. Say that a gate or site crosses \(j\) when \(j\) is strictly interior to its interval. In a 2-diagram read just the crossing gates in drawing-height order. An exchange of disjoint positioned rows preserves this list since two disjoint blocks cannot both cross \(j\).

In the plane with coordinates drawing height and history time construct a progressive diagram \(K_j\). Its strands are the occurrences on these lists transported through the history. A chain use crossing \(j\) gives a vertex whose incoming and outgoing lists are exactly the crossing gates in its input and output ports, including the empty list at either end. A use outside the cut leaves the list unchanged. The affected sublist is consecutive: the whole port is inserted at the single row of the site. A crossing vertex is present even when both sublists are empty. The diagram may have several floating components and they can enclose each other. At time zero or one a strand meeting the boundary is anchored. For a probe at a written slice use its actual wire gap to compute \(j\), then locate it in the face of \(K_j\) at that time by counting the crossing rows below it.

Every accessible face \(F\) of \(K_j\) gives exactly one probe component, with the homotopy geometry of \(F\times\mathbb R\), naturally also for configurations of distinct probes. Here is a way to see the width and accessibility assertions simultaneously. Draw an edge of weight \(d\) as a thick band of \(d\) parallel lanes, filling the internal gaps of that band. Between successive lane blocks keep a narrow open interval for each actual wire gap. At a gate use a transverse bar joining the incident lanes, covering all gaps strictly inside its interval; the lateral gaps continue. Rectangular collars of a gate with its incident strips are regular neighborhoods of its planar star, with the same boundary ports. Replacing the star by this drawing gives the same open-face configuration spaces and the same inclusions outside the change disks, as in [Response 61](#researcher-response-61). Use the common band positions on both sides of a change. In its time collar the bars of its crossing input rows end at the site and those of its output rows begin there. Equivalently use a small disk about this vertex in the height-time diagram, straightening the incident strands to its rim. At an exchange the order of crossing rows does not change; their heights move by an isotopy. In the narrow interval for coordinate \(j\), the barriers are precisely the strands and vertices of \(K_j\), times the transverse interval. An intrawire gap in an undivided band is filled. Its availability can change only across a bar or a change straddling \(j\), so it is constant on each open face of \(K_j\). This proves the product description on accessible faces; it avoids counting gaps internal to a weight-two edge as probe positions. Different accessible faces have separate position components. The planar face is a disk with one hole per directly enclosed floating component, so a single probe there has zero higher homotopy.

### A surface substitution that detects the face

Map every graph object to one object and each edge of weight \(d\) to the word \(e^d\) in a loop. Send an ordinary 2-gate to the graph-only comparison at its two actual image words, which have the same strict path. Keep each non-scalar 3-generator as a separate interval generator on its full weight, with identity 2-faces; transport it to the two images of its written ports. Such transports exist at the prescribed graph-only poles by the graph comparison. For example one can use a fixed graph-only 2-comparison at those poles, transport the identity-faced generator to it by whiskering with graph-only fits, then compose with fits to the written 2-faces. Keep a probe as a 3-scalar with its point fits. This gives a literal substitution into a presentation without generating 2-cells. It sends a positioned site to the interval at its actual weighted offset; the graph-only collars add no generating occurrence. In the original gap the map forgets the crossing gate strands but remembers the crossing *vertices* as forbidden points in the height-time plane. This differs from collapsing all graph edges to units.

Use the occurrence lifts in the interval presentation of [Response 23](#researcher-response-23) on the image of any proposed surface filler. For an occupied component keep its probe names and one crossing site from each directly enclosed component of \(K_j\). Each such component contains a site: a vertex-free strand spans the time boundary and cannot be a floating child. Apply the named specialization of Response 23 retaining the two lanes adjacent to \(j\); all the selected sites straddle both. For an exterior gap there are no crossing sites and the same test uses just the probes. The map on the chosen probe positions, at the fixed unmarked source history, is inclusion of \(F\times\mathbb R\) into the plane punctured at the selected sites times \(\mathbb R\). The original cylinder about a child goes once around its selected puncture. More precisely a compact planar core of \(F\) has exactly one retained puncture in each interior complementary disk and none in its exterior. The inclusion is a homotopy equivalence by retracting to the child meridians. This inclusion of open 3-manifolds induces injections on all positive homotopy groups of ordered configurations by the punctured-cover argument of [Response 63](#researcher-response-63).

The unmarked source chain has a 1-type base by Response 52. The unmarked target is now allowed all schedules of the retained intervals; at this one gap its base is an ordinary planar configuration space and is also a 1-type. Consequently their higher total groups are computed in the respective fixed-history fibres. Jointly over occupied components the indicated substitutions detect every higher difference sphere. The bound on probe names follows by forgetting points as in Response 63. Folding the names at the two-wire target detects nullity by the configuration covering. Thus a surface witness at the original type would give one at a detected raw obstruction in the stated bounded test. The test keeps the original number of holes even when many more crossing sites occur on their boundaries.

This gives unconditional cases. If at most one probe occupies each accessible trace face, all higher boundaries already fill raw. If every multiply occupied face has no enclosed component, only pure 3-scalar tests remain. Both published surfaces, and the binary surfaces omitting interchange, then reflect by [Response 48](#researcher-response-48). A sufficient certificate for this last condition is that every crossing site at each such gap has a nonempty crossing input list, or that all have nonempty crossing outputs: tracing a predecessor or successor reaches a boundary, excluding floating components. The face-by-face test also allows bounded faces without children.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_gap_trace.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/GapTrace.agda`. The solver constructs each trace from actual typed rows and signed seams, computes its direct children and returns their selected use indices. It independently compares the partition of all probe placements and every winding rank with the original three-dimensional face gluing of Response 61. A six-use control chain with a split, an enclosed site, a merge and exterior components gives 76 placements in five faces. A change involving one weight-two edge and two weight-one edges gives 15 actual placements and just one accessible middle face. Nine further compound traces pass the same checks. Agda checks the unequal-arity chain and its literal substitution retaining the interval events, including the fits at the compound two-gate port. [Weighted gap trace solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_gap_trace.py)

## Researcher Response 65

**For Q3(a) there is an exact decision of the source-based occurrence cut test on a class with no bound on new vertices, even along a successful computation. On general presentations this test is \(\Pi^0_1\)-complete.** There is an explicit finite presentation and old 3-source from which *every* old 4-query reflects although every single cut model extending all matched sources by generating copies requires infinitely many vertices. The positive and negative results concern both coherence rules, every admissible \(E\), and \(S\subseteq\{A,U\}\). The complexity statement concerns recognition by this sufficient condition for reflection; it does not classify conservativity itself.

### Two certificates for reflection at a written query

Write \(D_e\) for the literal occurrence diagram, with its universal term and map to the given computad \(X\). Fix an old 4-type with faces \(s,t\). Start at \(D_s\) and repeatedly attach \(D_g\) along **any** match of \(D_{s g}\) for a 4-generator \(g\), as in Response 62. Here every finite sequence is admitted, without a count bound or any intermediate quotient. Keep the distinguished lift of \(s\). Call a stage terminal if it carries a lift of the written \(t\) literally parallel to that lift. The test asks whether every terminal stage has a nondecreasing two-colouring on vertices with every generator of degree two through four crossing from left to right. A positive answer proves reflection: a surface witness has such a terminal development at its lifted old boundary, so the cut theorem gives a raw filler there, which folds. [Cut reflection](#researcher-response-49) · [Construction from the source of a witness](#researcher-response-62)

A certificate is a computad \(Y\to X\) with a lift of \(s\) such that every generator-preserving map \(D_{s g}\to Y\) over \(X\) extends over \(D_g\). It suffices to give its skeleton through degree three and require that every matching written source has a parallel matching written target. Adjoin copies of the corresponding 4-generators at those pairs. There are two ways to finish the certificate:

1. Give a cut on \(Y\), requiring crossing object poles for each matched 4-source as well. This certifies reflection for **every parallel old target** from \(s\).
2. Require that no lift of \(t\) in \(Y\) is literally parallel to the marked lift. This certifies emptiness in both theories at that query.

These two alternatives, allowing infinite \(Y\), are jointly **equivalent** to the test. Every finite development maps to a certificate extending its seed, by successive pushouts. For the converse, form a countable chain of finite developments by fairly extending all matches: enumerate those at each finite stage and dovetail the resulting requests, transporting each match to the current stage. Its colimit \(C\) has the extension property. A match into the colimit involves finitely many generators and finitely many literal type equalities, hence factors at some common stage; any eventual equalities of names hold at a finite stage. The same argument lifts a terminal match back to a finite stage, including the parallelism equations at the distinguished lift. If no terminal match appears, \(C\) gives the second alternative. Otherwise all sufficiently late stages are terminal. If the test holds, each has a cut. Pullback of a cut along the maps of stages is a cut, including when vertices become identified. Compatible cuts on the whole chain exist by the finitely branching tree lemma, applied to finite compatible strings of choices, of which there are arbitrarily long ones. They give a cut on \(C\) and the first alternative. A negative answer has a finite witness, enumerable by enumerating raw matches, pushouts and terminal lifts and testing the finite graph. This characterizes the test also when different occurrences of one generator have the same lifted source and different lifted targets.

### A decidable condition on the two written faces of each rule

The following translation gives a finite certificate whenever the condition below holds. It also specifies how to check the condition directly on the occurrence diagrams. Use one relation \(R_h\) for each generator \(h\) through degree three, with one coordinate for *every generator of* \(D_h\), including its top generator. For a finite diagram \(A\to X\), assign one variable \(x_i\) to each of its generators. Its conjunction \(P_A(x)\) contains for every \(i\) over \(h\) the atom of \(R_h\) whose coordinates are read along the canonical map \(D_h\to A\) at \(i\). Coordinates can repeat. Require each \(R_h\)-tuple to give all its proper generator projections in their respective relations. These are single-atom guarded implications.

In a computad model one can use its generator names as tokens, and store for each generator its entire canonical tuple of tokens. Conversely, from any relations satisfying the projection requirements decode a generator as its old label together with its **whole tuple**. Its written type is obtained using the lower projected tuples. The two parallelism equations hold because these projections compose along the actual maps of raw diagrams; equivalently substitute the projected tuples in the universal written type in \(D_h\). An assignment satisfying \(P_A\) gives a map from \(A\) to this computad. Conversely read the top token of the image of each \(i\). In the tuple of that image, every other token is then forced by following the projection to the corresponding lower generator. This gives the inverse, even if different tuples use the same top token. No functionality or injectivity axioms on tokens are needed.

For a 4-rule put \(A=D_{s g}, B=D_{t g}\). The two diagrams come with maps from their common lower source and common lower target diagrams. Let \(J\) be the pairs \((i,j)\) of generators in \(A,B\) obtained by following the same generator of either of these lower diagrams. The extension requirement on the skeleton is precisely
\[
 \forall x\;\big(P_A(x)\ \Longrightarrow\
    \exists y\,[P_B(y)\ \land\ \bigwedge_{(i,j)\in J}x_i=y_j]\big).
\]
Indeed those token equalities give equality of all the lower tuples on both common faces, or equivalently literal parallelism. For the cut use a unary colour predicate per old object, forbid right-to-left edges and require the exterior colours of each higher tuple to be left and right. Require the two exterior colours also whenever \(P_A\) matches. These are unary tests under the corresponding hypotheses. For target exclusion name the seed tuple by constants satisfying \(P_{D_s}\) and forbid the positive existential sentence matching \(P_{D_t}\) at its two common faces. This encodes exactly the two certificates above.

To check the guard, substitute a source variable for each target variable anchored by \(J\), recording the equalities between source variables when a target position has several anchors. Separate the target conjunction into pieces joined by shared **existential** variables; an atom with no existential and each residual equality can be checked on its own. For each piece let \(I\) be its set of source variables. Require that every two distinct members of \(I\) occur together in some atom of \(P_A\). This is a finite test for each rule. A single source atom containing all of \(I\) suffices but is unnecessary. In particular a variable source guards its entire interface, irrespective of the number of new vertices in its target. A repeated target coordinate may impose a genuine two-variable equality and must pass the same guard test.

Here is why the decision follows. Each required piece is an existential positive formula of its interface variables. An extension fails if some source assignment satisfies \(P_A\) and the negation of that piece. Under the pair condition the conjunction of source atoms itself clique-guards the free variables of that negation: each pair occurs in a conjunct, and the guard may contain the additional source variables. Existentially quantify the source assignment in this failure sentence. A singleton is guarded by self-equality, and sentences may be negated. Thus the implications belong to clique-guarded negation first-order logic. The projection axioms, unary colour requirements and terminal exclusion are in the same fragment. Its finite model property and decidable satisfiability, including constants, apply to each alternative. In particular a positive answer supplies a finite certificate, which can also be obtained by enumeration after the decision. The double-exponential decision upper bound is in the size of the **expanded logical sentence**. For Q1(a) the target-exclusion decision also works in every output degree \(n\ge2\): use relations through degree \(n-1\), replace the 4-rules by the \(n\)-rules, and use the same two lower face maps. Its positive answer certifies emptiness in both theories. Only the deduction of reflection from a cut uses the degree-four theorem. [Bárány, ten Cate and Segoufin, Theorem 32 and Proposition 34](https://lsv.ens-paris-saclay.fr/Publis/PAPERS/PDF/BCS-jacm15.pdf)

For example use \(P=(ff)f\) on a loop, with the left and right two-edge excursions \(B_L,B_R\) of Response 62. On the middle edge insert a 2-expansion \(f\to ff\) followed by a contraction \(ff\to f\), keeping the two outside edges fixed. A 3-birth from \(1_P\) to this placed gate and a 3-death back give an excursion \(M\). Take opposite 4-rules between \(B_L\) and \(B_R;M\). Their common interface is carried by a birth at the whole written \(P\) on each source. The lower diagram of the forward rule supplies a finite certificate for both: each source has just one lift there, and its opposite face is parallel. The two-edge gates and the middle expansion/contraction all cross the middle cut. Either colour is possible for the temporary midpoint. In the universal development from \(B_L\), each forward step of a repeated round trip introduces a new midpoint; the return attaches along the whole excursion and leaves that vertex distinct. The old exterior path is the same at the end of every round trip. Hence at this fixed old boundary even *actual witnesses* have unbounded universal vertex counts.

### Complexity and an infinite certificate that finite covers cannot replace

We describe a family of finite raw computads. On one object use loops for letters, with nonempty words given fixed raw bracketings. For a word rule \(u\rightsquigarrow v\) take fresh 2-generators \(u\to v\) and \(v\to u\). Let \(K\) be their temporal composite. A 3-birth \(1_u\to K\) and death \(K\to1_u\) give a 3-bubble. Add a 4-rule from \(1_{1_u}\) to this bubble, and its reverse as a separate declaration. The graph of the forward rule diagram consists exactly of the path for \(u\) and a fresh path for \(v\) between its ends. To see this, the two 2-generators share the entire intermediate \(v\) in \(K\); the birth identifies its two exterior \(u\)-paths via the common faces of \(1_u\). The death has the identical gate and is glued along it. No interior vertex of \(v\) is glued to \(u\). All higher generators have the two exterior poles. Thus a forward attachment adds the alternate path at any match of \(u\); a reverse attachment adds no edge or vertex and identifies none of the old ones. In particular pairing these 4-rules does **not** reverse the word rewrite. The reverse source already contains the whole pair of paths.

Use a rewriting system with one state letter on either side of each rule and on the initial word \(w\); all remaining letters are tape or delimiter symbols. Reserve a state \(H\) and a tape letter \(k\) for the final adjacent pair \(Hk\). Add a special rule on \(Hk\) by the same construction except that its gate is a 2-generator on \(H\) whiskered by \(k\), followed by a 2-generator on \(k\) whiskered by \(H\). These changes use adjacent edges, so a cut on the whole special diagram would require their common vertex to have both colours. Its source still matches any \(Hk\)-path.

Start at \(s=1_{1_w}\). Before a special attachment, colour every path by putting stationary tape edges on the appropriate side of its state and making every state edge cross. This starts on \(w\). At each matched \(u\) its exterior poles are left and right, so the fresh path can be coloured according to the position of the state in \(v\). This preserves all earlier colours and gives a cut at every stage. A special attachment is possible **exactly when a reachable word contains \(Hk\)**. In one direction, a word step adds the required alternate subpath and preserves the rest of the route. For the converse the graph stays acyclic, each fresh path being inserted parallel to a nonempty old path, and every vertex stays on a route between the initial exterior poles. Every occurrence of an edge of the newest alternate path in a full route traverses that entire fresh path, as its interior vertices were fresh. Replacing that segment by the matched \(u\) gives a route in the preceding graph. Induction shows that the word of every exterior route is reachable by forward rewrites. A matched \(Hk\) extends to such a route, which proves the converse. The reverse special rule cannot be the first special attachment because its source uses the fresh special birth and death.

Take a fixed universal Turing machine, encode a configuration by its tape word with endmarkers and a single state at the scanned letter, and simulate its local steps by nonempty word rules. A right move replaces the state and scanned letter with the written letter and new state before the next scanned letter; a left move also matches the immediately preceding letter. Include the endmarker cases inserting a blank when necessary. Use a fresh halting rule replacing the halt state by \(Hk\). These rules preserve the one-state condition and make the distinguished pair reachable exactly on halting inputs. With \(t=s\) every development is terminal already. The preceding equivalence and finite enumeration of bad developments prove \(\Pi^0_1\)-completeness of this occurrence cut test even with the finite computad fixed and only the input word varied. The query \(s\to s\) itself always has its raw identity. This distinguishes precisely the complexity proved here from the complexity question about conservativity in Q2. [Configuration-word simulation, §23.5](https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~ear/cs341/automatabook/AutomataTheoryBook.pdf)

There is a small explicit instance where the positive result needs an infinite-vertex certificate. Use the five word rules
\[
 S\rightsquigarrow aSb,\qquad S\rightsquigarrow T,\qquad
 aTb\rightsquigarrow T,\qquad aTr\rightsquigarrow Hk,\qquad lTb\rightsquigarrow Hk.
\]
Use the special rule above and the initial word \(lSr\). The reachable words are \(la^i S b^i r\) and \(la^i T b^i r\), \(i\ge0\). Thus every query from \(1_{1_{lSr}}\) to a parallel old target reflects by the cut argument. Suppose one cut certificate extending all matched sources had finitely many vertices. Between its fixed exterior poles it must have a path for \(la^i S b^i r\) for every \(i\), by iterating the expansion at the state edge. Choose \(i\) greater than the number of vertices. Along its \(a\)-segment two vertices coincide, so skipping the intervening loop gives a path for \(la^j S b^i r\) with \(j<i\). Extension at \(S\) supplies a \(T\)-edge there. Apply \(aTb\rightsquigarrow T\) exactly \(j\) times. The resulting path contains \(lTb\), which supplies \(Hk\) and forces the incompatible cut. This rules out even a certificate having infinitely many parallel generators on finitely many vertices. For target \(s\) the target-exclusion alternative is impossible too. The infinite certificate is supplied by the chain construction; every particular witness uses a finite cut diagram.

**Validation.** The checker evaluates the relation conjunctions and independently enumerates maps of raw computads. It checks both extension directions in the guarded example, a boundary equality identifying the two endpoints of a lifted edge, and target exclusion at a scalar seed admitting no cut. Six literal round trips grow from four vertices to ten. For each of the five word rules it unfolds the actual 4-declaration and checks its two labelled paths, including the absence of extra edges or vertices; five nested pushes yield exactly six balanced exterior words. Agda checks the guarded round trip, its literal fold from the occurrence diagram, and the expansion followed by its reverse in the balanced-word presentation. The general decision and hardness proofs are above. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_guarded_occurrences.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/GuardedOccurrences.agda`. [Relational and raw occurrence checks](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_guarded_occurrences.py)

## Researcher Response 66

**Gate dependence in Response 64 can be dropped.** On finite ordinary 3-computads with positive conserved wire weights and strictly positive 2-gate widths, degree-wise reflection for each individual surface is equivalent to its conjunction on two subclasses: the same weighted presentations without scalar 3-generators, and the single no-2-generator test computad of [Response 23](#researcher-response-23). The equivalence is needed only from degree six on; all ten surfaces already reflect below that degree. At a given query the second test still uses at most \(n-4\) probes and just one interval per directly enclosed component of the occupied trace face. This holds with arbitrarily many simultaneously available non-scalar rules. In particular one may use the previously established raw asphericity tests on the unmarked component alone, without testing whether a probe forms a simultaneous triple with two of its sites.

### Forgetting probes over a changing schedule

Under the weight hypotheses every change on an empty spatial path is a free scalar occurrence in planar evaluation. Its attaching terms are point-only fits: an ordinary 2-gate cannot act on a path of zero weight. There is a literal substitution retaining the lower computad and the non-scalar rules, sending the scalar declarations to point-only comparisons. In realization it forgets their marked uses. Denote the resulting space of fixed-end unmarked histories by \(B\), which need not be a 1-type. Use individual occurrence names at the chosen boundary. Since all the ordinary gates and non-scalar markers have positive input, each component of an unmarked lower chart is contractible: its planar components are input-anchored and the positioned pure braid group has no floating factor. The unmarked coefficient at any ordered partition is thus homotopy discrete, including its paths between successive expanded batches. Its points can be represented by marked batch drawings with the prescribed seam and end paths.

Here is the homotopy fibre of the forgetful map over any such point. Use the space \(M\) of Response 61 with **one disjoint changing disk for each site of a tied batch**. Two simultaneous sites can use the same wires at different drawing heights; disjointness concerns their small disks in the planar drawing. The fibre is the ordered configuration space of the named probes in the components of \(M\) having their object labels. In particular the identification is compatible with splitting the batch, with the actual seam transports, and with forgetting probe names.

For details, project the ordered partitions of all names to those of the non-scalar names. First take the homotopy left Kan extension of the marked coefficient diagram along this projection. At an unmarked schedule \(u\), one can use just the marked schedules projecting *exactly* to \(u\). Indeed in the comma category a marked schedule projecting to a coarser one has a contractible poset of further refinements projecting exactly to \(u\). Within each old tier place its ordered subtiers of unmarked sites at prescribed distinct times and let all its probe times vary in the intervening open time interval. The weak-order cells, allowing any ties, have exactly this refinement poset, and their order complex is contractible by the convex time region. A tier with only probes has its original tier as a minimum among refinements. Take the product over tiers. This proves finality for the Kan extension before imposing any geometric coefficient restrictions. [The corresponding shuffle calculation](#researcher-response-50)

Over a chosen unmarked drawing the exact-fibre calculation is now the one in Response 61 with several disks at a critical time. Subdividing an unmarked path in a regular gap at probe batches has contractible space of choices. At a critical time tied probes avoid all the disjoint disks, with planar points distinct within their equal-time blocks. Order the times and subdivide the allowed position cells as in that response. The resulting homotopy colimit is the configuration space in this particular \(M\).

It remains to check that these objectwise fibres are still the fibres after forming the unmarked homotopy colimit. For a refinement choose a collar about each split tier, disjoint from the other time collars. In its planar drawing take disjoint slightly larger neighborhoods of the site disks. On the annuli between them, the external wire arcs are constant in time, using radial collars. Move the change times to the prescribed suborder by a time shift supported in these neighborhoods. Explicitly in each smaller disk use an increasing piecewise linear reparametrization of the collar time, fixed at its ends; interpolate it to the identity across the annulus. At each fixed planar position the interpolation is still strictly increasing. Scaling the shifts from zero to their chosen sizes gives an ambient isotopy. This identifies the complements and the ordered configurations throughout the refinement.

To see that this checks the Kan transition itself, include the shift parameter as a base edge. At its initial endpoint the unmarked tier is tied; elsewhere take its ordered subtiers with separations proportional to the parameter. Subdivide by the probe time orders relative to these times, allowing equality with each other and with the subtiers. At the initial endpoint the faces are the exact marked lifts of the tied schedule. At positive parameter they are the exact lifts of the refined schedule. Specializing a positive face to parameter zero coalesces the subtiers and permits precisely the corresponding marked coarsenings: inside a coalescing tier any prescribed refined probe times can be realized by scaling their offsets too. Subdividing the position coordinates compatibly with the port drawings and their disks now gives the same flag diagram over this edge as in the Kan construction. Its generization maps relax collisions or insert the appropriate ports. This is the mapping-cylinder comparison for the two exact subdiagrams, including the refinements inside each end. On the configuration-space side the isotopy trivializes the family over the edge, so these transitions on fibres are equivalences. A different reading or seam representative transports configurations by the corresponding planar motion. Consequently all squares over unmarked refinements are homotopy cartesian. The homotopy-colimit fibre lemma identifies the computed fibres with the actual homotopy fibres over \(B\). This is a local assertion and allows monodromy around an unmarked loop. [Chachólski, Pitsch and Scherer, homotopy pullbacks and Puppe's theorem](https://www.epfl.ch/labs/hessbellwald-lab/wp-content/uploads/2018/10/ChacholskiPitschSchererHomotopypullbackpublished.pdf)

### Joint detection with the unmarked boundary

At a fixed history split its simultaneous batches in any order. The isotopy just constructed lets one read each probe component as \(F\times\mathbb R\) using the weighted-gap trace of [Response 64](#researcher-response-64). In the height-time plane splitting a batch separates its vertices in disjoint disks and preserves the correspondence of the complementary faces and their enclosed components. The all-interval substitution of that response is available on the entire presentation. After applying the named occurrence lifts in the interval target, retain one site per child of the chosen face and the desired probe names. On the chosen fibre this gives the same detecting maps on configurations as before. The unmarked bases of the specialized tests are 1-types, so their fibre groups inject into their total homotopy groups.

Let \(k=n-4\ge2\). If an original difference class projects nontrivially to \(\pi_k B\), erasing the scalar declarations already detects it at the old unmarked query. Otherwise exactness puts it in the image of the fibre group. If every specialized interval test vanishes, any fibre preimage maps trivially into all the specialized fibres by their injections into the targets. The projections of Response 63 followed by the selected-hole maps of Response 64 jointly detect this fibre group. The preimage, and hence the original class, vanishes. This argument makes no assumption on \(\pi_{k+1} B\). For a surface witness the unmarked substitution and all the interval specializations give witnesses on the same individual surface. Raw reflection at these tests therefore suffices. Conversely the scalar-free subclass and the fixed test context both satisfy the weight hypothesis. This proves the uniform equivalence.

For instance, consider a scalar-free network whose schedules have no simultaneous triple. Its unmarked space is aspherical by [Response 54](#researcher-response-54), since all non-scalar sites have positive spatial width. At an allocation with at most one probe per trace face, this gives raw fillers from degree six on even when many probes can tie with a pair of non-scalar sites. Another application takes the unmarked expansion/contraction forests of Response 50. At every weighted gap each straddling expansion has a crossing gate on its bare input and each straddling contraction has one on its bare output. A floating component of the trace would start at a contraction with no crossing inputs. Following its crossing output, the next use cannot be an expansion, by the hypothesis on contraction outputs and bare expansion inputs. Every succeeding contraction again has a crossing output, so the path reaches the time boundary, a contradiction. There are therefore no enclosed holes. The unmarked forest theorem and the pure 3-scalar theorem give reflection for both published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange on these weighted forests with arbitrary free probes. For the other four surfaces only the pure 3-scalar tests remain on this subclass.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_batch_traces.py --agda-dir DIR`, `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_gap_trace.py --agda-dir DIR`, and check `DIR/BatchTraces.agda` and `DIR/GapTrace.agda` with `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR`. The batch solver constructs the two face maps of each simultaneous planar drawing before serializing anything. Across 216 serial refinements and 3,672 tested placements the weighted traces give the same partition, winding ranks and selected hole representatives as the simultaneous face incidences and component comparison. The examples include three sites on the same two wires, a spectator exchanged at a seam, and different ways to consume the five intermediate gates. The checker computes the time-lift cell homology across nine split shapes (959 cells), and verifies by conjugating actual three-strand braids that a full twist of two interval sites acts nontrivially on the probe's two meridians. Agda checks the two refinements of a same-wire batch with a birth of two gates and a death of another. The JSON solver accepts simultaneous batches, their port drawings, signed seams and optional chosen serial orders. [Concurrent weighted trace solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_batch_traces.py)

## Researcher Response 67

**The two published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange reflect in every degree on the planar expansion/contraction forests of [Response 50](#researcher-response-50) with arbitrary additional free 3-scalars.** No wire weights are required. For each of the four binary surfaces containing interchange, uniform reflection on this class in degree \(n\ge5\) is equivalent to reflection in that degree on the one-object mixed test of [Response 45](#researcher-response-45), with at most \(n-4\) occurrences of its 3-scalar. At a particular history one needs just one stationary 2-scalar occurrence per directly enclosed floating component of a chosen middle face. A moving lens can contribute a puncture even when neither written end contains a lens.

The criterion is on the non-probe uses. Designate each as an expansion from a bare generating 2-gate or a contraction to a bare generating 2-gate. Require that no contraction output occurrence feeds an expansion. This includes the global colour criterion of Response 50. The extra probes have both planar poles empty on the empty spatial path at their object, with the point-only fits of [Response 44](#researcher-response-44). An identity port of a structural rule can contain no generating gates and still have spatial wires. Such a rule keeps its spatial port in the following calculation.

### Computing the probe space at a middle drawing

Name the occurrences and forget the probes. At fixed 2-ends the components of the unmarked history space are contractible by Response 50. In each component choose the representative which does the expansions first, traverses the signed middle seam and then does the contractions. Insert an open regular time slab at its middle drawing. If \(F\) ranges over the planar faces of that drawing and \(m_F\) is the number of probes in the corresponding component, the named fixed-end history space there has weak homotopy type
\[
                 \prod_F \operatorname{Conf}_{m_F}(F\times\mathbb R).
\]
Only faces at the probe's object can receive it. The signed seam transports the faces and gate occurrences. The product is for a fixed allocation and named component.

Here are details of forgetting in the presence of lower planar braids. Use the exact marked schedules over an unmarked schedule as in [Response 66](#researcher-response-66). Cofinality there concerns the ordered partitions alone. At this stage retain the full unmarked coefficient space of batch drawings, seam paths and paths to the fixed ends. Form the exact marked diagram **over** this space. Its homotopy colimit can be computed fibrewise: pullback to a point preserves homotopy colimits in spaces over a space (equivalently model them as families with transport over its fundamental infinity-groupoid). For a fixed unmarked drawing, forgetting added points on each planar slice is the configuration bundle in its faces. In a local trivialization move the wire drawing and the disjoint marked site disks by ambient planar isotopy, carrying all additional points along. This is the planar face bundle of Response 44, applied also to a drawing with the site markers as gates. Along a seam pull back that bundle along the actual path; subdividing the path at new regular probe times has contractible choices. The same description at both ends uses the specified end paths. This computes the homotopy fibres of the exact coefficients and their refinement maps, including when a lower drawing has automorphisms.

Consequently the time-order calculation of [Response 61](#researcher-response-61) gives configurations in the complement \(M\) of the changing diagrams, with one disk per changing site at each critical time. For passage between unmarked schedules the disjoint-disk time isotopy and subdivided edge of Response 66 apply at each point in the source coefficient. The target point here is its actual image under splitting the batch. Wire collars suffice for the time shift, with no assumption on gate width. The comparison on homotopy fibres is an equivalence there, and transport along paths gives the comparison at other representatives. Thus the coefficient squares are homotopy cartesian. The homotopy-colimit fibre lemma of that response gives the stated configuration fibre over the **actual** unmarked history. This argument allows nontrivial planar transport before taking fixed ends. In particular one can choose the sorted representative in the unmarked base without trying to sort past individual probes at prescribed locations.

For one probe, cut the sorted movie into its regular gaps and its critical ties. The face space at an expansion tie maps by an equivalence to the face space immediately *before* it. Replacing the marker disk by the bare input gate restores exactly that gate and its incident wires. Outside the disk the diagrams agree, and inside, each complementary sector just extends its collar. This also holds for a point gate, where the collar is annular, and for a batch of disjoint disks. At a contraction the equivalent side is the one immediately *after* the tie. The complementary face spaces over a time interval are glued by the double mapping cylinders of these two maps, by the one-probe time-order calculation. Starting at the left end and gluing expansions, the inclusion from the newest gap is an equivalence at every step: in the homotopy pushout the map from the tie to the accumulated prefix is an equivalence. Induct backwards from the right for contractions. Regular seam isotopies preserve these maps. Gluing at the middle therefore gives exactly one middle face for each component of \(M\), and its open slab includes by a homotopy equivalence.

To pass to configurations use the **open inclusion** of that slab. An open inclusion of connected orientable 3-manifolds that is a homotopy equivalence still gives a weak equivalence after removing any common finite list of points. Indeed it identifies the fundamental groups and hence the lifts of each puncture in the universal covers. Removing the lifts preserves simple connectivity. Excision identifies the relative homology at these lifts by the oriented local maps, so the map of punctured covers is a homology equivalence, hence a weak equivalence. Apply the exact sequences of the forgetful configuration fibrations inductively, as in [Response 63](#researcher-response-63). Thus placing *all* probes in the middle open slab induces the displayed equivalence for every allocation. Contractibility of the unmarked base component identifies its fibre groups with the total groups. In particular the named degree-five obstruction group is the product of one free group of rank equal to the number of holes of its face for each probe. Every degree-six-or-higher boundary with at most one probe in each middle face fills raw.

### Realizing the stationary tests by substitutions

First collapse the spatial graph and flatten every generating 2-gate to a scalar, keeping the structural rules and probes as in [Response 48](#researcher-response-48). Given a surface witness, lift it with the scalar gate and event occurrences distinguished at the common source. The wire-and-event lift used in Responses 50 and 63 remembers the full incidence network, including empty ports. At degree five it enforces the same transported names at the common end of the two paths, which is needed before comparing them as a named loop.

Fix a middle face, and select one core gate from each of its directly enclosed floating components. Give those middle occurrences distinct 2-scalar names. In the expansion forest assign to each named gate the ordered packet of selected leaves below it, and do the same in the reversed contraction forest, using the middle braid to identify its leaves. A spectator crossing the middle receives the same singleton or empty packet from both sides. A zero-leaf branch receives the empty packet. At each structural occurrence the concatenation of the child packets equals the parent packet in the written port order. This defines a substitution from the named scalar-rule presentation: send a named 2-gate to its packet and send each structural 3-rule to a raw comparison with **no generating 3-use**. These comparisons exist at the written poles because the two scalar words have the same named particles. Use their planar permutation path, with the raw bracketing and graph-only end fits as in Response 48. A surviving probe goes to a free 3-scalar and the other probes to point-only identities with their fits. The two forests can partition the leaves differently. Rectangular cabling on the middle seam followed by restriction to the selected strands gives the actual planar path between the packet readings.

Represent a difference class in the chosen open slab by the preceding equivalence. On the slab this composite substitution keeps exactly the selected punctures and probe coordinates. In the plane with only these punctures the inclusion of the original face is a homotopy equivalence: meridians of the directly enclosed components freely generate its fundamental group and map to a meridian basis about their representatives; both surfaces are aspherical. This can also be seen by filling the other complementary regions while leaving a puncture in each bounded hole. The slab inclusion in the stationary punctured-plane movie is therefore an equivalence on configurations by the open-inclusion argument. The structural paths before and after the slab give fixed transports at the written ends; they do not change this detection. The stationary unmarked space at those ends is a path space in the planar 1-type, so its fibre groups inject into its total groups. Test one occupied face at a time. Coordinate projections to at most \(n-4\) named probes jointly detect the resulting group by Responses 44–45, and their name-cover argument folds both scalar colours to the single names of the fixed test.

This proves reflection from the stationary tests for each individual surface. The mixed test itself occurs with no structural uses, giving the converse. Response 48 settles it for the six indicated surfaces, and the ordinary lower-degree reflection theorems cover degrees through four. For the other four surfaces a history with no holes in its occupied middle faces needs only the pure 3-scalar tests.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_peak_probes.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PeakProbes.agda` with an existing output directory. The solver accepts a signed interleaved peak history and returns the positioned middle diagram, one representative per hole, both forests' packet assignments and the restricted seam. Ten mixed-versus-sorted comparisons cover repeated gate colours, a completely erased branch, two nested lenses and positive and negative seam powers. Fifty executed positioned probe words recover their signed free words, including commutators. On five rules with nonempty spatial ports the independent simultaneous face-incidence solver gives the same fifteen middle faces and hole counts. Agda checks the written two-child expansion, probe and contraction and their substituted stationary term. The underlying normalizer also passes `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_peak_forests.py`. [Middle-face and packet solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_peak_probes.py)

## Researcher Response 68

**There is a finite one-object computad and a written 4-type empty in every theory in Q1, including the ordinary join, that has an inhabitant under every interpretation in a model with finitely many 1-cells.** More generally, for the family below, raw and surface inhabitation are both exactly acceptance by a word-rewriting system. A countermodel finite through its nonidentity cells exists **if and only if** a regular language containing the input is closed under forward rewriting and avoids acceptance. The same criterion characterizes countermodels with finitely many 1-cells and unrestricted higher cells. This concerns actual inhabitants at the given boundary. It holds for every admissible \(E\) under both rules, as well as the Gray and ordinary theories. For ordinary CaTT and its strict-sound surfaces there is such a family in every output degree at least four.

### A word step acts on a specified old boundary

Use tape letters and disjoint state letters, all loops at one object. A configuration \(l q r\) has one state. Each word rule \(u\rightsquigarrow v\) has one state on each side and acts in arbitrary exterior tape words. Acceptance means reaching a configuration containing the distinguished state \(H\). Give each state a 3-generator \(Z_q:1_q\to1_q\). Write each tape word as a left fold of binary composition starting at the identity. For an exterior tape pair write \(P(x)=(l *_0 x)*_0 r\), using those terms even when an exterior word is empty. At \(w=lqr\) put
\[
 W_w=P(q),\qquad K_w=P(1_q),\qquad A_w=P(Z_q),\qquad E_w=P(1_{1_q}).
\]
The old query \(Q(w)\) is \(A_w\to E_w\). Both ends are literal 3-loops on \(K_w\). These are fixed term operations of the two tape terms and the indicated state.

For each rule introduce two fresh 2-generators \(c:W_u\to W_v\), \(d:W_v\to W_u\). Write \(F(x)=(c *_1 x)*_1 d\), and introduce a 3-birth \(b:K_u\to F(K_v)\) and death \(e:F(K_v)\to K_u\). Put \(B(x)=(b *_2 F(x))*_2 e\). Introduce the two 4-generators
\[
                      A_u\longrightarrow B(A_v),
                 \qquad B(E_v)\longrightarrow E_u .
\]
Finally give \(Z_H\to1_{1_H}\) a 4-generator. All faces here are written raw terms. In particular the 1-whiskers on the empty reading remain in the two declarations. A raw answer \(k:Q(v)\) between the two middle faces gives the raw composite of the first declaration, \(B(k)\), and the second declaration, at precisely \(Q(u)\).

We need the stronger implication for a step with arbitrary outside words \(a,b'\), **even given an answer as a new variable** at \(Q(avb')\). This also takes care of the different bracketings in the expanded words. Make two objects, take a loop copy of every tape letter on each side, and put every state from left to right. Lift all displayed declarations using the left copies before the state and the right copies after it. Add the variable \(k:Q(avb')\) there. The cut theorem with compound attachments applies. In its indexed crossing hom the histories of \(A_{aub'}\) and \(E_{aub'}\) are the single marked event at that configuration and the empty history. Use the first 4-declaration with exterior indices \((a,b')\). The new history has a birth, one marked event between the two indexed 2-events \(c,d\), and a death. That mark has exactly the exterior indices of the state in \(avb'\): outside indices concatenate on the left and on the right. The new variable erases that event at the intermediate word, with \(c,d\) as its two local path contexts and the birth and death as its chronological contexts. The second indexed declaration now returns the empty reading. Response 49 realizes this three-step route at the *original written faces*. Fold the tape and object copies back. This proves the conditional raw judgement; no assumed answer has to factor through a whisker. At an accepting configuration the exterior whiskering of the accepting generator is already the desired literal answer. Backward induction gives a raw inhabitant whenever the configuration accepts. [Indexed moves at a cut](#researcher-response-46) · [Realization at compound raw faces](#researcher-response-49)

The cut proof gives the conditional judgement for either coherence rule of each \(E\). One can also use a full-rule derivation projected to Gray and then to ordinary CaTT. In the latter projection each generic full Gray witness has full ordinary positional support, so the same raw head and its projected substitution are admitted. All the written declarations above use only variables, identities, fixed whiskerings and temporal products. Consequently the same positive implication is valid in models of any of the languages in the question, just by interpreting the raw term with the indicated value of \(k\). [Projection of raw heads and support](#researcher-response-2)

### A strict countermodel from a language invariant

Let \(J\) be any forward-closed set of configurations avoiding \(H\). Choose a monoid \(M\) of tape grades, generated by the image of the tape alphabet, such that membership of \(lqr\) in \(J\) depends only on \(([l],q,[r])\). The free tape monoid always works. For regular \(J\) use the transition monoid of the tape letters in an automaton recognizing \(J\); reading the state letter between the two tape transitions determines membership. Write \(J(m,q,n)\) for this predicate on grades.

Construct a one-object strict 4-category by describing its hom as a strict monoidal 3-category. Its objects (global 1-cells) come in three parts:

- Zero state letters gives the discrete 3-category on \(M\).
- One state letter gives objects \((m,q,n)\). Between each ordered pair put one local arrow. Its local 2-endomorphisms are the subsets of \(M\times M\); both compositions of these 2-cells take union, with the empty set as identity. Between two parallel such cells put one local 3-cell exactly for inclusion in the forward direction.
- Two or more state letters gives a terminal 3-category.

There are no higher cells between the three parts. The monoidal product on objects adds the numbers of states truncated at two, multiplies two zero-state grades, and acts on a one-state triple by multiplying its exterior grades. On the subset \(D\) at a one-state arrow the simultaneous left and right actions of \(a,b\in M\) are
\[
                       (a\cdot D\cdot b)(c,d)
                         \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad D(ca,bd).
\]
Products reaching the terminal part are unique. Associativity of multiplication gives the two commuting exterior actions in their indicated orders. Inverse image preserves union, the empty set and inclusion. Within the one-state part, union is associative, commutative and unital and the top homs are thin. These facts verify strict functoriality, units and interchange at every cut. Use only identities above global degree four. If \(M\) is finite there are finitely many nonidentity cells in total.

Send the tape and state generators to their grades and state triples, with \(q\) going to \((1,q,1)\). The local arrows for \(c,d\) are forced. The birth and death are parallel local 2-cells with the required local-arrow faces, and can each take the empty set. Assign \(Z_q\) the set of \((c,d)\) for which \(J(c,q,d)\) holds. The mark on a configuration now tests membership in \(J\) at every further exterior pair, while its empty reading has value the empty set. The advance generator is well typed by forward closure at all those pairs. Every pair of grades has tape representatives, so this is precisely the ordinary closure under placement of a word rule. The closing generator compares empty sets, and the accepting mark is empty at all exterior grades. This interprets the computad. If \(w\in J\), the interpreted mark contains \((1,1)\), excluding a 4-cell to the empty reading. Every surface under consideration is sound in ordinary strict categories, including insertion and the join. In particular taking \(J\) to be the set of configurations with **no** route to acceptance proves that both the raw and the surface answer to \(Q(w)\) are exactly reachability of \(H\).

Conversely, interpret the raw computad in *any* model of one of these theories and let \(J\) be the set of words at which the interpreted query has empty fibre. The conditional raw judgement proves forward closure, and the accepting generator excludes \(H\) from \(J\). If the model has finitely many 1-cells, \(J\) is regular. A finite automaton can accumulate the interpretation of each tape segment by the chosen left fold, retaining the left value and state while it reads the right segment. At the end these three finite data specify both interpreted faces since their constructors were fixed above. The finite table of their fibre emptiness gives the accepting table of an automaton; there is no computability assumption on the higher cells of the model. This proves both converses in the finite-countermodel criterion. In fact finiteness of the set of tape-term values at the chosen object suffices.

### One old query and the recognition complexity

Take the word rules
\[
 S\rightsquigarrow aSb,\quad S\rightsquigarrow T,\quad aTb\rightsquigarrow T,
 \quad aTr\rightsquigarrow Hk,\quad lTb\rightsquigarrow Hk ,
\]
with tape delimiters \(l,r\). Use the **marked** declarations above and the old query \(Q(lSr)\). The reachable words have equally many \(a\)'s and \(b\)'s around \(S\) or \(T\). They avoid \(H\), so the query is empty even on the join. A regular invariant containing the seed must contain \(la^n S b^n r\) for every \(n\). Repetition of a state on a sufficiently long \(a\)-prefix in a recognizing automaton gives an accepted \(la^i S b^j r\) with \(i<j\). Switching to \(T\) and cancelling \(i\) pairs enables \(lTb\rightsquigarrow Hk\), a contradiction. Hence **every interpretation in every model with finitely many 1-cells fills this empty old type**. The earlier occurrence example concerned the vertices of models satisfying an extra extension condition. Here the finiteness obstruction applies to arbitrary semantic countermodels, without an extension condition. [Word-rule occurrence construction](#researcher-response-65)

For one fixed finite computad the following two disjoint sets of input configurations are recursively inseparable and each is \(\Sigma^0_1\)-complete: those with a raw (equivalently surface) answer, and those with a finite countermodel. To see this, fix a universal deterministic machine accepting output zero and stopping without acceptance on output one. Its local tape rules give the above form with one state on each side, including the endmarker extensions with a blank; only acceptance introduces \(H\). A run ending in rejection has a finite forward-closed set of reachable configurations avoiding \(H\), so it has a finite countermodel. The first set has the usual halting reduction. For the second, enumerate finite automata, testing membership of the seed, exclusion of words containing \(H\) as state, and closure under the finitely many rewrites. Closure is decidable, for example by checking all left and right tape grades in the automaton's finite transition monoid for each rule. Intersect with the language of configurations when testing these properties. This enumerates precisely the seeds with a finite countermodel. [Simulation by configuration-word rewriting, Theorem 23.4](https://www.cs.utexas.edu/~ear/cs341/automatabook/AutomataTheoryBook.pdf)

For hardness of the second set, given a machine computation \(T(z)\), construct a program which, on input \(d\), concurrently tests whether \(T(z)\) halts and searches for a regular invariant certificate at the universal starting configuration for program \(d\) on input \(d\). Return one on the first test succeeding and zero on the second succeeding, with any fixed tie convention. Take \(d\) to be the code of this constructed program and output its self-application seed. Returning zero would imply both acceptance and a verified countermodel, hence cannot occur. If \(T(z)\) halts the self-application therefore rejects and has a finite countermodel. If it never halts, a certificate would force return zero, so none exists. This is a computable many-one reduction. Likewise a recursive separator accepting the first set and rejecting the second would be contradicted by a program that on input \(d\) returns one if the separator accepts the self-application seed for \(d\), and zero otherwise, run on its own code. These are recognition results for actual old queries and their countermodels. They do not decide recognition of conservative *contexts* in Q2.

For ordinary CaTT take \(j\) bipointed suspensions of the marked presentation, its queries, and the raw conditional and accepting derivations. Suspension takes raw substitution to substitution with the two pole entries and takes an ordinary admitted head to a head on the suspended tree with the suspended positional value. Hence the conditional judgement with a new answer variable still holds. Suspending each strict countermodel \(j\) times gives the same empty fibre in the indicated iterated hom, preserving finiteness of the nonidentity cells. Conversely the suspended tape terms are evaluated by a fixed suspended binary constructor on cells of degree \(j+1\); the same two finite accumulators prove regularity in any model finite in that degree. Thus the exact inhabitation and finite-countermodel classifications hold at output \(j+4\) for each ordinary strict-sound surface separately, and for the join. In particular in degrees five and six one has explicit surface-empty boundaries whose emptiness no finite model detects. These suspensions use only the ordinary raw positive derivations and strict negative interpretations. [Ordinary suspension and forward hom](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-11)

**Validation.** The checker constructs both generating changes of each frame at the written faces. For \(S\rightsquigarrow aTb\rightsquigarrow H\) it places the accepting generator, composes the two nested raw proofs, and places the result between nonempty exterior tape words. Agda checks all four judgements in the Gray kernel and checks the suspended conditional propagation at degree five in the ordinary kernel. In nine exterior contexts it independently reads the indexed events of the grouped and ungrouped raw faces, checking that the successor mark lies between exactly the two specified 2-events. The program also verifies the left and right subset actions and their distribution over union in 768 cases using a noncommutative transformation monoid. For all 1,114 pointed unary automata through four states it checks the mismatch rewrite after pumping an \(a\)-prefix. The general classification uses the proofs above. Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_marked_computations.py --agda-dir DIR` and check `DIR/MarkedComputations.agda` and `DIR/SuspendedMarkedComputation.agda` with `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR`. [Marked computation checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_marked_computations.py)

## Researcher Response 69

**Free probes beside arbitrary concurrent point-site rules reduce to the unmarked query and scalar-rule queries.** A point-site rule here replaces one closed planar diagram by another on an empty spatial path. Both diagrams can contain nested wire components. Extend any such ordinary 3-computad by independent free 3-scalars as in [Response 67](#researcher-response-67). For the published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange, degree-wise reflection on this extension follows from reflection at its unmarked queries. For each of the other four surfaces it suffices in degree \(n\ge5\) to test the unmarked queries and the one-object scalar-rule presentations with at most \(n-4\) probe occurrences. Taken uniformly over the whole point-site class this is an equivalence, since both tests are subclasses. This removes the dependence-chain hypothesis of [Response 63](#researcher-response-63). Unmarked histories may have nontrivial higher homotopy. In particular, in degree six and above the six surfaces reflect after adding arbitrarily many probes to any unmarked point-site network passing [Response 54](#researcher-response-54)'s asphericity criterion.

There is an exact raw degree-five decision on **fixed-history probe loops** in this class. Even one probe in a fixed scalar-rule computad realizes the word problem of every tame link group, with the link specified just by the history. The examples below supply distinct positive and negative controls for the remaining interchange surfaces.

### Why a flat probe fibre injects even over a higher-dimensional base

For any ordinary 3-computad, the forgetful homotopy-fibre calculation in Response 67 up to the choice of a sorted representative uses only the planar charts and their disjoint site disks. It applies before assuming a forest. At fixed unmarked history, split the site batches and call the resulting one-probe spacetime \(M\). Its face and tie inclusions include the signed seam paths. The named fibre is the product of configurations at its assigned components.

There is a homotopy section whenever every occupied component meets an end where a probe of that colour can be placed. Choose the positions for these probes in their actual faces at the **fixed** initial or final diagrams, distinct when necessary. Precompose the variable unmarked history by a fixed initial interval carrying its assigned probes and postcompose by the analogous final interval. On erasure this just inserts constant end collars and contracts back by reparametrization. This works on all families, or by raw composition with the fixed probe histories and the raw unit fits. At the chosen base history join the section configurations to the prescribed ones in the fibre: ordered configurations of a connected open 3-manifold are path-connected. The section makes all connecting homomorphisms into that fibre zero. In particular its groups inject in the total groups for every positive degree, including the fundamental group. This statement permits allocations involving both ends.

For the all-gate flattening, **every** flat probe can be placed in an initial collar. At each regular time the plane minus the finite list of gates is connected; at any critical time there are still positions outside all the site disks connecting its two neighboring slices. The flat movie thus has one component and all its probes have the same object. The section uses actual fixed-end composition and does not require a continuous choice of centers for a family of knotted or braided drawings.

To compare the original and flat fibres, choose one serial refinement of the specified *unmarked* history. The isotopy of Response 66 identifies its configuration fibre with the original one.
