For point sites, a continuing face is in bijection with its continuation across a use, and internal faces of a disappearing or appearing closed port terminate or start there. Consequently each component of the original one-probe movie traverses an interval of gaps. Apply the construction of [Response 59](#researcher-response-59) on this interval: retain a core gate of each directly enclosed child; when the interval has an interior endpoint, retain an enclosing gate and use the punctured **sphere**, so that the peripheral around the full appearing or disappearing port contracts. The projection can then be extended constantly past that endpoint. Between endpoints an entire connected child is replaced together or its chosen gate persists, and seam paths carry its representative. These arguments use point sites and a single chosen **representative history**, with no incidence requirement between consecutive events. They give a homotopy left inverse to the one-probe inclusion on each component. By the punctured-cover and configuration argument of Response 63, its coordinatewise comparison is injective on all positive groups. When several source components land in the same flat component, project away the probes in the other components. These comparisons jointly detect the source fibre. The reduction to at most \(n-4\) probes uses the coordinate-projection bound of the same response.

Now let an original degree-\(n\) difference class admit a surface witness. Erasing probes tests its projection to the unmarked base. If that projection is raw trivial, exactness supplies a fibre preimage. Flatten the witness and take the occurrence lifts at its common lower source before specializing the selected probes, as in Response 63. If the specialized flat queries have raw fillers, the image of the preimage is zero in each specialized **total** group. The initial-collar section makes it zero in the corresponding flat **fibre** as well, so joint detection kills the preimage. This proves the reduction. The flat scalar-rule tests reflect for the six surfaces by [Response 48](#researcher-response-48). Moreover the same argument without a surface assumption makes the inclusion of each original named probe fibre injective on positive groups: an element dying in the original total dies in each flat total. This includes an original probe face unavailable at either end.

For a loop exhibited in such a fibre, degree-five raw nullity can therefore be tested independently on each probe's loop in its own component of \(M\). The pair diagonals have codimension three, so removing them from the product does not alter its fundamental group. A finite positioned movie gives a finite polyhedral model: use compact polygonal regular drawings, disjoint port disks and piecewise linear representatives of the finitely many seam exchanges in a box with end collars. Triangulate their trace, take a regular neighborhood of the omitted trace and crop the exterior collars. Each complementary component has the homotopy type of a compact 3-manifold with boundary, with the same fundamental group as its open interior. A triangulation gives a finite group presentation and an edge word for each loop. These groups are residually finite. Dovetail ordinary relation proofs with finite permutation representations separating the word; one side terminates. This is a decision for specified fibre loops, with no assertion of an efficient bound or a decision on arbitrary loops projecting to the unmarked base. [Aschenbrenner and Friedl, residual properties of 3-manifold groups](https://arxiv.org/abs/1004.3619)

### The two-particle birth and death computad

Use one object, a 2-scalar \(a\), three 3-generators consisting of a birth from the empty diagram to \(aa\), a death from \(aa\) to the empty diagram, and a free probe. At empty 2-ends take \(b\) pair births, an arbitrary signed braid \(\beta\) of the \(2b\) outputs, and \(b\) deaths consuming consecutive pairs in the final reading. Choose disjoint disks within each birth or death batch. In the one-probe spacetime, a two-output birth joins two strands in a cap. Locally its disk with the two outgoing strands has the same complement up to homotopy as a rounded cap: thicken the disk in a small ball to a 3-ball with the two outgoing tubes attached, which is a relative regular neighborhood of a boundary-parallel joining arc. At a death use the reversed construction. Between them the tubes follow the signed seam. Thus the probe space is homotopy equivalent to the complement in **three-space** of the plat closure \(L_\beta\). All tame links admit such a presentation. In particular \(\pi_1(\mathbb R^3-L_\beta)\) injects in the raw degree-five difference group of this fixed computad at the specified history. The finite-end placement is included via the section above. One can use unlabelled copies of the same declarations: a raw nullhomotopy of a fibre loop lifts from its chosen initial occurrence names. [Birman, plat presentations and cap meridians](https://doi.org/10.4153/CJM-1976-030-1)

There is also a one-probe higher obstruction for each **nonempty** plat. The large sphere enclosing the whole link is nonzero in \(H_2(\mathbb R^3-L_\beta)\), since its image after retaining only one point of the link has degree one. Fibre injectivity makes its based class a raw degree-six nonfilling. More explicitly the Hopf link is represented in the unit 3-sphere in \(\mathbb C^2\) by \(z_1z_2=0\). Its complement is the product of its two phase circles with \(0<|z_1|<1\). To pass to three-space puncture this product at infinity. In its universal cover this removes one point per deck element of \(\mathbb Z^2\) from open three-space. The small spheres about these lifts freely generate second homology by excision and hence second homotopy by simple connectivity. The one-probe degree-six subgroup there is the additive group \(\mathbb Z[\mathbb Z^2]\), even though its degree-five group is abelian. For these scalar-rule examples Response 48 excludes fillers on the six surfaces at every raw nonfilling.

The solver computes the degree-five test directly. With meridians \(x_i\) at the first strand of each birth pair, initialize the second strand to \(x_i^{-1}\). Transport this list along the signed braid by the Artin action of Response 58; impose the reverse-order product of each final pair as a relator. Van Kampen on the two cap rows gives exactly this presentation. It also follows by taking the two gluing maps from the middle punctured plane into the two trivial tangle complements. The missing point at infinity leaves the fundamental group unchanged. For a fixed exterior probe the same calculation works on replacing each strand by a closed connected gate core with its contents. Each core is one hole in that face; a birth or death inserts or consumes its two exterior children together, and the projected packet braid gives their actual meridian transport. This yields the same van Kampen presentation even if those children enclose further faces.

For two birth pairs use the middle crossing \(\sigma_2\) and the probe commutator \([x_1,x_2]\). At exponent two the Hopf plat has cap relator \(x_1x_2^{-1}x_1^{-1}x_2\), so the commutator fills raw, and on all ten surfaces. At exponent three the commutator survives: the two generators acting by transpositions \((1\;2)\) and \((0\;1)\) on sheets \(0,1,2\) satisfy both computed death relators but the word sends sheet 0 to 2. This rules out raw filling and filling on the six surfaces. For the other four it gives a specific test with zero abelian winding; a surface filler there would refute reflection. The finite permutation action certifies the **raw** obstruction only.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_plat_probes.py --rice CHECKOUT` with a built Rice catt-strict checkout. The checker recovers eighteen cap words by pushing an actual fifth strand around each death pair and conjugating by the lifted signed seam. Five commutator queries return verified relation derivations or three-sheet certificates. It also executes 3,582 positioned exchanges on 259 decorated seams containing point and lens packets, restricting the actual braid back to one gate per core. The raw, `--su` and `--sua` checkers accept the written four-strand, three-crossing plat with a parked probe. For an individual decision, pass a JSON file such as `{"pairs":2,"braid":[2,2,2],"word":[1,2,-1,-2],"sizes":[3,1,2,4]}`. The executable decision uses ordinary relator derivations and dovetailed finite symmetric-group models with no fixed search cutoff. [Plat probe solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_plat_probes.py)

## Researcher Response 70

**All ten ordinary surfaces reflect in every degree on finite 3-computads whose 3-rules replace nonempty closed planar diagrams at points.** Each port must contain at least one generating 2-gate, and the spatial path of each site is empty. The graph and 2-declarations are arbitrary. A port can contain several disconnected components, with other components nested inside them. There are no restrictions on the number of simultaneous sites or on which subsequent rules consume the gates of a port. Every parallel old boundary in degree five and above on this 3-skeleton has a raw filler. The same conclusion at an individual supported computation only needs the stated port condition on its uses. With arbitrary additional declarations first in degree \(h\ge4\), the global result gives reflection through \(h+1\) by the higher-attachment theorem.

This settles a case of Q1(b,c) beyond the scalar nonempty-port theorem: a 3-rule can consume whole wire-and-gate components, act inside a component created by another rule, or repartition the gates of several ports. With arbitrary additional free 3-scalars, the two published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange still reflect in all degrees by [Response 69](#researcher-response-69).

### The cubical complex at fixed ends

Use named 2-gate and 3-event occurrences in the change-index model as in [Response 50](#researcher-response-50). The gate-incidence network is read after flattening all gates to scalar particles. Every use consumes and produces a gate. Each gate lies on a full incidence path from an initial gate to a final one, including length-zero stationary paths. Repeated colours do not produce a permutation ambiguity over the fixed ends: the initial gate names determine the uses consuming them, and the name of a use and its output-port index determine its new gate names. Induct along the finite network. Equivalently use the occurrence cover with these records at the common raw boundary, as in [round-1 Response 82](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-82).

At a cut use the groupoid of positioned planar diagrams with those live gates. At a cube use the planar groupoid with one point marker per active rule plus its spectator gates. Face maps insert the actual positioned ports. Include only components and cubes realizing the network. An individual cube has different order ideals at all its corners. The schedule coefficient is the homotopy pullback of these groupoids with the seam and fixed-end paths, and the raw arrow space is the homotopy colimit over batch splittings, by the change-index calculation of Responses 50 and 54. In particular, mere incomparability of the gate records is not a simultaneous-chart test.

Trivialize the groups at cuts and cubes simultaneously as follows. Flatten into the named scalar-rule presentation and then use the full-incidence-path substitution of round-1 Response 82. Give each full path a distinct scalar colour. A gate maps to the packet of paths through it; a use maps to a planar path between the two partitions of the same packet. This gives a map of the cubical families, including their fillings, into the groupoid \(Y\) of configurations of these names. On a cut the map on loops is the positioned gate braid followed by nonempty block cabling. It is injective: restriction to one strand per gate inverts the cabling on that braid, and the gate reading is faithful. On a cube first insert its input ports. Every active marker then contributes a nonempty packet of gates, so restriction to a gate from each packet similarly detects the joint braid. This applies to arbitrary marker and spectator placements. [Positioned braid faithfulness](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-79)

Pull back the universal cover of \(Y\) to each groupoid. All the resulting components are contractible, since the positioned planar groupoids are 1-types and their loop maps inject. Use these components to label the vertices and cubes, with faces induced by the lifted characteristic maps. Call this cube complex \(U\). As in round-1 Response 82, fixing a starting lift splits the schedule space by its ending lift. For each such pair the coefficients become the discrete sets of composable lifted cubes: cube parameters and paths in a matching component are contractible. Refinements are their cubical faces. Thus the resulting raw space is the order complex of directed cube chains, with the specified vertices. We verify the link condition for \(U\) with the original wire geometry.

### A common corner for several closed ports

First work at a component of a cut without the cover. A germ of a named edge there specifies its sign, its rule and the individual occurrences of its expanded port. In a drawing of this cut the port is a nonempty collection of whole floating components in **one** parent face, together with *everything* enclosed in those selected components. Here the collection refers to its outermost components; each of them can enclose further faces. The port disk meets no exterior wire. Both the oriented wire-and-gate diagram of its contents and the parent face must match the prescribed port. A closed port cannot select a gate on an anchored wire.

If edge directions pairwise span squares, they are distinct independent event directions, their gate sets are disjoint, and no selected port takes its disk inside another selected port. The last assertion includes spectator-looking faces inside a selected component: every gate inside them belongs to its port, and every other selected port contains a gate. Consequently there is a simultaneous joint-chart **component** at this corner. In each parent face group the specified disjoint sets of sibling components into small disks. Configurations of finitely many named siblings in a connected face are connected, so they can be moved to prescribed disjoint disks, carrying their enclosed diagrams. Perform the grouping inside unaffected ancestors as well. Replace each grouped port by its point marker. This also proves uniqueness of the component: the unchanged anchored skeleton and its incidence orders stay fixed, the unchanged floating cores and all their face allocations stay fixed, and the marker of each port occupies its specified parent face. A planar drawing in that component is recovered by inserting the prescribed diagrams. This uses the planar classification by anchored diagram and floating components with their particular face addresses. Internal braids and the different ways of transporting these groups to disks are accounted for by the **lifts** at the corner. [Planar component groupoids](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-164)

Choose a grouped representative of this whole corner. Let \(G\) be its named positioned loop group. For direction \(i\), the image \(H_i\) of the chart with that single port collapsed is the image of a retraction on \(G\). On a positioned cut loop forget the wires and gates of that port except for a chosen representative gate on an outermost core, retained as a **point** at the parent object. Keep every spectator wire and gate. This projects to a legal loop at the collapsed chart: on each drawing slice erase the internal wires and count the surviving wires to the left of a retained row. A switch of two retained rows preserves its disjointness inequality under this projection; an erased row contributes no switch. No spectator was inside the selected components, and this remains true during the loop by planar transport. This is the closed-port path restriction of [Response 55](#researcher-response-55), even when the selected siblings separate along the path. Insert the whole original port again at the retained point. On flattened gate braids the resulting endomorphism \(p_i\) gives the representative strand the entire packet size and the other strands of that block size zero. It fixes exactly \(H_i\), since restriction splits insertion. In particular an internal braid of two children inside a consumed lens does not survive just because both have the same enclosing gate.

For disjoint ports their restriction and insertion commute, as is also seen by their signed block cablings on disjoint coordinates and positioned braid faithfulness. The image of \(\prod_{i\in J}p_i\) is exactly the group of the joint chart inserted at the corner: restrict all those ports, then insert all of them. It follows that this group is \(\bigcap_{i\in J}H_i\). These conclusions include toggling a mixture of incoming and outgoing edges by using the actual port on the chosen corner side.

At a particular lift of that vertex, an incident lifted direction of type \(i\) specifies a left coset \(g_iH_i\) in \(G\). This is the component calculation for pulling the universal corner cover back to its incident chart. The cosets of a pair spanning a lifted square meet. For completeness, suppose \(x\) lies in the first several cosets. Translate them by \(x^{-1}\) to the corresponding subgroups. The next translated coset \(C=x^{-1}g_jH_j\) meets each of these subgroups if the original cosets meet pairwise. If \(w\in C\cap H_i\), then \(p_i(w)=w\) and \(p_i(H_j)\subseteq H_j\) by commutation, so \(p_i(C)\subseteq C\). Apply the product of these projections to \(x^{-1}g_j\) and multiply back by \(x\) to obtain a common element. Their whole intersection is one coset of the simultaneous chart group. Thus compatible germs span exactly one lifted cube, including uniqueness for a pair. This proves that the links of \(U\) are simplicial and flag.

Each component now has a CAT(0) cubical universal cover. Lift directed cube chains starting at one fixed vertex once more and fix the ending vertex. A directed edge path between the two lifts is a geodesic, because each step increases the number of executed events by one and an undirected edge changes it by at most one. All edge geodesics between the same two vertices are therefore directed. Their cubical interval is the ideal complex of the finite poset of separating hyperplanes. All directed cube chains in that interval are the schedules of this poset, with contractible nerve by the convex strict event-time region. Conversely every cube of a directed chain belongs to the interval by serializing its directions. This is the fixed-end argument of round-1 Response 82, now with the verified positioned link condition. The raw arrow space is homotopy discrete. The globe comparison gives the raw fillers at the asserted boundaries, and raw 4-tracks have reverses in a 3-computad. The ordinary lower-degree and higher-attachment results give the stated conservativity bounds. [Cubical interval description](https://arxiv.org/abs/1101.2428)

One additional raw consequence with probes is useful in degrees six and above. At a serial representative each occupied one-probe component follows a face over an interval as in Response 69. All its cut and tie spaces are punctured planes. On a continuing face the tie-to-port maps are injective on fundamental groups: at a site in that face the peripheral maps to the product of its nonempty list of outermost child meridians, a primitive word, and spectator meridians stay independent. A site elsewhere transports this face unchanged or changes the interior of a hole whose core persists. At an internal end of the face's life the adjacent external tie does not meet that component. Thus the graph-of-spaces criterion of [Response 61](#researcher-response-61) makes the component aspherical. With at most one probe per occupied component the entire fibre is aspherical, and its unmarked base component is contractible by the theorem just proved. Every such degree-six-or-higher boundary fills raw, also for the four interchange surfaces.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_closed_port_links.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/ClosedPortLinks.agda` with an existing output directory. The solver accepts the jointly collapsed drawing, a nonempty positioned port per chosen marker, and a signed cut loop specifying each lifted direction. It returns a common braid or an incompatible pair. The fixture has two exterior ports and a third at a different object inside a spectator lens. One exterior port contains a lens with two mobile children and a separate point. Across 128 comparisons the projected words agree with actual positioned loops on the collapsed drawings re-expanded by the whole ports. Nine compatible and eighteen incompatible triples give the same verdict in all six orders. A commutator with zero linking on every pair of gate strands prevents a simultaneous lift. Agda checks a compound closed-port change composed beside a rule acting inside another lens at its own object. ## Researcher Response 71

**A path can contain arbitrarily many occurrences of the same moving rule and still admit an exact reduction to ordinary hom computads one dimension lower.** For finite 3-computads satisfying the marked-edge condition below, each of the ten individual ordinary surfaces is conservative in every dimension. For the join in Q1(c), conservativity on this class is equivalent to the absence of a positive planar bubble in any of the indicated hom computads. The first failure, if there is one, is in dimension five. With a marking supplied, recognition on this class is $\Pi^0_1$-complete.

### A cut for each marked occurrence

Choose marked generating edges of an ordinary computad $X$, with a label $c$ on each. Call the other edges passive. For each label choose two sets $L_c,R_c$ of passive edges. Require that both strict edge words of each declaration $g$ of degree at least two have the form $a e b$, with $e$ marked, $a$ a path in $L_c$, and $b$ a path in $R_c$. The mark must have the same label $c$ on both faces. Its edge, its object poles and the two passive paths can all change. The attaching terms themselves may be weak composites.

Between successive marks of labels $c,d$ on a path, read the passive word $h$. Call the path **protected** when each such gap satisfies
\[
              R_c\cap L_d=\varnothing
              \quad\text{or}\quad h\notin R_c^*L_d^* .
\]
In particular every one-mark path is protected. Every path is protected if all right alphabets are disjoint from all left alphabets. More generally the all-path hypothesis is decidable on the finite graph: for each pair of marked edges with intersecting alphabets test for a connecting passive path in $R_c^* L_d^*$. This is reachability in two copies of the graph, with a transition from the first to the second at each vertex. Include the empty connecting path and allow the two occurrences to use the same generating edge.

Grade paths by a category with the vertices of $X$ as objects. Use the passive arrows and formal arrows $c_{x,y}:x\to y$ for every label and ordered vertex pair. Impose $l c_{x,y}=c_{x',y}$ for $l:x'\to x$ in $L_c$, and $c_{x,y}r=c_{x,y'}$ for $r:y\to y'$ in $R_c$. A marked edge maps to the formal arrow with its label and poles. All generating 2-relations respect this grading. Orient the two relations towards absorption of a passive letter. Forgetting vertex tags gives a terminating confluent word reduction: disjoint moves commute, either order in $l c r$ gives $c$, and either absorption of the middle letter in $c p d$ gives $cd$. Thus the ordered labels and each remaining passive word are invariants of the grade. The residual word in a gap is empty exactly when $h\in R_c^*L_d^*$. At a protected grade the overlap $c p d$ with $p\in R_c\cap L_d$ cannot occur in any representative, even one reached by expanding letters. The other overlaps commute with their vertex tags. Termination and local confluence on this class give a unique **typed** normal form. Every factor of a protected grade is protected, since adjoining words at the ends leaves all gaps internal to that factor unaffected.

Take the factorization interval $X_w$ of Response 32 at a protected grade $w$. Its endpoints are the frames $(1,w)$ and $(w,1)$. Between them the interval is a sequence of single residual passive edges and one computad for each occurrence $c_{x,y}$ in the typed normal form. Consecutive pieces share just their joint vertex. The piece for $c_{x,y}$ is as follows.

- Its left vertices are $L_c$-paths $a:x\to z$. Its right vertices are $R_c$-paths $b:t\to y$. These are disjoint copies, even for $x=y$. The two empty paths are the entry and exit.
- A left edge appends a passive letter and a right edge consumes the first passive letter. A marked generating edge $e:z\to t$ of label $c$ gives an edge from $a$ to $b$ for each such pair. A higher declaration $g$ of label $c$ with object poles $z,t$ occurs with those same indices. Its boundaries are its written boundaries placed at that frame. In particular a crossing edge in a pole $A e B$ uses the indices $aA,Bb$.

Here is the verification at *all* interval frames. Expand the factors of a factorization into words and put a cut between them. At a gap with nonempty residue there is a unique maximal absorbable right run before the residue and left run after it. At a gap with empty residue the split $R_c^* L_d^*$ is unique by disjointness. The outermost runs have just one possible absorbing mark. Inside a left run the part already read is a passive suffix of the **prefix** factor, and the unread part is absorbed by the mark in the suffix factor. Inside a right run the unread part survives as the passive prefix of the **suffix** factor. In a residual word the frame retains its two literal fragments. These are exactly the vertex addresses above: the number of marks read locates the occurrence, and the typed normal forms of both factors retain its indicated path, including its endpoints. At the split of two runs both are absorbed on their respective sides, giving the common joint. Conversely each address specifies both factors by writing the completed portion of the normal form, the retained path and the remaining portion, using the marked arrows with the prescribed poles. The formal arrows for all vertex pairs ensure this also indexes vertices whose passive path has no actual crossing continuation. Placing one generator at a frame now gives exactly the listed edges and higher declarations. The recursive placement of its attaching terms remains in this piece, by the same calculation applied at its own single-mark grade. This proves the interval description. The overlap exclusion matters: if $p:0\to1$ belongs to both alphabets, absorbing $p$ on opposite sides in $c_{0,0} p d_{1,1}$ gives two different seam vertices in irreducible typed words.

### Raw homs and the surface calculation

Let $B_c(x,y)$ be the ordinary computad with an object $[a,e,b]$ for each crossing edge of the piece and a generator $[a,g,b]$ of degree $\dim(g)-1$ for each higher copy. Project its actual attaching type by the indexed cut of round-1 Response 14. The two passive graphs here are trees: the path from the entry to a left vertex and the path from a right vertex to the exit are unique. Thus the indexing paths in that cut are exactly $a,b$. For instance a crossing column preceded by the stationary path $A$ and followed by $B$ sends its crossing labels to the indices $aA,Bb$. This prescription applies recursively to the actual heads in its faces. The raw entry-to-exit hom maps by a weak equivalence to the free ordinary realization of $B_c(x,y)$. [Indexed cut and fixed-hom equivalence](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-14)

For clarity, the raw hom across the whole interval maps by a weak equivalence to the **cartesian product** of these free ordinary realizations. To verify the product step in the ordinary enriched realization, glue two successive parts $D,E$ at the sink $z$ of $D$ and source $z$ of $E$. These are cellular pushouts along an object. There are no backwards homs across it. A mixed hom from $u$ to $v$ is the free composite of $D(u,z)$ and $E(z,v)$ over the joint endohom. Indeed composites entering the second part have a single passage through the joint. The mixed hom carries the left and right actions by the two parts, and its defining composition balances the two actions at $z$. In the enriched pushout the universal such bimodule is given by their relative tensor: maps out are balanced pairings, since all actions before or after the passage compose within one part. The derived balancing uses the two-sided bar with terms $D(u,z)\times \mathrm{End}(z)^k\times E(z,v)$. The endohom is equivalent to the unit: any path at the joint in its own part is confined to that vertex, where only the free point acts. Thus this bar is equivalent to the constant product. Pure homs within either part acquire no new passages back into that part. Iteration gives the assertion, with a terminal factor for each passive edge. To identify the actual projections, clamp all pieces other than the selected one to its nearest endpoint. This can be done by a raw substitution, choosing fillers recursively for the resulting parallel faces on a point. It is the identity on the selected piece. In the balanced pairing this evaluates the other factors in the terminal hom and composes by units. Composing the clamp with the indexed cut therefore realizes the corresponding product projection. This last identification lets us use the product equivalence on prescribed raw faces. [Ordinary enriched path products](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-12) · [Raw realization of hom attachments](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-6)

For an old query inhabited on a surface, first place the derivation at its canonical frame by Response 32. Clamp and project to each marked occurrence. For insertion the projected equations are insertion equations. For the full binary surface the indexed cut also uses unary disc removal; its addition preserves old inhabitation in each dimension. For their join $J$ the projected derivation is itself a $J$-derivation. In detail the substitution on a labelled spine is the same for both sets of rules. Stationary labels evaluate to paths. The binary generator calculation uses the lower word and disc equations, and the insertion calculation uses lower insertion, including disc and endo removal. In a mixed derivation the arguments and boundary equalities of a last rule have already been projected by induction; its generic calculation takes place in this same lower algebra. Ordinary strict evaluation checks the bound arity condition there.

One ingredient of the root insertion calculation merits checking for mixed typings: disc trivialization still holds over $J$. Induct on output degree and then on the structure of the term on a disc. Normalize its maximal arguments by this induction to the variables or identity towers specified by their strict disc values. The faces fix the other entries up to conversion. Prune an identity at a maximal position whenever its head is not itself the identity being computed. Each pruning reduces the number of positions and retains the surviving labels up to conversion; newly exposed faces are already classified by the lower-degree induction. The identity head itself acts by identity on its classified argument. With only variable maximal arguments left, a labelling by disc variables is a subdisc inclusion: a branch would require two consecutive composable disc variables at its branching level. On that subdisc the generic head faces have smaller degree and trivialize by induction. Thus disc removal in the degree of its top variable, or endo removal above that degree, computes the indicated strict value. This argument uses pruning and congruence in $J$ on the actual generic type, without assuming a mixed term has a pure insertion typing. It supplies the disc calculation of round-1 Response 12 for the last-rule induction. If each projected old query has a raw filler, their tuple gives existence at the projected faces, and local essential surjectivity of the raw hom equivalence at the specified iterated boundary gives a raw filler upstairs. Refold it into $X$. The graph-only case contributes a discrete path hom. Degree-zero and degree-one existence just asks for a vertex or a path. [Two-sided placement](#researcher-response-32) · [Rule projection](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-12) · [Disc removal for binary surfaces](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-15)

Conversely, a failure on a $B_c(x,y)$ gives a failure one degree higher in $X$. Use the single protected grade $c_{x,y}$. As in the necessity direction of the indexed cut, represent a homotopy inverse of the raw projection by an ordinary map from the free cofibrant lower computad into the fibrant entry-to-exit hom. Suspend the failed surface derivation and substitute its raw generator images there. Each word generator and each insertion generator suspends to the same rule in the hom, so this also works in the join. A raw filler at the resulting old boundary would give existence at the lower boundary by reflection along that weak equivalence. Folding to $X$ preserves this obstruction because any proposed raw filler there lifts back at the canonical frame. An empty indexed computad contributes no failure. Consequently, when all paths are protected, for insertion, for the full binary surface, and for $J$, the first global failure is exactly one more than the least first failure among the $B_c(x,y)$, taking $1+\infty=\infty$. The single-grade direction needs no hypothesis on any other grade. [Suspension and necessity at an indexed hom](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-14)

### Dimensions, recognition and persistence

In a 3-computad the indexed computads have dimension at most two. The separate ordinary surfaces reflect in every such computad by the dimension-two theorem, using finite dependency-closed subcomputads for a finite derivation. The full binary result gives all its subsurfaces; strict units follow from insertion. For $J$, Response 1 gives the exact criterion on each indexed computad: no positive positioned 2-history from the empty path at an object back to that same empty path. For an infinite indexed computad, a positive history still has a finite spelling, and the count-and-permutation obstruction to a raw filler interprets *all* its 2-generators. Conversely every finite dependency-closed subcomputad of a bubble-free one is bubble-free and the lower reflection proof applies there. A positive history gives the lower failure at degree four and no lower failure is possible below four. The shift proves the degree-five claim. Enumerating indexed generators by the lengths of their passive paths and dovetailing over finite positioned histories semidecides failure. Hardness already holds on bipointed suspensions of finite ordinary 2-computads: mark every edge with the same label and take both alphabets empty. The indexed computad at the suspension poles is the original one. [Individual surfaces in dimension two](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-10) · [Joined criterion and hardness](#researcher-response-1)

A bad indexed history in the 3-skeleton also precludes all-dimensional reflection for $J$ in **every finite higher extension**, even one that violates the marking condition. Choose its object $[a,e,b]$ and an actual bracketed raw path $p$ for $a e b$. The cut of $1_p$ is a raw identity on that object (or a structural representative of the empty path). Fit the ends of the positive planar history to this literal lower 1-cell by raw path comparisons. The resulting lower 2-cell has positive generating 2-count. Local essential surjectivity for the raw hom equivalence at $1_p,1_p$ produces a raw 3-cell between those literal identities whose projection is equivalent to the lower 2-cell. Strict evaluation in the free strict 2-category preserves this count across equivalence: every 3-cell there is an identity. The raw 3-cell must use a generating 3-declaration, since the cut of any expression in the 2-skeleton uses only the 1-skeleton of the indexed computad in its strict value. Folding preserves the occurrence. This gives exactly the positive raw bubble to which Response 4 applies in the extended context. [Persistence under finite higher extension](#researcher-response-4)

For Q3(b,c), in arbitrary finite rank assume each generating 3-cell has at least one generating 2-event in its ordinary strict 2-source. Then each local 2-generator has a nonempty input word: every such event crosses the mark and projects to one indexed generating edge. The dual hypothesis on all targets works as well; the orientation can be chosen separately in each indexed computad. Response 36 now gives the individual lower results through degree five, and the cut gives reflection at every protected query through degree six. The unrestricted questions still include contexts where a higher rule changes several marked occurrences at once, as well as the directed kernels of Q1(a). [One-sided dimension-five theorem](#researcher-response-36)

For a one-object example take passive loops $l,r$, a marked loop $f$ with wings $\{l\},\{r\}$, and rules $\alpha:lf\to fr$, $\beta:fr\to lf$. Put $t=\alpha;\beta$ and add a 3-cell $t\to t;t$. Every indexed 2-rule consumes a nonempty word, so all indexed computads are bubble-free and the join reflects. On $(lf)^n$ the same generating rules can act in all $n$ blocks. Adjoining $1_{lf}\to t$ and $t\to1_{lf}$ gives a positive bubble at the indexed object $[l,f,1]$ and the first joined failure moves to degree five.

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_marked_blocks.py --agda-dir DIR` with an existing directory, then `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/MarkedBlocks.agda`. Across words through length six the checker compares the frame and the independently read block address in both directions at 66,073 positions on 4,392 protected grades. A two-object test checks unique typed normal forms on 2,511 paths and realizes the two seam choices in the overlapping example. Positioned rule moves give exactly the cube edges for one through seven copies of $lf$. Agda checks the projected bubble at its specified identity faces, the projected positive-input rule, and actual raw spatial composites of the bubble on up to three consecutive interval pieces. [Frame and typed-boundary checker](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_marked_blocks.py)

## Researcher Response 72

**All ten ordinary surfaces reflect in every degree for mixtures of nonempty closed point-site rules and the following rules on spatial wires.** At each wired site require the condition below on **each** written 2-port, with at least one spatial wire at the rim of its disk. The two ports may have different internal graphs. This includes repeated many-to-many changes between branched diagrams on the same wires; no private gate colours or separation of the different rules' supports is required. Every old boundary in degree five and above on such a 3-computad fills raw. Adding arbitrary independent free 3-scalars preserves all-degree reflection for the two published surfaces and the four binary surfaces omitting interchange. For any of the four containing interchange, in degree \(n\ge5\) the class with probes reduces to scalar rules with both ports nonempty together with at most \(n-4\) occurrences of free probes.

For a wired port, all its spatial boundary wires must meet one connected component of its gates and wires, called its core; in particular no wire passes unchanged through the disk. The other components, if any, must be in **bounded internal faces** of this core, allowing further nesting. Require two terminal gates \(a,b\) on the core. Every boundary input ends at \(a\), every boundary output starts at \(b\), and every core gate is on a directed path from \(a\) to \(b\). Paths of length zero are allowed, as are \(a=b\) and an empty list of inputs or outputs. Use the nonempty closed-port condition of [Response 70](#researcher-response-70) at point sites. These are finite tests on positioned diagrams; they also apply to a supported computation using just such ports. The conclusion through \(h+1\) with further arbitrary declarations first in degree \(h\ge4\) holds for the probe-free global condition.

### Simultaneous contraction on the spatial wires

Use the named cubical complex and full-incidence-path cover of Response 70. The cover kills the cut and cube groups for the same reason as before: flattening reads the positioned gate braid, each site expands to a nonempty list of gates, and every named gate is on a full path of 3-event incidences. The extra issue is the corner link: recognizing the same gates after flattening is insufficient evidence that they can be contracted on the actual wires.

At a cut, transport all specified edge germs to one ordinary drawing. A wired port exposes its particular core gates and everything inside the port's bounded internal faces. From just after its first gate until its last, the running core wires are a nonempty interval if one includes its enclosed contents between the extreme core wires. To see why an exterior row cannot lie strictly inside this span, take a point between two such wires in a horizontal slice. Through each wire choose a directed path from the first to the last gate. The path up one and back down the other has winding number of absolute value one about that point, by crossing the horizontal ray at that slice. Thus it is separated from the rim by the core. Wires or gates in the intervening regions belong to the port, including components wholly enclosed there. No exterior wire can enter or leave a core gate mid-block. The same argument applies in any other reading of the given corner, since incidence and the face addresses of its floating components transport with the drawing.

Consequently one can gather the port at its first row using legal planar switches: bring each later port row leftwards in the temporal reading past the intervening exterior rows, in port order. The exterior rows operate on the left or right of the running block, with the offset changed by the ordinary switch rule. This works across forks and joins, even when several interior rows can exchange with each other. The grouped block contracts to its site with the specified rim wires. For several pairwise square-compatible directions, their gate sets are disjoint. Grouping and contracting one wired block leaves each of the other wired cores and its enclosed contents intact. In particular it cannot put an exterior gate in one of their internal faces. Closed point ports are whole sibling subtrees, with their contents; pairwise compatibility precludes a second port inside any selected subtree. One may contract all wired blocks, then group the point ports in their respective remaining parent faces. The simultaneous chart component is unique: the ordered rim attachments and ports fix the replacement vertices on the anchored or floating core, and the spectator face addresses and the collapsed point markers determine the remaining planar component. This constructs the common downstairs cube; directions inducing different transports to it still have to pass the lifted test.

For a wired block choose its first core gate as braid representative. In the product description of a named planar loop by its sibling braids, contraction preserves the motion of the core to which the marker belongs. Its outer face sectors and the corresponding sibling lists are unaltered: in the disk the connected core meets all the rim legs, so each outside sector persists, and the extra complementary faces are internal. The factors for motions confined inside the contracted block are forgotten. More explicitly, in the flattened gate braid retain every spectator strand and one strand of the wired core. An exterior sibling motion acts on this representative exactly as on the corresponding marker, by unframed cabling of its containing component. Sibling motions in surviving faces give the same positioned factors after contraction. Every internal factor loses all its moving strands. These are all generators of the named planar loop group by the sibling braid calculation. Hence restriction gives an actual loop class of the collapsed chart; expanding the marker again gives its retraction on the corner group. On ambient braids this endomorphism cables the representative by the port size and erases the rest of that port. This reasoning covers an anchored block (whose core itself contributes no floating motion) and a block lying on a floating spectator lens. [Named positioned loop groups](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-164)

The endomorphisms commute for disjoint ports and their product retracts to the simultaneously inserted chart. The coset argument of Response 70 therefore makes the lifted links flag. Its CAT(0) interval argument gives homotopy-discrete fixed-end arrow spaces, proving the probe-free assertions at the written boundaries.

### Comparing a probe during a change of its enclosing wire diagram

For the probe argument alone the two terminal gates are **unnecessary**. Suppose at every nonpoint site that each port has a connected gate core meeting all its boundary wires, with any further components only in bounded internal faces of the core and no through wire. At point sites allow arbitrary closed ports. On this larger class the unmarked query and the all-gate scalar-rule tests jointly detect differences with free probes, as in [Response 69](#researcher-response-69). Thus for the six indicated surfaces it suffices to establish reflection on the unmarked queries. Here the argument concerns the probe fibre over one chosen unmarked history, so it does not require simultaneous contraction of several wired blocks.

In the site disk a connected core meeting all rim legs has exactly one complementary sector at each open rim interval, prior to any identifications outside the disk. Distinct intervals are separated by paths in the core joining boundary points; equivalently, first take an embedded spanning tree containing all the boundary legs, then add its remaining edges. No enclosed decoration changes the outer sectors. Replacing the marker star by this port thus preserves the equivalence relation on the continuing faces outside the disk. Every other port face is internal and starts or ends on that side. For a given face through regular cuts the life interval calculation of Response 59 still applies.

To give the one-probe inclusion into the flat spacetime a left inverse on a selected component, keep one gate **incident to the selected face** on each directly enclosed child core. If its life has an internal endpoint also keep a gate incident to the face on its outer boundary and compactify the target plane to a sphere as in Response 59. Such a face is bounded away from the exterior of the drawing: at the internal endpoint it lies inside the changing disk and the continuing sector bijections preserve contact with the fixed exterior. Its outer boundary belongs to one connected wire-and-gate core, which can be anchored. Begin with any such choices at the first available cut and transport them across seams.

At a wired change, representatives outside the disk persist. If a chosen boundary gate in the disk changes while the face continues, retain the marker at the tie, then choose a gate on the new core incident to that same rim sector. For a surviving sector the only core inside the disk available to carry such a representative is the core meeting the rim. Its enclosed decorations are invisible from that sector. Thus at most one selected puncture needs this replacement, and on both sides the retained site expands to exactly one retained puncture. If no representative changes, retain none in the disk. A continuing child cannot be born in such a sector, since all the other port components are in internal faces. These restrictions intertwine the punctured-plane or punctured-sphere comparisons with port expansion up to isotopy inside the disk. At point sites use the old rule: a site in the face substitutes the list of representatives of its outermost children; replacement of the whole enclosing component ends the life of a face inside it.

At an internal endpoint all of the selected children and the outer boundary representative lie in the **one** changed port; this is why adjacency to the moving face was required of the outer representative. The flat site meridian around the entire list is null in the punctured sphere by the peripheral relation, and all spectator meridians map trivially. This extends the projection constantly over the unavailable side. On available gaps and ties the composite from the chosen original face is an equivalence, since one puncture lies in each complementary hole and the added outer puncture, when used, lies outside its outer boundary. Taking the interval homotopy colimit supplies the one-probe left inverse. Compatible choices of homotopies of the edge maps suffice on this subdivided interval.

The comparison of spacetime complements is the orientation-preserving open inclusion obtained by erasing wires and keeping gate points and the same critical disks. [Response 63](#researcher-response-63) lifts the injection to configurations of any number of probes, testing one component and at most \(n-4\) names at a time. The flat initial-collar section from Response 69 injects each specialized flat fibre group into its total group, including for empty-port changes. Erase the probes to test the original base class, then apply these flat tests to a fibre preimage as in that response. This proves the stated joint detection. For the two-terminal subclass the unmarked groups already vanish. Its flattenings have nonempty input and output on every structural use, and the six-surface scalar-rule theorem applies. Conversely the nonempty scalar-rule presentations with the indicated free probes are themselves examples of the subclass, so the bounded reduction for the four interchange surfaces is an equivalence when taken uniformly.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_two_terminal_ports.py --agda-dir DIR`, `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_closed_port_links.py`, and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/TwoTerminalPorts.agda`. The recognizer checks each side on gate occurrences, including repeated colours, directed paths, surviving wires and which faces touch the port rim. The gathering test independently enumerates 280 readings of two branched changes on adjacent wires. It executes all 560 gatherings in the two possible orders and checks the typed blocks at the resulting offsets. In the probe test a bounded face with two holes survives a replacement of its lower boundary gate by two gates and is then consumed with only the second of those gates. Actual simultaneous face maps give exactly its two available cuts. Restricting the terminal probe braid to the continuing boundary gate kills the full cap in the punctured sphere; choosing the other gate gives a nontrivial peripheral. Agda checks opposite changes between the fork/parallel-branches/join diagram and a two-gate serial diagram on adjacent spatial wires. [Wired port recognizer and probe checks](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_two_terminal_ports.py)

## Researcher Response 73

**All ten ordinary surfaces reflect in every degree on finite 3-computads under this port test.** Require both 2-faces of each generating 3-rule to contain a gate. On each face with spatial wires, every boundary wire must meet the **same connected wire-and-gate component** inside the port; no wire passes untouched from rim to rim. On an empty spatial path any nonempty closed diagram is allowed. Additional floating components may lie in *any* face of the wired component, including its exterior sectors. Every old boundary of degree at least five fills raw on this 3-skeleton. The criterion may instead be applied to the uses of a single supported computation. Under the global criterion the extension through \(h+1\) of [Response 70](#researcher-response-70) holds for further arbitrary declarations first in degree \(h\ge4\).

In particular the terminal gates of [Response 72](#researcher-response-72) are unnecessary. A left wire can split and its right branch merge with the right input, leaving the other branch as the left output. The right input cannot even reach the left output in this port. The issue in the proof is realizing all the proposed spatial contractions in a **progressive** drawing at once.

### The angle contribution at a rim sector

A progressive planar graph has a cyclic order and a linear list of inputs and outputs at each gate. Give the angle between consecutive darts label zero when one is incoming and one outgoing. At a source or sink label the angle around the outside of its ordered list \(+1\); all other same-direction angles have label \(-1\). Count angle *occurrences*, including both visits to a bridge. An upward plane embedding of a connected graph has angle sum \(-2\) on each internal face walk and \(+2\) on its outer walk. Conversely a plane graph with these face sums, the stated vertex counts, and the compatible edge directions admits an upward drawing with those lists. One can use the characterization for undirected plane maps with a consistent splitting: the angle data recovers the splitting starting from an exterior source, up to simultaneous reversal. The given directions are such a splitting. In particular one need not assume separately that a quotient graph obtained by the contractions is acyclic. One can subdivide edges to apply the criterion with no parallel edges; the new one-in/one-out vertices contribute zero on each side. A self-loop may likewise be subdivided into a cycle of length three before applying the criterion. [Didimo and Pizzonia, Lemma 1 and Theorem 1](https://jgaa.info/index.php/jgaa/article/download/paper68/2902/2709)

Temporarily set the floating components of a port aside. Draw the remaining connected core between two fans with bottom and top poles, one ray for each actual boundary wire in order. Add two exterior side edges from bottom to top. Apply exactly the same construction to the single marker star. Each open rim interval of the port meets one local exterior sector of the core and distinct intervals meet distinct sectors: a spanning tree reaching all rim legs already separates them in the disk. Extra core edges bound internal faces. In both framed drawings the sector gives an internal face, and the contributions at its fan poles agree dart for dart. Thus the contribution from the core along the sector walk equals that of the marker angle there. This also covers a source, a sink and a single rim leg, where a face walk may turn back along the same edge. The two side edges separate the framed unbounded face from the port. At a port with rims only at one end the single sector extending around the other end meets both side edges and is counted only once.

In an ambient component several such sectors may be in the same face. Their contributions then add, with multiplicity. Replacing any number of disjoint connected cores by their stars therefore preserves the angle sums on **every surviving face**. Each internal core face disappears with that core. The combinatorial embedding has a disk for this replacement: thicken the core with its named rim legs and fill its bounded internal faces, cutting the legs at short transverse arcs. For a genuine germ there is no spectator inside those filled faces. The resulting disks for disjoint cores can be chosen disjoint unless one lies inside a filled face of another. A pair admitting a joint square excludes that possibility. This step uses connectivity inside the disk before adding its boundary.

### The positioned corner link

Consider a family of pairwise square-compatible signed edge germs at a named cut as in Response 70. First discard the ancillary floating subtrees belonging to the selected ports, recording their owners, their positions in the written ports and their ambient face addresses. Disjointness includes their contents. A spectator cannot be inside one of these discarded subtrees. Nor can it lie in an internal face of a selected core, since a single port insertion accounts for the whole contents of such a face. Contract the selected wired cores by the disjoint disk replacements above. Outside the disks the ordered input and output lists persist; at a new marker use its own ordered lists. The local sector calculation preserves all face sums, so the result is upward drawable. For the boundary-connected part include the bottom and top fans and both side edges as above. The two side edges enclose the diagram between the heights of the two poles, so cutting the fans near the poles restores the rectangle with the prescribed bottom and top wire orders. Treat each floating connected component with edges by the same face-sum criterion on its own plane map with its specified outer face. Single point components need no angle data. Place these drawings by translation and positive scaling inside their specified parent faces in nesting order.

Closed point ports contract as in Response 70. Their selected outermost siblings can be positioned in disjoint disks in the requisite parent faces. For a wired port the recorded exterior satellites can, on re-expansion, be placed in the prescribed sectors of its own disk. These sectors inject into the local complement and map to the same ambient face addresses as before, even when two sectors have the same ambient address. The other point and wired markers keep their prescribed locations relative to the unaltered cores. Re-expanding the joint drawing gives the original named cut component: it has the same directed plane maps with the same *linear* port orders at every vertex, the same framing at the ends and the same nesting in particular faces. These decorated maps classify exchange components of progressive string diagrams. Applying the same check after expanding all but one marker gives the specified single-germ components. Conversely removing the selected contents, replacing the specified cores, and retaining the other face addresses determines this one joint component uniquely. [Delpeuch and Vicary, directed maps and disconnected diagrams, §§7–8](https://arxiv.org/pdf/1804.07832)

For the lift calculation choose one gate **on the core** of each wired block. It need not be the first gate in the written port. In a cut loop forget its ancillary satellites and their subtrees. This is the ordinary forgetting map on the sibling braid groups of the cut, including when other siblings braid around a satellite in an exterior sector. Now restrict the connected core to the one gate strand as in Response 72. Internal factors vanish; the remaining factors give exactly a positioned loop at the collapsed chart. Indeed the separated planar sibling motions generate, a pure motion of the containing component carries the representative strand with it, and all remaining occupied sectors keep their face addresses. Inserting the full port again acts on the flattened gate braid by cabling this strand by the whole ordered packet, with size zero on all other port strands. Restriction splits this insertion. Disjoint ports have commuting retractions, with joint image the simultaneously inserted group. The coset intersection argument of Response 70 lifts the joint component to a unique cube on a clique of lifted germs. Its cubical interval proof now gives homotopy-discrete fixed-end raw arrow spaces and the asserted old-type conclusions.

This proves the probe-free assertion also for exterior satellites. When all satellites are internal, the probe comparison in Response 72 applies without its terminal hypothesis; six surfaces therefore continue to reflect with arbitrary free probes on that subclass. A site on a wire can introduce a new hole in a continuing exterior face when exterior satellites are present, a case outside that comparison.

**Reproduce.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_port_angles.py --agda-dir DIR` and `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/PortAngles.agda`. The solver checks the actual framed dart walks. Across gate lists of length at most three the 1,107 nonpoint ports passing the spanning test agree with the marker at every rim sector; 1,053 of these fail the two-terminal test. It separates 11,130 ports with a through wire or multiple boundary components. Independently, all thirty positioned readings of a pair of staggered ports and an intervening wired spectator give all six grouped marker readings. A point preceding the core in a wired port braids with one child of a neighboring point port: its twist vanishes on contracting the wired site but obstructs the common lift of the two specified germs. Agda checks the actual staggered split, rebracketing, opposite-side merge, and the two spatially adjacent changes. The JSON modes recognize both faces of supplied rules or decide the given lifted corner at a grouped drawing. [Angle and corner solver](../../../tools/semistrict_surface_port_angles.py)

## Researcher Response 74

**For the ordinary join $J$ in Q1(c), the complexity of Q2 depends on the fixed graph of generating edges and on the dimension being tested.** Let $G$ be any finite directed multigraph with at least one vertex. Only the declarations above its fixed graph are input. Call a strongly connected component *branching* if it contains a vertex with two different outgoing edges internal to the component. Completeness below is under computable many-one reductions.

| Input and reflection question | Condition on $G$ | Result |
|---|---|---|
| 2-computads, all dimensions | No branching component | Decidable by inspecting the empty 1-faces of the 2-generators |
| 2-computads, all dimensions | A branching component | $\Pi^0_1$-complete |
| Arbitrary finite computads, through degree four | Acyclic | $\Sigma^0_1$-complete |
| Arbitrary finite computads, through degree four | A directed cycle, including a single loop | $\Pi^0_2$-complete |
| Arbitrary finite computads, through any fixed degree $n\ge5$ | At least one edge | $\Pi^0_2$-complete |
| Arbitrary finite computads, all dimensions | No edges | Decidable: exactly the contexts with no positive-dimensional declarations reflect |
| Arbitrary finite computads, all dimensions | At least one edge | Undecidable, already on 3-computads with a fixed 2-skeleton |

Thus a single loop separates the two bounded input problems: through four it can support uniform-halting hardness with higher attachments, while its 2-computads have an elementary all-dimensional decision. The all-dimensional upper bound on arbitrary computads with edges remains $\Pi^0_2$. The last row does not identify its exact degree.

### Closed planar histories on the fixed graph

By Response 1, failure on a 2-computad is equivalent to a nonempty positioned history from an empty path at some vertex $x$ back to that empty path. Its first move must have empty input at $x$ and its last must have empty output there. If the component of $x$ has no internal branching, it is a singleton without a loop or a simple directed cycle. Every loop at $x$ stays in this component. In the first case either of the required moves is already a scalar. In the second, the output of a birth and the input of a death at $x$ are powers of the same cycle based at $x$. For positive exponents $m,n$, use the birth $\operatorname{lcm}(m,n)/m$ times, then the death $\operatorname{lcm}(m,n)/n$ times. A zero exponent already gives a scalar. Thus the two necessary tests at the *same vertex* suffice, even with arbitrary additional rules between different vertices. [Planar criterion](#researcher-response-1)

For hardness it suffices first to encode the one-object hard instances over two loops. Use the stack code $c_i=P(PQ)^iQ$ of round-1 Response 113. From **every** starting stack each $c_i$ emits its original edge $i$ and restores the stack. In a positioned rewrite $l\,c(u)\,r\to l\,c(v)\,r$, read $l$ from the chosen initial stack, and read $r$ from the resulting stack. These two exterior outputs are the same on both sides. What lies between them is precisely the original rule $u\to v$, counted once even if a side is empty. Starting inside a code causes no exception. Every positive binary bubble therefore decodes to a positive original bubble, and encoding gives the converse. This use of the code reflects the **whole context predicate** on 2-computads, including histories not given by encoding an old expression. [Stack action and typed encoding](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-113)

Choose two internal outgoing edges from a branching vertex $x$. Follow each by a shortest return to $x$ to obtain distinct first-return paths $p,q$. Substitute them for $P,Q$ and place all rules at $x$. Any history from an empty path can move only if it starts at $x$. Inductively each visited path is a concatenation of $p,q$. Its visits to $x$ are exactly the boundaries between these paths, so even an empty-input step starts at a block boundary. Each matched nonempty rule consumes whole blocks. Cutting at the visits to $x$ recovers the binary history, preserving positivity. This proves hardness on the specified graph; enumerating finite histories gives the upper bound.

### A uniform-halting test on a single wire

For finite higher presentations, Response 35 characterizes reflection through four by two raw repairs for each elementary braid of a pair of floating decorated components of the 2-skeleton. On an acyclic graph any closed planar history at $x$ has empty spatial path on every row. Thus a connected floating component is just one generating scalar at $x$, with no bounded face. There are only finitely many tests, even if the 2-generators on nonempty paths change their lengths. Dovetail searches for all required directed witnesses. Hardness uses the one-scalar group-word construction at any chosen vertex, keeping all the other graph declarations. The raw substitution sending all vertices to the chosen one and all graph edges to its identity fixes the one-scalar presentation. The only component tests are its original ones. [Ambient component tests and one-scalar hardness](#researcher-response-35)

Here are details for the cyclic case. First work on one generating loop $f$. Use Response 37's fixed-alphabet simulation with birth $d$, death $z$, bit letters $L_0,L_1,R_0,R_1$, and heads $Q,H$. Give $d$ type $1_x\to f$, $z$ type $f\to1_x$, and all other letters type $f\to f$. For the moment remember the two original wire colours on the ports of these gates: $d$ outputs left, $z$ consumes right, the bit letters keep their own colour, and $Q,H$ go from left to right. Retain the original machine changes with their inverses and both directions of their inverse comparisons. The terminal closed component is $T=dHz$. Give its diagonal braid the two original 4-repairs. The earlier simulation makes every colour-correct chain reach $T$ up to signed moves exactly in the uniform-halting case. In the other case some fully encoded chain belongs to a signed component disjoint from $T$. [Machine rules, malformed bit codes and uniform-halting reduction](#researcher-response-37)

After identifying the wires there are additional chains. Use a fresh unary gate $X:f\to f$. For each two consecutive old gates with mismatched colours replace that pair by $X$, retaining a left endpoint $d$ or right endpoint $z$ of the pair. Thus $dz$ becomes $dXz$, $dR_i$ becomes $dX$, and two mismatched interior letters become just $X$. Include $AX\to X$ and $XA\to X$ for every unary gate $A$, including $X$. Finally include $dXz\to dHz$. Give each of these changes its reverse and both directed inverse equations just as for the machine changes. All rules are typed on the single wire; retaining the endpoints preserves the birth and death even in the closed-pair rule.

A closed connected component is now a chain $dWz$. Its drawing has no bounded faces. If it contains $X$, absorption and the final change take it to $T$. If it has a mismatched pair, the corresponding new change introduces $X$. The other chains are exactly the colour-correct old ones. Starting in such a chain, a signed original move preserves the colours. A first new move is impossible unless the chain already equals $T$: forward mismatch detection needs a mismatch, reverse detection and either direction of absorption need an $X$, and reverse finalization consumes the whole connected chain $T$. In particular no sequence of inverse error moves can repair a colour-correct nonhalting component.

If the machine halts uniformly, each connected component is isomorphic to $T$ modulo mutual raw 4-reachability. Naturality of the braid and its two repairs at $T$ supply every component test, including the full twist for two distinct component types. For a nonhalting chain $C$, use the ideal of diagrams having a closed component in the signed component of $T$. In a planar context each machine or error change replaces one connected string segment by another with the same open ports and retained endpoints. It preserves membership in this ideal, including when the segment is already closed. Gluing does not open a closed component. Consequently the two terminal 4-repairs cannot occur in a raw filler comparing the diagonal on two copies of $C$ with the identity: their occurrence would put the ordinary 2-value in the ideal. On the other declarations use the list model, assigning a singleton to $d$ and the empty list to every other 2-generator. All the changes preserve birth count and take the identity permutation, so all inverse comparisons exist. The diagonal on the two births transposes them. The identity fixes them. This excludes the remaining raw fillers, while $J$ gives the converted identity. In the uniform case the two slides of the terminal repair still give the degree-five failure of Response 37: the same singleton assignment interprets every added inverse comparison by an identity in its marked model. The first failure is therefore four or five according to the machine. [Ideal, braid naturality and marked slides](#researcher-response-37)

For an arbitrary $G$ with a directed cycle, choose a simple cycle $p$ based at $x$ and substitute its bracketed path for $f$. Introduce no gates on other paths. Every row of a closed history starting at $x$ is a power of $p$. A gate can start only at a visit to $x$, so each unary gate takes one *whole* copy of $p$ to another. Roots at other vertices have no birth. The planar diagram is consequently the unary string diagram with each wire replaced by the band of edges of $p$. The additional bounded gaps between adjacent strands of a band have exterior vertices internal to $p$. None is $x$ and none admits a positive closed history. Thus the connected decorated components used in the ambient tests are precisely the thickened closed chains, with no possible additional decoration. In the uniform case their raw isomorphisms and repairs are the substituted unary ones.

For the negative direction, send all vertices to the unary vertex, the last edge of $p$ to $f$, and every other edge to an identity. Take $p$ right-associated. Its image is $f$ preceded by left units. In ordinary strictly unital syntax the image equals $f$, so each thickened declaration can be assigned the original unary declaration at its prescribed boundary by conversion. A proposed raw repair would give a strictly unital unary repair at the original boundary, and strict-unit conservativity would give a raw one. The same reasoning preserves absence at the marked fifth-degree boundary. This avoids any assumption about how an arbitrary raw witness might use the spare edges of $G$. The 2-skeleton, consisting of the nine chosen gates on the fixed cycle, is independent of the machine. The general $\Pi^0_2$ upper bound gives completeness. [Strict units in arbitrary ordinary contexts](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-50)

### Shifting the obstruction and deciding the unbounded problem on edgeless graphs

Let $Y$ be any one-object ordinary computad. On a fixed graph with a chosen edge $e$, put its suspension generators over $e$, identifying the suspended object of $Y$ with that edge. Its poles can coincide. Mark just $e$ and use empty left and right alphabets. Every path is protected, including paths traversing $e$ repeatedly. The sole nonempty indexed hom computad is $Y$, since the only marked generating edge is $e$. Response 71 gives the exact shift of the first $J$-failure by one. To shift several times, take the intermediate graphs to be the one-object one-loop graph; the final graph can be the given $G$. Applying this $n-4$ times to the unary machine family proves the bounded claim for each $n\ge5$. Applying it just once to the binary 2-computad bubble family proves $\Pi^0_1$-hardness of **unbounded** recognition on every graph with an edge, with two fixed 2-generators $e\to e$ and only the 3-declarations varying. The shifted machine family concerns bounded recognition since all its members eventually fail. [First-failure shift](#researcher-response-71)

On an edgeless graph, choose a generator $g:s\to t$ of the least positive dimension $m$, if one exists. Then $m\ge2$ and its boundary is supported on one point. Write its $(m-2)$-poles $a,b$, so $s,t:a\to b$. Choose the point-only return $v:b\to a$, right-whisker $g$ by $v$, and fit its two resulting faces $s;v,t;v:a\to a$ to $1_a$ by point-only comparisons in the required directions. All these point comparisons exist by raw globe lifting at the point. The resulting raw $m$-cell $1_a\to1_a$ still uses $g$ once. Response 4 excludes unbounded reflection in every finite extension, including the given one. With only vertices, strict evaluation and point lifting give raw existence at every surface-inhabited old boundary. More generally the same argument works on any graph if there are higher generators and no 2-generators: $m\ge3$, the two graph-only poles have the same strict path, and the return and comparisons lift on the line of that path. [Finite-extension obstruction](#researcher-response-4) · [Graph-only lifting](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-2)

### The rank-one issue for the directed question

There is a separate reduction for Q1(a) which requires no raw model structure for admissible $E$. Write $\lambda$ for the congruence generated just by the left unit equation $1_x *_0 f=f$ on 1-arrows, with no coherence-head equation. For either coherence rule, universal $A,U$ reflection through any specified degree is equivalent to the conjunction of universal $\lambda$ reflection through that degree and $A,U$ reflection through that degree on the one-object two-loop graph. The forward implication is inclusion. For the converse fold objects as in round-1 Response 20: its free groupoid grading, literal placements and retraction of the tree of copies use the ordinary strict projection and point fillers, both available for $E$. Encode the one-object input over two loops as above. Decode in the **free $\lambda$ model** on the input using the state-indexed family construction of round-1 Response 113. Each encoded edge is right-associated, with all entries identities except its final original edge, so it recovers precisely that edge modulo $\lambda$. Assign each higher declaration its original variable in every state. Its actual type agrees by substitution in declaration order. The head calculation uses the column states of the generic tuple and the original head action at those labels, with the $E$ condition still on its bound tree. Thus an encoded raw filler gives a $\lambda$-filler at the specified old boundary. The first hypothesis returns a raw filler there; the second supplies the encoded raw filler from the encoded $A,U$ witness. This isolates the path-unit premise for the directed theories. For ordinary CaTT it already follows from strict-unit conservativity. [Object identification](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-20) · [Indexed model](../answered/semistrict-surface-conservativity-2026-11-24.md#researcher-response-113)

**Validation.** Run `python3 tools/semistrict_surface_fixed_graph_join.py --rice /path/to/catt_strict`. The checker compares first-return detection on all 512 three-vertex simple digraphs and a parallel-edge example, and checks the sparse criterion against positioned search on 386 rule sets. On 3,906 single-wire chains it checks malformed cleanup and the only possible first reverse error move on a legal chain. An independent bounded signed search separates 2,781 terminal-connected chains from 20 legal nonhalting chains. It also compares 92 positioned events on a subdivided cycle with their single-wire positions. Rice typechecks 24 raw inverse comparisons for malformed and terminal changes on the two-edge cycle, checks the strictly unital return, and rejects the direct padded return in raw syntax. With `--agda-dir DIR` the checker emits the point-only boundary fits in degrees two through five. Run `agda --safe --no-libraries -i src -i DIR DIR/FixedGraphPointBubbles.agda` to verify their types at the specified identity faces.
